# Master Build Plan — Logistics & Cargo Management System

## Overview

This document is the **roadmap** for building the full backend of a multi-tenant SaaS Logistics & Cargo Management System on AWS serverless infrastructure. The work has been broken into independently executable task files, each small enough for an AI to process within context limits.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12 · AWS Lambda (ARM64/Graviton2) · API Gateway (HTTP API) · Aurora Serverless v2 (PostgreSQL 15.4) · Cognito · S3 · SQS · EventBridge · Terraform · GitHub Actions

**Architecture:** Serverless-first, multi-tenant with shared-database row-level isolation, event-driven async notifications.

> **IMPORTANT:** Before implementing ANY task, read `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md`. It contains mandatory conventions (idempotency, structured logging, correlation IDs, security headers, Terraform standards, etc.) that apply to every task.

**Frontend:** the backend above is complete. The frontend (React + TypeScript SPA, design already approved in `Shipx_frontend_design/`) has its own task-file set in [`frontend/00_frontend_master_plan.md`](frontend/00_frontend_master_plan.md) — start there to build the UI against this API.

---

## Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)

| ADR | Decision | Rationale | Tradeoff |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-001 | Serverless Lambda over ECS/Fargate | Zero idle cost, auto-scaling, event-driven fit | Cold starts (mitigated by provisioned concurrency on critical paths) |
| ADR-002 | Shared DB with RLS over DB-per-tenant | 60-80% cheaper, simpler ops, sufficient isolation for SMB target | Noisy-neighbor risk (mitigated by per-tenant connection limits at RDS Proxy) |
| ADR-003 | Cognito over custom auth | Managed service, free tier 50K MAU, JWT integration with API Gateway | Limited customization, vendor lock-in |
| ADR-004 | EventBridge + SQS over direct invocation | Decoupled services, built-in retry, DLQ for poison messages | Eventual consistency for notifications (~1-5s delay) |
| ADR-005 | Cursor-based over offset pagination | Stable pagination under concurrent writes, no performance cliff at high offsets | Slightly more complex client implementation |
| ADR-006 | Python over Node.js/Go | Team expertise, Pydantic validation, psycopg2 maturity | Slower cold starts vs Go (acceptable with provisioned concurrency) |
| ADR-007 | Aurora Serverless v2 over standard RDS | Auto-scaling ACUs, auto-pause in dev, pay-per-use | ~15% premium over provisioned RDS at steady-state |
| ADR-008 | ARM64/Graviton2 Lambda | 20% cost reduction, better performance | All dependencies must support ARM (psycopg2-binary does) |

| 7.125 ✅ | Entra login fix — CORS + email username | Native Entra login was broken in the deployed Azure SPA: `EntraClient.signIn` did the ROPC password grant **directly from the browser**, but Entra's token endpoint sends no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` for that grant → every login CORS-blocked (`net::ERR_FAILED`). Fix: proxy login through the backend — new public `POST /v1/auth/login` (`entra_refresh.password_grant`, mirrors the existing server-side refresh), and `EntraClient.signIn` now POSTs `{username,password}` there (needs only `VITE_AUTH_PROVIDER=entra` + `VITE_API_BASE_URL`; `VITE_ENTRA_*` dropped from the frontend). Also: users now sign in with their **real email** — the backend maps `alex@gmail.com` → `alex_gmail.com@<tenant>.onmicrosoft.com` via `email_to_upn`+`ENTRA_USER_DOMAIN` before ROPC (idempotent; no re-provisioning needed). Route registered public in `shared/http_routing.py` + AWS api-gateway TF; Azure container already passes the `ENTRA_*` env. Tests: `test_entra_refresh.py` (+6: password_grant mapping, email→UPN, invalid_grant, login route Entra-200/Cognito-404), `EntraClient.test.ts` rewritten. ruff/mypy/tsc/eslint clean. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, frontend `04_auth_and_session.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`. |
| 7.126 ✅ | Entra interactive login (auth-code + PKCE, BFF) | Replaced the ROPC login (7.125) with the secure, multi-tenant-correct flow: the SPA redirects to Entra's hosted sign-in and returns to `/auth/callback`. This is the only flow that triggers the `entra_token_claims` custom-claims provider (so `tenant_id`/`role` land in the token — ROPC got `groups` GUIDs but no `tenant_id`, breaking `buildSession` for tenant users), and it supports MFA/Conditional Access/social. **BFF, not client-side MSAL** (keeps the no-web-storage posture + CI guard): backend owns the OAuth exchange over 3 public routes — `POST /v1/auth/authorize-url` (server-generated PKCE/state/nonce in a short-lived httpOnly tx cookie → authorize URL), Entra redirect, `POST /v1/auth/callback` (validate state, `authorization_code` grant server-side, set httpOnly refresh cookie, return id_token). New `services/auth/entra_oauth.py` + `entra_refresh.authorization_code_grant`; frontend `EntraClient.beginLogin`/`completeLogin`, `AuthCallback.tsx`, `SessionProvider.beginLogin`/`completeLogin`, LoginScreen "Continue with Microsoft Entra" button. Cross-site cookies handled via `AUTH_COOKIE_SAMESITE=None` (Azure FrontDoor↔ContainerApps); `ENTRA_REDIRECT_URI` derived from the frontend URL. Entra app-reg prereqs automated by **`scripts/setup_entra_claims_provider.sh`** (idempotent: adds the `/auth/callback` SPA redirect URI, creates+binds the onTokenIssuanceStart custom auth extension to the SPA app, ensures the resource/audience app + consent, prints the `entra_claims_caller_*` tfvars) + documented in `docs/runbook.md`. Tests: `test_entra_oauth.py` (11), `EntraClient.test.ts` rewritten; backend 88 auth tests + ruff/mypy, frontend 863 tests + tsc/eslint/build clean. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, frontend `04_auth_and_session.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`. |
| 7.127 ✅ | Entra account model — member/UPN default + optional B2C local accounts | Live Entra login federated gmail/outlook emails to `login.live.com` and failed (`invalid_request`/`redirect_uri`) when the user typed their raw email. Diagnosed to the account model: interactive sign-in home-realm-discovers a consumer email to a *personal* Microsoft account. Attempted email-based **local accounts** (`creationType: LocalAccount` + `emailAddress` identity) but **this tenant rejects them** (*"Name coexistence user creation is not enabled"*) — so the supported path is **member/UPN accounts, signed in with the UPN** (which never federates). `EntraIdentityProvider` keeps provisioning member accounts by **default** and gains an `ENTRA_LOCAL_ACCOUNTS=true` opt-in for true B2C tenants with email sign-in enabled; also hardened regardless of mode: resolve users by email identity (`$filter identities/any` + `ConsistencyLevel: eventual`) when email≠UPN, sync groups by object id, and per-user ops (disable/enable/update/resend/reset/sign-out) resolve to the oid first. `setup_entra_platform_admin.sh` honors the same `ENTRA_LOCAL_ACCOUNTS` flag (member by default; local account via Graph when true). Tests: `test_entra_identity_provider.py` rewritten (member default, local opt-in, email-resolution, missing-user no-op — 13). ruff/mypy clean. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md` — member/UPN default + the federation gotcha, **plus an ordered "set up a Microsoft Entra External ID tenant for email+password sign-in" runbook** (the supported path for email-as-username: new External ID tenant → user flow → app reg → point tfvars + `ENTRA_LOCAL_ACCOUNTS=true` → run the two setup scripts → migrate users). |
| 7.128 ✅ | Entra tenant setup made reproducible — and three latent re-break bugs removed from the setup script | User: "ensure that all the fixes that we did for the entra and all login/signup related issues is captured in terraform for what can be in terraform and what can't be in terraform will be added to a script, which can be executed with the require credentials." Every Entra fix from the dev auth build-out had been applied by hand (portal clicks + one-off Graph PATCHes), so staging/prod could not be stood up. Audited it by **diffing the env vars the auth code reads against the ones Terraform sets**, which found two real gaps: `APP_BASE_URL` (activation/password-reset email links — it falls back to `ENTRA_REDIRECT_URI`'s origin, so dev worked *by coincidence*, but staging/demo/prod set neither and would have emailed an **empty link**, stranding every provisioned user) and `ENTRA_AUTHORIZE_PROMPT` (the `select_account` account picker — code-defaulted, undeclared). Both now Terraform variables wired into the container_app env. **Three bugs found in `setup_entra_claims_provider.sh` that would have recreated this session's exact failures on any new environment:** (1) step 1 registered the callback under the **`spa`** platform, the very thing that caused `AADSTS9002327` → it now registers under `publicClient` *and removes it from `spa`* (leaving it in both keeps the SPA rule in force); (2) step 6 printed a `login.microsoftonline.com` issuer/JWKS, which is wrong for CIAM and is precisely what produced `AADSTS1100001/1003002` → it now **reads `issuer` + `jwks_uri` from the tenant's OIDC discovery document** instead of assembling a host; (3) step 4b claimed every tenant-scoped sign-in fails without the claims mapping — disproven this session (CIAM discards the mapping; `resolve_identity_from_db` resolves from the DB by `oid`) → rewritten as accurate and explicitly not load-bearing. New **`scripts/setup_entra_auth.sh`** covers what Terraform cannot (the CIAM tenant is a *different* tenant from the subscription's, so one provider config can't reach both, and the deploy identity is deliberately only a subscription Contributor): Graph provisioning app + the **three** application permissions — including `User-PasswordProfile.ReadWrite.All`, absent from the runbook and the cause of the `403 Authorization_RequestDenied` on set-password — admin consent **verified via `appRoleAssignments` rather than trusting the command's exit code**, secret minting (printed once, never written to disk), and disabling self-service sign-up so Entra only *authenticates* (left on, a stranger can mint a directory user with no `tenant_user` row — an account that authenticates and resolves to nothing). Guards: refuses to run unless the logged-in tenant matches `ENTRA_TENANT_ID`, plus `DRY_RUN`/`SKIP_SECRET`. Password reset is **reported, not changed** — flipping a tenant-wide authentication method as a side effect of a setup script is not acceptable. Finally, a Terraform **`auth_config_guard` precondition** fails the *plan* when `auth_provider = "azure"` with the Entra values empty: that was previously a healthy deploy where every sign-in failed, which looks like a code bug and is a missing tfvar. staging/demo/prod tfvars gained the full fill-in checklist. `terraform validate` + `fmt` clean, both scripts syntax-checked. Docs: `docs/runbook.md` (corrected the two now-wrong Entra claims in place), `LOCAL_TESTING.md`. **Flagged:** on CIAM the `*-entra-claims` Container App is no longer load-bearing and could be retired. The user-flow sign-up API shape could not be verified live — the available `az` session is in the workforce tenant, not the CIAM tenant — so that step checks and reports rather than assuming success. |
| 7.129 🚧 | Azure Defender for Cloud remediation — subscription baseline (first of several) | Defender scored the subscription **30%**. Business policy set by the user: staging, demo and prod must satisfy **every** recommendation, paid and unpaid; dev gets only the free controls. **The blocking structural finding: Defender plans are enabled and billed per *subscription*, not per resource group**, and dev + tfstate + (future) prod all share `21156c4a-2977-448d-8de2-c523028d2f28` — so the dev exemption is *not expressible* until dev is moved to its own subscription (free under the same billing account). User chose to split it. Delivered here: a new **`infrastructure/azure/subscription/`** root — deliberately separate from the per-environment stack, because three environments sharing a subscription would each try to own the same settings and fight on every apply. It owns the six Defender plans (`Arm`, `Containers`, `CloudPosture`, `OpenSourceRelationalDatabases`, `KeyVaults`, `StorageAccounts` with the malware-scanning + sensitive-data extensions that recommendation 26f0d5bb actually requires), the subscription security contact and high-severity alert email — closing **9 of 24 findings, including 6 of the 8 Highs**. The dev tfvars writes tier `Free` **explicitly** rather than omitting the plans, so a plan switched on by hand in the portal surfaces as drift instead of being silently ignored; `malware_scanning_cap_gb_per_month` bounds the per-GB scanning charge. **Three findings deliberately not remediated**, each to carry a written Defender exemption rather than being left silently red: private link on the **frontend** storage account (it is the public SPA origin behind Front Door *Standard*; private-link origins need Front Door *Premium*, ~$330/mo vs ~$35, and would break the public site), private link on **tfstate** (reached by GitHub runners over the internet), and VNet rules on the **assets** account — the app issues **shared-key SAS URLs browsers hit directly**, so default-deny kills every upload/download, the same pattern as S3 presigned URLs. Also noted: ACR is **Basic**, and *both* ACR findings require **Premium** (~$50/mo/env vs ~$5) — user approved Premium for non-dev only. **Still outstanding:** free-tier env hardening (Key Vault RBAC — blocked on granting the deploy identity *User Access Administrator*, the same prerequisite keeping `grant_acr_pull = false` — KV firewall, purge protection, KV/Service Bus diagnostic logs, Postgres Entra auth); paid env hardening (private endpoints, ACR Premium); and two app-code migrations as their own PRs — disabling storage shared-key access **requires switching presigned URLs to a user-delegation SAS, not just swapping the credential**, since SAS generation currently needs the account key; and Postgres Entra-auth-only needs `shared/db.py` to fetch an AAD token as the password. `terraform validate` + `fmt` clean. Docs: `docs/runbook.md` → "Azure security baseline". |
| 7.130 ✅ | Warehouse management: empty picker on packing lists, names blocked forever after delete, and "Set default" that looked dead | Three defects reported from live use: *"the warehouse location isn't appearing when I create a packing list"*, *"I cannot set a default"*, and *"I deleted a warehouse and could not recreate it until I changed the name"*. Each had a different root cause, all found by reading code before changing any of it. **(1) The picker was always empty.** `_WAREHOUSE_READ_ROLES` omitted `china_office_staff` — precisely the role that creates packing lists — so `GET /v1/warehouses` 403'd; and `CreatePackingList.tsx` caught it with `.catch(() => [])`, rendering **"No warehouses configured"**. A permission error displayed as a configuration state, which is exactly why it survived: the saved data was never wrong (the backend applies the default via `_resolve_arrival_warehouse`), only the UI. Fixed on both sides — the role added, and the page now tracks load failure separately so "unavailable" never reads as "none exist". **(2) Names stayed reserved forever.** Migration 041 declared `UNIQUE (tenant_id, name)` over *every* row while deletion only set `is_active = FALSE`. Migration 056 replaces it with a partial unique index `WHERE is_active`; the service also pre-checks the name so the caller gets a 409 rather than a raw unique violation surfacing as a 500. **(3) Delete is now a real delete**, per the user's explicit instruction. Raised the blocker first rather than building it blind: `release_note`, `pickup_record` and `payment_record` reference `warehouse(id)` with **no ON DELETE action**, so a hard delete is *refused by Postgres* once a warehouse appears on a handover document — and blanking those would damage the record of whose goods were released. User chose hard-delete-if-unused, refuse-if-used: `DELETE` really deletes, or returns 422 `WAREHOUSE_IN_USE` **naming the counts** and the UI offers to retire it instead via a new `PATCH /{id}/deactivate`. **(4) "Set default" was not broken** — route, transaction, role gate and 30 existing tests were all correct. Its only feedback was an error banner rendered at the *top of the page, above the table*, so a failure on a lower row had no visible effect; fixed with `scrollIntoView` plus explicit success confirmation. Also added the missing `is_default` checkbox to the **create** modal — promoting a warehouse previously meant finding a separate row action afterwards. **Found along the way, fixed generally:** `BusinessRuleViolationError.rule_id` is set by dozens of rules across every service and was **never serialized** into the RFC 7807 body, so no client could branch on which rule refused a request without matching English prose; `error_response` now emits it and `ApiError` exposes `ruleId`. Guards kept honest: a bare `PATCH {is_active:false}` is refused (`WAREHOUSE_DEACTIVATE_VIA_DELETE`) since it would bypass the default/last-active checks, and promotion requires `is True` rather than truthiness so a stray value can never silently reassign a tenant's default. 1380 backend (+11) and 877 frontend (+8) tests pass; the 7 frontend failures were verified identical on `main` by stashing the branch. ruff/eslint/tsc clean. Docs: `13_api_payment_release.md` (ground-truth section), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (056 + superseded note on 041), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (amends the stale 2026-10-03 claim in place), `02_shared_utilities.md`, `04c_terraform_api_gateway.md`, `17_testing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. **Flagged, not fixed:** `useCreateWarehouse()`/`useSetDefaultWarehouse()` in `frontend/src/hooks/useWarehouses.ts` are unused (`Warehouses.tsx` calls `warehousesApi` directly) — note that an earlier draft of this entry wrongly called the whole file dead code; `useWarehouses()` itself IS used by `BillingDetail.tsx` and `ReleaseNoteDetail.tsx`, and the grep that said otherwise had excluded the very string an import line contains; `vite.config.ts`'s `test.exclude` uses plain strings not globs, so running vitest from the repo root collects the Playwright specs. |
| 7.131 ✅ | Searchable country/state pickers and a real phone field app-wide — plus the public-tracking phone lookup that never worked | User: *"anywhere in the application where a country/state is required, use a drop down with search instead of typing"* and *"phone number fields are typed manually, it should be a phone field with country code defaulted to Nigeria."* Three new design-system inputs built on the existing `Combobox`: **`CountrySelect`** (196 countries, searchable by name *or* dial code, operating markets first), **`StateSelect`** (country-driven, label follows local usage — State/Province/Region), and **`PhoneField`** (dial-code picker + national number, **defaults to Nigeria**). Rolled out to **11 phone sites, 2 country sites and 1 state site**. **`PhoneField` emits E.164**, which is the whole point: it is what the DB stores, so customer de-duplication works and the backend never has to guess a country (a `+` number is passed through `normalize_phone` untouched, so a dial code `_DIAL_CODES` has never heard of is still stored correctly). **The live bug found while auditing:** public tracking compared the typed phone against stored E.164 as one exact string, so a customer entering `08012345678` never matched their own `+2348012345678` — the feature only worked if you happened to type the full `+234` form. Fixed **server-side too** (`_phone_match_candidates`, matched with `= ANY(...)`, offering both the normalised and raw forms), because the endpoint is public and saved links/old clients bypass the UI. **A second bug, caught by a page test:** `toE164` tested emptiness *before* stripping the trunk `0`, so the lone `"0"` that exists for one keystroke while typing `0803…` emitted a bare `+234`, which `parsePhone` read back as national digits — the next keystroke produced `+234234…`, doubling the dial code and growing with every character. Fixed and pinned with a character-by-character type-in test. Migration **057** adds `warehouse.country` (the state field had no anchor), backfilled from each tenant's own `destination_country` rather than a blanket 'Nigeria' so a tenant configured for another market is not mislabelled; changing country in the form **clears** the state, since a state belongs to one country's list. Dataset accuracy is pinned where it matters: **`test_phone.py::TestFrontendDialCodesAgree` parses `countries.ts` from the backend suite** and asserts agreement with `shared/phone.py` — without it both sides could stay internally consistent while disagreeing. Chose a curated 196-country dataset over adding `libphonenumber-js` (~80KB+) to format one field; `StateSelect` falls back to **free text** for countries with no list (an empty dropdown would make the field unfillable), and an unrecognised stored country/state is preserved rather than blanked. City deliberately left as free text. 1385 backend (+5) and 927 frontend (+50) tests pass; the 7 frontend failures are the pre-existing unrelated set. tsc/eslint/ruff clean. Docs: `frontend/03_component_library.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (057), `13_api_payment_release.md`, `10_api_tracking.md`, `17_testing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. **Also corrected a false claim I made in 7.130:** `useWarehouses.ts` is NOT dead code — the grep behind that claim excluded the very string an import line contains. Only its two mutation hooks are unused. **Known limitation documented:** +1 and +7 are shared by several countries and cannot be resolved from the prefix alone; the tie-break favours an operating market, and the ambiguity is cosmetic because the number round-trips to the identical string either way. |
| 7.132 ✅ | Packing-list goods dates: reordered, constrained, and their meaning corrected | User: *"During packing list creation, the dates for loading date and arrival date, I want it to be flipped… the arrival date will be first and the loading date will be second. With a clear description stating what they are for"* — arrival = received in the Chinese warehouse, loading = loaded into the container, loading must be >= arrival (same day allowed), arrival cannot be in the future. **Clarified on a follow-up** that "arrival date" meant the **existing `delivery_date`** column, not a new field — I had started to ask whether to add one, which would have been the wrong build. **The semantics were wrong before this, not just the order:** migration 052 and the form copy described `delivery_date` as the *expected customer delivery* (a destination-side future estimate), when it is the date the supplier **delivered the goods to the China warehouse**. Both dates are origin-side events, which is precisely why one must precede the other; destination estimation already lives on `container.estimated_delivery_date` (migration 002), so nothing is lost. **No schema change** — reorder, rephrase, and validation only. Section is now "Goods dates" with per-field helpers ("Received in the China warehouse. Not a future date." / "Loaded into the container. Same day as delivery or later.") instead of one shared subtitle. Guarded three ways on purpose: `max`/`min` on the native pickers so the commonest mistakes are **unreachable** rather than rejected after the fact; inline errors that **disable Next**; and the real enforcement on a shared `_ShipmentDatesMixin` used by **both** `CreatePackingListRequest` and `UpdatePackingListRequest`, so a PATCH cannot write an ordering the POST path refuses. `loading_date` left **unbounded above** — a packing list is written against a container still in `loading` status, so a planned load date is legitimate; both fields stay optional. **Timezone trap avoided in both layers:** the China office is UTC+8, so for several hours each evening their local calendar date is already tomorrow in UTC — a naive `toISOString()` or UTC-"today" comparison would reject the date on their own wall calendar. The frontend builds the local date field by field and the backend allows one day past UTC, with a test pinning that allowance so a later "tightening" cannot silently reintroduce it. **A weak test caught while writing them:** the "Next is disabled" cases initially passed for the wrong reason (`canProceedFrom1` also needs a customer and container, so the button was disabled regardless of the dates) — now they pick both first, so only the date rule can disable it; the two "accepts" cases are what exposed it by failing where they should have passed. Also noted: the Postman create/update bodies never carried these dates at all; added with the rules documented. 1393 backend (+8) and 935 frontend (+8) tests pass; tsc/eslint/ruff/mypy clean. Docs: `08_api_packing_list.md`, `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (corrects the stale 052 description in place), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.133 ✅ | State management: 40 of 45 mutating files never invalidated the cache, so deletes and updates didn't show until a hard refresh | User: *"When I delete something, or update something, sometimes the page doesn't refresh. For example, I deleted a container and the container was still there until I refreshed the page… do a holistic check on all the application pages."* **Root cause was architectural, not a one-off.** `lib/queryClient.ts` sets `refetchOnWindowFocus: false` and a 3-minute `staleTime`, justifying both with *"Mutations call invalidateQueries immediately … Mutations + invalidateQueries handle freshness correctly"* — an audit of all 45 files that mutate found that true in **5**. With nothing else to correct the cache, a stale list never self-healed. The reported case exactly: `ContainerDetail.tsx` holds its data in `useState`, calls `containersApi.delete()` **directly**, and navigates back to a `ContainerList` reading a cached `useInfiniteQuery`; a `useDeleteContainer` hook that *would* have invalidated existed and was never wired up — the same dead-mutation-hook shape found earlier on warehouses. **Fixed centrally rather than at 40 call sites** (user's choice, recommended): every successful non-GET now invalidates its domain's query roots in `api/client.ts`, driven by a new path→key registry (`api/cacheInvalidation.ts`) covering all **107** mutating paths — which fixes every existing site *and* every future one, whether the author remembers a hook. **The safeguard is the point:** `api/cacheInvalidation.test.ts` scrapes every mutating path out of `api/*.ts` and fails CI if one has no rule, so the registry cannot drift (via `import.meta.glob`, not `node:fs` — the app tsconfig has no Node types, the same constraint that sent the countries/dial-code cross-check to pytest in 7.131). Deliberate choices, each documented: invalidate **roots** not exact keys (a list key embeds its filters; React Query only refetches queries with active observers, so cost is bounded by what's on screen); **cross-domain effects explicit** per rule (deleting a container frees its packing lists; approving a packing list moves container CBM and can create billing; a warehouse rename must reach the packing-list picker and the release-note letterhead); **fire-and-forget** so a cache problem can't turn a successful write into a UI error; **nothing on GET** (that's how you build a refetch loop); **nothing on a failed write** (it changed nothing, and refetching would repaint the list and mask the failure); pre-auth paths map to an **empty** root set so they still match and can't be satisfied later by an accidental catch-all. `refetchOnWindowFocus` left **off** (user's choice) — once invalidation works it's unnecessary, and enabling it would add Aurora queries on every tab switch app-wide; the queryClient comment now says so explicitly instead of asserting an assumption. 943 frontend tests pass (+16), same 7 pre-existing failures; **no existing test needed changing**, which is the useful signal — behaviour added without altering any page's contract. tsc/eslint/build clean. Docs: `frontend/09_state_management_and_data_fetching.md`, `17_testing.md`, and the `lib/queryClient.ts` header comment corrected in place. **Known limitation, documented not hidden:** this fixes *cross-page* staleness. A page holding its own `useState` copy (`ContainerDetail`, `Warehouses`, most config pages) is not a query observer, so invalidation can't refresh it in place; those still reload themselves manually. Migrating them onto the existing mutation hooks is follow-up work, deliberately not bundled. |
| 7.134 ✅ | RLS audit: four policies could never match a row, and layer 3 turned out not to be enforced at all | User: *"yes fix the RLS"*, following the gap flagged earlier. Investigating four policies that referenced a session variable nothing sets turned up **three** defects, two of which were hiding each other. **(1)** `container_document` (022), `notification_template` (024), `rate_history` (025) and `warehouse` (041) each read `current_setting('app.tenant_id', TRUE)` while `_set_tenant` sets **`app.current_tenant_id`** — so the setting is NULL, `tenant_id::text = NULL` is NULL, NULL is not TRUE, and the policy **denies every row**. **(2)** None of the four ran `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` (migration 005 does, on its 21 core tables), and Postgres exempts a table's owner from non-forced RLS — the app connects as the owner, so the broken policies were never evaluated. **The two masked each other**, which is why this survived four migrations *and* a ticked acceptance criterion: fixing only the name changes nothing observable, adding only FORCE takes those tables offline. Migration **058** fixes both together, and also makes the predicates **PLATFORM-aware**. **(3) The larger finding:** `_set_tenant` sets the literal `'PLATFORM'` for deliberate cross-tenant reads, but 005's core policies cast with `current_setting(...)::uuid` and `'PLATFORM'::uuid` **raises** rather than returning false — yet `resolve_identity_from_db` reads `tenant_user` (a FORCE'd table) under PLATFORM scope and succeeds in dev. That proves **the connecting role bypasses RLS entirely**, so layer 3 of the documented four-layer model is not isolating anything on *any* table, and 058 alone does not change that. Isolation today rests solely on layer 2 (every repo function passing `tenant_id`) — real, but a single layer. **The deliverable that would have caught all of it:** `tests/tenant_isolation/test_rls_enforcement.py` (13 tests), the first test to exercise the policies rather than the application. Its design point is *who connects*: CI's Postgres runs as a superuser, so a normal connection passes whether RLS works or not — the fixture creates an unprivileged probe role and **asserts `NOT rolsuper AND NOT rolbypassrls`**, because the test is worthless if the probe can bypass what it measures. Covers own-tenant-only reads, direct cross-tenant reads, PLATFORM scope, fail-closed on an unscoped connection, `WITH CHECK` blocking cross-tenant writes, and `pg_class`/`pg_policy` assertions pinning FORCE and the setting name. Skips when no DB is reachable so local runs stay green. **Corrected three stale acceptance criteria in `05c_database_rls_indexes_seed.md` that asserted the opposite of reality**, including a cross-tenant test that did not exist. Remaining work, deliberately unbundled (credentials/rollout, not schema): point the app at the non-owner `logistics_app` role 005 already creates, confirm it lacks SUPERUSER/BYPASSRLS, and **re-point 005's 21 core policies at the PLATFORM-aware predicate first** — enforcing RLS without that step would break public tracking, activation and sign-in simultaneously. 1436 backend tests pass + 13 RLS tests skipped locally; ruff/format clean. Docs: `05c_database_rls_indexes_seed.md` (full audit), `17_testing.md`. **Also recorded:** one unreproducible failure of `test_integrates_with_the_real_fernet_envelope` in a combined run — not caused by the new file, no cached global or randomising plugin to explain it; flagged as intermittent with cause unknown rather than guessed at. |
| 7.135 ✅ | RLS prerequisites completed: one canonical PLATFORM-aware predicate on every tenant-scoped table, plus the grants and role hardening the switchover needs | User: *"take the migration to the next level"*. Auditing beyond 058's four tables found the problem was not confined to them: **no table in the schema had a policy compatible with how the app connects.** Three styles had accumulated — 005/027/039 cast `current_setting(...)::uuid` (**raises** on `'PLATFORM'`), 022/024/025/041 read a setting nothing sets (fixed in 058), and 042/044/046/048/051/055 had the right name but **no PLATFORM branch** (denies every platform-scoped read). So enforcing RLS at any point would have broken public tracking, activation and sign-in simultaneously. Migration **059** normalises all of them onto one predicate (`= 'PLATFORM' OR tenant_id::text = current_setting(..., TRUE)`) and sets `FORCE` on each. **Catalog-driven, not a hardcoded list** — a hardcoded list is precisely how four tables drifted — selecting every `public` table with RLS enabled *and* a `tenant_id` column, and it **raises if it matches zero tables** so it cannot report success after doing nothing. Tables without `tenant_id` (`tenant`, `platform_settings`, `platform_audit_log`, `tenant_onboarding_request`) are platform-scoped by design and untouched. **Two further gaps found that would have broken the switchover itself:** 005's `GRANT ... ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public` applied only to tables existing *then*, so every table from 022 onward had **no grant** — the switchover would have failed with "permission denied" on whichever newer table was touched first, a confusing way to discover a 30-migration-old omission; 059 re-grants and adds `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` so future migrations' tables are covered automatically. And `logistics_app` was created in 005 as a **LOGIN role with the literal password `'CHANGE_ME_IN_SECRETS_MANAGER'`** that nothing ever used — 059 strips LOGIN, leaving the real credential to the switchover where it belongs. Tests extended to 19, the load-bearing ones catalog-driven: every tenant-scoped table is FORCE'd, no policy casts to `::uuid` or reads `app.tenant_id` or lacks a PLATFORM branch, a guard that the catalog query finds ≥20 tables (else every assertion passes vacuously), functional isolation on `customer` (one of 005's original 21, proving the rewrite reached them), and that `logistics_app` is NOLOGIN **and** has grants on post-005 tables. A future migration adding a non-canonical policy now fails CI. **Bug caught in my own migration before commit:** the predicate was interpolated via `{_PREDICATE!r}`, which renders Python **double** quotes — Postgres reads those as a quoted identifier, not a string literal, so the policy would have failed at runtime; replaced with inline dollar-quoted SQL and the f-string removed so the mistake can't recur. 1437 tests pass, 19 RLS tests skip locally (they run in CI). **Stated plainly rather than glossed:** 058/059 could not be executed locally — Docker is down and the Homebrew postgresql@14 install is broken — so they were reviewed by reading the generated SQL and are verified by these tests in CI against postgres:15. Remaining: the switchover itself (real credential + `DATABASE_USER` via Terraform), which must come **after** 059 is applied. Docs: `05c_database_rls_indexes_seed.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.136 ✅ | `e2e-local-stack` was permanently red for two real test defects, not "environmental" | User turned Docker on, which made this reproducible. I had merged past this job several times calling it environmental — **that diagnosis was wrong**, and it reproduced locally in minutes. **(1) Test emails were not unique.** `auth-logout.spec.ts` and `customer-invite.spec.ts` both built accounts as `` `e2e-${Date.now()}@example.com` ``, but `playwright.config.ts` sets `fullyParallel: true` — four workers start at once and two hitting that line in the same millisecond produce the **same address**, so the second invite acceptance returns `409 DUPLICATE_RESOURCE`. The API log showed it plainly: four accepts milliseconds apart, first `200`, rest `409`. Confirmed by `--workers=1`, which removed three of the four failures. Fixed with a shared `uniqueTestEmail()` helper on `crypto.randomUUID()`. **(2) The 30s Playwright default was too tight.** The single failure that survived serial execution timed out on the *first field of the invite form* — nothing the spec tests. `claimInviteAndLogin` does two full auth round-trips against Moto, and logout's Cognito `RevokeToken` intermittently 500s and is retried four times (`reached max retries: 4` in the log). Describe block now sets `timeout: 120_000`. **Result: 3 failures → 4 passed in 4.8s**; the previous 3.4-minute serial run was almost entirely the failing test's timeout and retries. Also used Docker to close an outstanding caveat on PR #87: migrations **058 and 059 verified locally** — `make stack` applied them and **seeded successfully**, which additionally proves 059's new `WITH CHECK` does not block writes, and all 19 RLS tests pass locally as well as in CI. Docs: `17_testing.md`. **Worth recording as a process note:** a permanently-red job trains everyone to ignore it, and both bugs were sitting in the API log the whole time. |
| 7.137 ✅ | Four more `e2e-local-stack` defects, found by running the *whole* suite | Fixing 7.136 made `auth-logout.spec.ts` pass **in isolation**, which is not the job passing — a full `--project=local-stack` run on a freshly seeded stack still showed **8 failures across five other specs**, all four causes real. **(3) Loading skeletons were counted as data.** `Table.tsx` renders `SKELETON_ROWS = 5` placeholder `<tr>`s with a full set of `<td>`s and no marker, so `dataRows()` matched them and **any mid-load row count returned 5** regardless of the data. Instrumenting `container-list.spec.ts` showed it: real counts `initial 2 / filtered 0 / restored 2`, but the test read the skeleton's **5** as `filteredCount` and failed `restored >= filtered`. Same cause as all three `bulk-packing-list-actions` failures. Skeleton rows are now `aria-hidden` + `data-skeleton-row`, the `<table>` sets `aria-busy`, `dataRows()` excludes them, and a new `waitForTableSettled()` waits on `aria-busy="false"` — replacing a fixed 300ms guess and correcting the old comment claiming no DOM signal existed. **(4) `networkidle` is the wrong wait here.** `local_server.py` runs `threaded=False` *deliberately* (`shared/db.py`'s connection singleton is not thread-safe; one-at-a-time also matches Lambda), so the API serializes — under four Playwright workers no 500ms idle window opens and the wait throws at 30s. Four tests died on that line, **never on an assertion**. Now `settleNetwork()` waits 5s and **carries on if it times out**; all 18 direct uses replaced. **(5) No browser upload could ever work against Moto.** Uploads are a browser `PUT` to storage on a presigned URL, so the browser preflights — and Moto validates the presigned signature on the `OPTIONS`, returning `SignatureDoesNotMatch` (the signature was for the `PUT`; real S3 answers preflight unauthenticated). This is why manifest import **succeeded under curl**, which ignores CORS, and failed in the app. `bootstrap_local.sh` now applies bucket CORS (mirroring `modules/s3/main.tf`), and — since CORS alone can't fix the signature check — `vite.config.ts` proxies `/__storage` with `local_server.py` rewriting presigned URLs onto it, making the `PUT` same-origin so no preflight is sent. Production untouched and already correct. **(6) A stale spec and a missing label.** `claims-and-refunds.spec.ts` typed into a container field `MyClaimsNew.tsx` **removed on 2026-09-06**; fixing it exposed that the replacement `<select>` had a sibling `<label>` with **no `htmlFor`** — no accessible name at all. **Result: 86 passed, 15 skipped, 0 failed** on a fresh stack. Docs: `17_testing.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, frontend `07_domain_modules.md`. **Two things left open and flagged, not fixed:** the suite is **not idempotent** (specs mutate seed state and `test.skip` once consumed, so re-running without `make stack` turns failures into skips — 15 of 101; runs also accumulate users, reaching 50 `tenant_user` rows against a starter limit of 10), and **7 frontend unit tests fail locally** (`storage.test.ts`, `colorScheme.test.tsx`, `ThemeToggle.test.tsx`), verified pre-existing by stashing every change. **Superseded by 7.139** — they do *not* fail on `main` in CI, and the cause is a Node version difference, not a missing CI job. |
| 7.138 ✅ | `e2e-local-stack` was still red after 7.137: CI never provisioned `frontend/.env.local`, plus the local/CI worker divergence hiding everything | **The cause was not in the specs.** The job copies the backend `.env.local.sample` and nothing ever created the frontend's env file, so `VITE_API_BASE_URL` was undefined and `src/api/client.ts` threw `Missing required env var` **before any request was sent** — every API call failed, `InviteClaimScreen`'s fetch rejected, the page rendered "This link is no longer valid." and the specs waited 120s for a field that could never appear. Reproduced byte-identically by moving `frontend/.env.local` aside; the giveaway was which test *passed* — the one that **expects** the invalid state. Workflow now copies `frontend/.env.local.example` **before** `make bootstrap` (which patches the generated Cognito IDs in, but only into an existing file) and appends `VITE_COGNITO_ENDPOINT` for the real-Cognito login in `auth-logout`. **The divergence that hid all of it:** Playwright defaults `workers` to 1 on CI but to half the CPU count locally, so local and CI were different tests — in parallel, state-sharing specs **skipped** and `networkidle` timed out; serially they **ran** and failed (local 0 failed vs CI 8 failed). `playwright.config.ts` now pins `workers: 1` and a 90s project timeout. **Four more defects only a serial run reaches:** (a) `.or()` is not "either outcome" — approving the last pending list shows **both** "1 succeeded" and the empty-list text, a strict-mode violation, so the test failed *because it worked*; needs `.first()`. (b) **StrictMode's double fetch overwrites user input** — `<StrictMode>` runs `CbmRateConfig`'s load effect twice and the second `getCbmRate()` resolves after the form renders, re-applying persisted config: it reverted "By package type" to "Single rate" mid-test and reset a typed rate so the save persisted the **old** number (rate history showed a `+$0` change). Dev-only, but fixed by settling first and asserting `toHaveValue` before submit so it fails loudly. (c) **A test depending on a sibling having run** — the sealing test established neither of its preconditions, and no ordering can fix it (alphabetically `bulk-packing-list-actions` approves lists *before* `manifest-import` creates this one), so the server rightly refused and the test only ever "passed" by skipping; it now sets both up over the API. Roles are not interchangeable: approve wants `tenant_admin`, manifest verify requires `china_office_staff` and **403s** `tenant_admin` — the first version ignored the response, so a failed precondition looked met; hence `expectOk()` on every setup call. (d) **A transient error asserted as permanent** — `queryClient` retries once, so a query losing a race against the serialized API renders an error then recovers; `/admin/customers` failed this way with **no error text in the captured snapshot**. Now asserts the settled state (`toHaveCount(0)`) after arrival. **A regression of mine, recorded:** `waitForTableSettled()`'s `catch` only fires after its 10s timeout, so every navigation to a table-less page burned 10s — that alone pushed `smoke-navigation` past the per-test limit; fixed with an immediate `count()` bail-out. Suite wall clock 9.8m → 5.1m. **Result: 86 passed, 15 skipped, 0 failed** against a full CI replica (both env files from tracked samples, `make stack` from scratch, serial). Docs: `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.139 ✅ | 7 failing frontend unit tests were Node 26 shadowing jsdom's `localStorage` — and a correction to what I said in 7.137 | **Correcting myself:** I reported these as "failing on `main`" because "`ci.yml` has no frontend job". The second half is wrong and so is the conclusion — **`frontend-ci.yml`'s `build-and-test` runs `npm test`** and it passed. They failed on **this machine only**. Cause is the Node version: CI pins `node-version: "22"`, locally Node **26.12.1**, and Node 26 defines its own global `localStorage` that is `undefined` without `--localstorage-file`, which **shadows jsdom's** inside Vitest's jsdom environment. jsdom was never at fault — proved it directly (`jsdom window.localStorage: object` vs `node global localStorage: undefined`), and the origin was fine (`http://localhost:3000`, non-opaque, which is jsdom's actual requirement). So `window.localStorage` read back `undefined` and anything touching it threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'setItem')`. `src/test/setup.ts` now installs a spec-shaped in-memory `Storage` **only when the global is missing**, `configurable`/`writable` so tests can still swap individual methods (`colorScheme.test.tsx` replaces `setItem` to assert it is never called); on Node 22 nothing changes. **958 passed, 145 files, 0 failed** (was 7 failing). **The real gap, ~~flagged not fixed~~ now closed in 7.140:** nothing pinned a local Node version (no `.nvmrc`, no `engines`), so a developer on a newer Node runs a measurably different suite than CI — worth pinning, in its own change rather than inside an e2e fix. Docs: `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.140 ✅ | Pinned the frontend Node version — closing the gap flagged in 7.139 | 7.139 found that 7 unit tests failed on this machine and passed in CI purely because **nothing pinned Node**: `frontend-ci.yml` asked for the floating major `"22"` while local was **26.12.1**, and Node 26's own global `localStorage` shadows jsdom's inside Vitest. Local and CI were running measurably different suites with nothing to say so. Now **`frontend/.nvmrc` = `22.23.4`** is the single source of truth and all five `setup-node` steps (four in `frontend-ci.yml`, one in `frontend-deploy.yml`) read it via `node-version-file:` instead of restating `"22"` — one place to bump, and no way for the two to drift again. `frontend/package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": "22.x" }`, so `npm ci` prints `EBADENGINE` on a mismatch (verified: it names `required: 22.x` / `current: v26.12.1`). **`engine-strict` is deliberately left off** — this machine has no version manager at all (no nvm/fnm/volta/asdf), so enforcing it would hard-block `npm ci` with no way to switch; a loud warning is the right trade until a manager is installed. `LOCAL_TESTING.md` §2 gained a **Node row it never had** (the frontend needs Node and the prerequisites table simply omitted it) plus a subsection recording the jsdom/`localStorage` trap so the next person does not re-debug it; the stale `18_cicd_pipeline.md` snippet (`setup-node@v4` + `node-version: "22"`) was corrected to match the real workflow. Verified on Node 26: `tsc -b --noEmit` clean, **958 passed / 145 files / exit 0**. **One thing flagged, not fixed:** the full parallel run intermittently reports a post-test unhandled timer rejection from `Warehouses.test.tsx` (`surfaces a set-default failure instead of failing silently`); it does **not** reproduce in isolation (3/3 clean) and exit code stays 0, so it gates nothing — a cross-file timer leak worth its own change. Docs: `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.141 ✅ | ruff 0.2.0 → 0.17.3 — Dependabot's #98 was incomplete, and the bump had three hidden consequences | **#98 bumped only `pyproject.toml`. ruff is pinned twice:** `pyproject.toml` (`ruff==0.17.3`, what CI runs via `uv run ruff check .`) and `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (`rev:`, what the commit hook runs). Merging #98 alone would have left pre-commit on 0.2.0 and CI on 0.17.3 — **the same silent local/CI drift as 7.140**, where a commit passes locally and fails in CI. Both pins now move together. **(1) 123 files reformatted**, three distinct transformations: a blank line after the module docstring (the only change in ~98 of them); parenthesized context managers (`with a, b:` → `with (a, b):`), which appears because `target-version` moved to py313 and is formatting only, unrelated to the `SIM117` rule the config still ignores; and adjacent implicit string concatenation collapsed (`"foo " "bar"` → `"foo bar"`). That last one rewrites literals in `services/notification/service.py`, which holds **customer-facing SMS templates**, so the values were *checked, not assumed* — every module-level literal in all 123 touched files was `ast.literal_eval`'d before and after and compared: **0 value changes**. Kept as its own commit so the pin bump stays reviewable. **(2) ruff 0.17 formats Python fenced blocks inside Markdown**, which silently rewrote 11 spec docs (collapsing `raise`s onto 130-char lines, `(1024 ** 3)` → `(1024**3)`). Those snippets are illustrative spec, not compiled code, and line-length 130 is tuned for source — `"*.md"` is now excluded from the formatter. **(3) `target-version` py312 → py313**, retiring a comment whose premise ("0.2.0's newest known target is py312") had expired; `ruff check` verified clean at py313. Also: the `ruff` pre-commit hook id is a **legacy alias** in 0.17 — renamed to `ruff-check`. **Two gaps found while documenting it:** `pre-commit` was **never declared as a dependency** (it only worked from the system PATH, so a fresh clone could not run the documented command) — now `pre-commit==4.5.1` in the dev group; and `LOCAL_TESTING.md` never mentioned the hooks **at all**, so contributors had no way to know the formatter runs *only* there and not in CI. Also recorded the `uv sync` vs `uv sync --extra azure` trap: a plain sync prunes the extra and `test_entra_identity_provider.py` then fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'azure'`, which reads as a regression and is not one (it bit me mid-task). Corrected the stale `18_cicd_pipeline.md` lint snippet, which still showed `pip install ruff==0.2.0` on Python 3.12 against a real workflow that uses `uv sync --extra azure` on 3.13. Verified: `ruff check` clean, `ruff format --check` clean (300 files), mypy `shared/ services/` clean (149 files), **1460 passed / 19 skipped**. Docs: `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.142 ✅ | Reconciled three conflicting line-length values, plus four stale toolchain values | Noticed while bumping ruff (7.141), whose formatter now actively enforces the setting. **Three different line lengths were documented at once:** `CLAUDE.md` said "100 characters max", `01_project_setup.md` showed `line-length = 120`, and `pyproject.toml` — the only one anything reads — sets **130**. Nothing enforced 100 and the codebase never matched it: measured **603 lines over 100 chars in `shared/` + `services/`, across 85 of 149 files**, longest 151. Settled on 130 and corrected `CLAUDE.md`, noting that a few lines still exceed even 130 where the formatter cannot break them (a long URL or string literal). While there, `01_project_setup.md`'s `pyproject.toml` snippet was stale on **four** values against the real file: `requires-python >=3.12` → `>=3.13`, ruff `target-version py312` → `py313`, `line-length 120` → `130`, mypy `python_version 3.12` → `3.13`. Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `01_project_setup.md`. |
| 7.143 ✅ | `retention-days` is clamped by a repo setting — the 7-day retention from 7.138 was a **no-op** | Found by reading an artifact's *real* expiry after #101 merged, instead of trusting the workflow: `lambda-packages-42287528345` was created `2026-11-06T20:23Z` and expired `2026-11-07T20:20Z` — **one day**, against `retention-days: 7`. **`retention-days` cannot exceed the repository's "Artifact and log retention" setting and is silently clamped to it** — no log line, no annotation. That setting was **1 day** following the quota incident, so every `retention-days: 7` in `ci.yml` and `frontend-ci.yml` did nothing. Confirmed by computing `expires_at - created_at` across all artifacts: October's show 29–30 days, everything from 2026-11-05 shows **1**. **Not cosmetic:** `Deploy Pipeline` fetches `lambda-packages-{run_id}` **by run id**, so a 1-day window meant only a build from the last 24h could be redeployed — anything older would have failed at *Download lambda packages*, and this would have surfaced as a mysterious deploy failure, not as a retention problem. Raised the repo setting to 7 (`gh api -X PUT .../artifact-and-log-retention -F days=7`; note **`-F` not `-f`**, which 422s with `Invalid property /days: "7" is not of type integer`). Corrected the `ci.yml` comment, which claimed a week of redeployable builds it was not delivering, and the `18_cicd_pipeline.md` snippet still showing `upload-artifact@v4` + `retention-days: 30`. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.144 ✅ | pytest 8.3.5 → 9.2.0 — Dependabot's #91 was **unsatisfiable**, and the blocker turned out to be an unused dependency | #91 bumped `pytest` alone and **could not resolve at all**: `uv lock` failed with `Because pytest-asyncio==0.23.8 depends on pytest>=7.0.0,<9 and logistics-system:dev depends on pytest==9.2.0 … requirements are unsatisfiable`. It also touched only `pyproject.toml`, leaving **`uv.lock` unregenerated** — the same incompleteness as #98, and `uv.lock` is the source of truth per CLAUDE.md. The obvious fix was to bump `pytest-asyncio` to 1.4.1 (what uv resolves), but **the project has no async tests at all** — zero `async def` in `tests/`, zero `@pytest.mark.asyncio`, no `asyncio_mode` setting, and zero references to `pytest_asyncio` or `event_loop` anywhere in the repo. So `pytest-asyncio` was **dropped** rather than bumped: that clears the conflict, removes an unused dependency, and avoids inheriting 0.23 → 1.x's breaking changes (fixture loop scope, `event_loop` removal) for a plugin nothing uses. `pytest-cov==4.1.0` and `pytest-timeout==2.2.0` resolve fine against pytest 9 and stay pinned as they were. Verified all three CI invocations, not just the bare one: `pytest -q` → **1460 passed / 19 skipped**; `pytest tests/unit/ --cov=services --cov=shared --cov-report=xml` → **1395 passed**, `coverage.xml` written (so pytest-cov 4.1.0 is genuinely compatible); `pytest tests/tenant_isolation/ -v` → **42 passed / 19 skipped**, the skips being the RLS tests still awaiting the `logistics_app` credential, unrelated to pytest. |
| 7.145 ✅ | reportlab 4.1.0 → 5.1.0 — verified by **rendering** the PDFs, not by trusting the tests | reportlab is a **runtime** dependency bundled into the Lambda packages, so a major bump changes what customers receive. The existing tests would not have caught a regression: `test_export_receipt_pdf.py` asserts only `data.startswith(b"%PDF")` and `len(data) > 500`, which a garbled or empty-bodied PDF passes. So all three exporters (`generate_receipt_pdf`, `generate_release_note_pdf`, `generate_packing_list_pdf`) were rendered on **both** versions with fully-populated fixtures — exercising the optional branches (warehouse block, credit grant, balances, multi-item tables) — and compared three ways: extracted text via `pdftotext -layout` (**identical**, 33/55/18 lines), page counts (1/1/1), and finally **rasterised at 150 DPI with `pdftoppm` and SHA-256'd — pixel-identical**. The raster check is the one that matters: the receipt's QR code is a vector `Drawing`/`QrCodeWidget`, not an image XObject, so it is invisible to both text extraction and `pdfimages`. The rendered receipt was also eyeballed to confirm it is correct rather than identically-broken (branded header, scannable QR, table, naira-words line). **A failed check worth recording:** a first attempt compared PDF content streams by inflating them with zlib and counting operators; it reported "0 ops — IDENTICAL" for all three, which was a **false pass** — reportlab writes `/ASCII85Decode` streams, so nothing was ever parsed and empty was being compared to empty. Caught because `0 rects` is impossible for a page with a QR code. Replaced with the raster comparison. Also: `uv lock` reports `Removed chardet v7.6.0` — reportlab 5 drops that transitive dependency; verified nothing in the repo imports it. Like #98 and #91, Dependabot's #92 touched only `pyproject.toml` and left `uv.lock` stale — **three of three** backend dependency PRs with the same gap. Verified: 1460 passed / 19 skipped, ruff clean, mypy clean. |
| 7.146 ✅ | gunicorn 23.0.0 → 26.2.0 and flask-cors 6.0.2 → 6.0.5 (#93, #94) | **Correcting an assumption I made out loud:** I called both of these "local-dev only". flask-cors is (`scripts/local_server.py`), but **gunicorn is production** — `docker-entrypoint.sh` execs it to serve `services.http_app.app:app` and `services.entra_token_claims.app:app` on Azure Container Apps. A three-major bump there is a production change, so it was verified by actually running it rather than by the suite: `--check-config` passes for **both** entrypoints with the exact flags `docker-entrypoint.sh` uses (`--workers/--threads 1/--timeout/--access-logfile/--error-logfile`), which also proves each WSGI app still imports; then gunicorn 26.2.0 was started for real and `GET /health` returned **200** `{"status":"ok","server":"shipx-container"}` with **2 workers booted** (`Booting worker with pid:` ×2) and access logging intact — the `--threads 1` constraint matters because `shared/db.py`'s connection singleton is not thread-safe. flask-cors is pinned **twice** (the `container` extra and the `dev` group) with a comment requiring the pins stay identical; both moved together. Note gunicorn lives in the `container` extra, so it is absent under a plain `uv sync` — it needs `uv sync --extra azure --extra container`. Verified: 1460 passed / 19 skipped, ruff clean, mypy clean. |
| 7.147 ✅ | Root-caused the stale-`uv.lock` pattern: Dependabot was on the wrong ecosystem, and nothing gated it | **Five of five** backend dependency PRs (#98, #91, #92, #93, #94) bumped `pyproject.toml` and left `uv.lock` untouched, although CLAUDE.md makes the lock the source of truth. Not a coincidence — `.github/dependabot.yml` declared `package-ecosystem: "pip"`, and **the pip ecosystem has no concept of `uv.lock`**, so it could never have updated it. It stayed invisible because `uv sync` *silently re-resolves* when `pyproject.toml` and the lock disagree: CI went green while the committed, hash-locked lockfile was being ignored — which defeats the point of committing it. Fixed at both levels: **(1)** the ecosystem is now `"uv"`, which updates `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` in the same PR; **(2)** `ci.yml`'s lint job runs **`uv lock --check`**, verified to exit **1** on a deliberately desynced lock and **0** when in sync, so a future bump that forgets the lock fails the build rather than passing quietly. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.148 ✅ | ESLint 8 → 9 with a flat config — what #97 actually required | Dependabot's #97 (`eslint-plugin-react-refresh` 0.4.26 → 0.5.7) **failed CI**, and correctly: `npm ci` died with `ERESOLVE`, because 0.5.7's peer is `eslint@^9 || ^10` while the project sat on `eslint@^8.57.1`. Not a bump — it needs the **ESLint 9 migration**, since 9 no longer reads `.eslintrc` by default and 10 drops it entirely. Migrated `.eslintrc.cjs` → `eslint.config.js` (flat), `eslint` 8.57 → 9.39.5, `eslint-plugin-react-refresh` → 0.5.7, added `@eslint/js@^9` (the default `@eslint/js@10` wants eslint ^10 and fails to resolve), and changed the lint script from `eslint src --ext ts,tsx` to `eslint src` (`--ext` is gone in flat config). **Deliberately kept `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` at 5.2.0**, whose peer range already allows eslint 9 — that decouples this from #16 (react-hooks 7, ~41 new `set-state-in-effect` errors), which stays its own task rather than riding along. **Two translation bugs caught by running it, not by reading:** (1) spreading `tsPlugin.configs.recommended.rules` by hand silently drops the `eslint-recommended` overrides that switch **off** core rules TypeScript already covers — producing **40 bogus `'React' is not defined  no-undef` errors**; using the plugin's own `configs['flat/recommended']` fixes it. (2) In react-hooks 5.2 the flat config is **`recommended-latest`**; `recommended` is still eslintrc-shaped with `plugins` as an array, which flat config rejects outright (`Key "plugins": Key "0": Expected an object`). **Equivalence was proven, not assumed:** `eslint -f json` captured before and after, normalised and diffed — **identical**, every finding matching on file, line, column, rule, severity *and* message (6 warnings, 0 errors). Verified: typecheck clean, 958 passed / 145 files, build succeeds. Docs: frontend `01_project_setup.md` (its tree still listed `.eslintrc.cjs`, and omitted `.nvmrc` and `.env.local.sample`). |
| 7.149 ✅ | eslint-plugin-react-hooks 5 → 7 (#16): the React Compiler rule set, and **16 real defects fixed** | v7 is not a bump — it brings the **React Compiler rules: 16, up from v5's two** (`rules-of-hooks`, `exhaustive-deps`), surfacing **41 errors**: 33 `set-state-in-effect`, 4 `refs`, 3 `purity`, 1 `immutability`. The flat config also **moved again** — in v7 it lives under `configs.flat[...]`, while the top-level `recommended` *and* `recommended-latest` are both eslintrc-shaped (`plugins` as an array), which flat config rejects with `Key "plugins": Key "0": Expected an object` (v5 had the flat one at `recommended-latest`). **16 fixed, several of them genuine bugs, not lint noise:** `CompanySettings` re-seeded its form from a TanStack Query result in an effect, so invalidating the config cache mid-edit silently replaced what the user had typed — the same class as the CBM-rate bug in 7.137; **my first fix reproduced a different bug** (keying on branding *contents* remounts after every save, discarding the "Saved" confirmation — caught by its own test), so it now keys on whether branding has *arrived*, and the post-save re-sync turned out to be unnecessary since the form already holds what was persisted. `useDateRangeFilter` and `ActivityLog` computed `Date.now()` during render, so a "Last 7 days" window **slid forward while the page sat open** — two loads with the same selection queried different ranges; now pinned when the preset is chosen. `colorScheme` stored `resolved` as state that duplicated a derivation, costing a second whole-app render on every theme change. `ActivityLog`'s category/entity-type reset cleared one render late, so the first query after switching category still used the stale entity type. Plus: ref writes during render (`useIdleTimeout`, `ActivityLog`), a ref **read** during render (`EvidenceUploader`, now state), `Date.now()` in a render-path SLA countdown (new `useNow` hook — one clock per table, and the countdown actually ticks), accumulator mutation in `TenantReports`' donut, and derived initial state in `PaymentSuccess`, `AuthCallback`, `ActivateScreen`, `InviteClaimScreen`, `Combobox`. **Two mistakes of my own, caught by the tools:** `tsc` rejected an unchecked index access in my donut rewrite, and my ActivityLog dep change introduced **2 new `preserve-manual-memoization` errors** by closing over an object — fixed by making every dep a primitive. **25 deferred, explicitly:** all one class — a hand-rolled `load()` useCallback invoked from an effect — whose correct fix is migrating to TanStack Query (see 7.150), not a lint tweak. Each carries an `eslint-disable-next-line … -- hand-rolled fetch effect; migrate to TanStack Query (master plan 7.150)`, so the rule still governs all new code and the debt is greppable. Lint is back to the **exact** pre-existing baseline — 0 errors, the same 6 warnings. Verified: typecheck clean, **958 passed / 145 files**, build succeeds. |
| 7.150 ✅ | Migrate 25 hand-rolled fetch effects to TanStack Query — **done, 0 suppressions left** (slices: 7.155 customer billing, 7.156 the remaining 23) | Surfaced by 7.149 and deferred there with explicit, greppable suppressions (`grep -rn 'migrate to TanStack Query' frontend/src`). All 25 share one shape: a `load()` `useCallback` that calls `setLoading(true)`/`setError(null)` synchronously and is invoked from `useEffect`, which cascades a render and duplicates logic the query layer already provides. **The project has a mature TanStack Query setup** — 24 domain hooks in `src/hooks/`, key factories in `src/api/keys.ts`, 27 files already using `useQuery` — but these pages predate it: only 2 of the 23 affected files import a query hook at all. `Warehouses.tsx` hand-rolls a loader calling `warehousesApi.list()` directly while **`useWarehouses` already exists and is unused by it**, and `handleSetDefault` duplicates `useSetDefaultWarehouse`. Worth doing area by area (customer, nigeria, china, tenant-admin, platform) rather than in one PR, each verified against the e2e suite. Affected: `ContainerDetail` ×2, `PackingListDetail`, `MyBilling`, `MyBillingDetail`, `MyClaims`, `MyShipments`, `MyShipmentDetail`, `BillingDetail`, `CarryoverLedger`, `ClaimDetail`, `ManifestDetail`, `RefundDetail`, `ReleaseNoteDetail`, `PlatformAuditLog`, `PlatformTenantDetail` ×2, `AdminCustomerDetail`, `ChargeTypes`, `ContainerCapacities`, `NotificationTemplates`, `StaffUserDetail`, `Warehouses`, `WarehouseDashboard`, `ActivityFeed`. |
| 7.151 ✅ | Root-caused the intermittent frontend test flake: a jsdom gap throwing inside `requestAnimationFrame` | Flagged three times while it escalated — `Errors 1 error` (exit 0), then 4, then **real failures** in `App.test.tsx` and `CompanySettings.test.tsx` that passed in isolation and on re-run, i.e. it had reached the point of reddening CI at random. **`Element.prototype.scrollIntoView` is absent in jsdom**, not a no-op, so `Warehouses.tsx:204` — which scrolls its error banner into view from inside a `requestAnimationFrame` — threw `TypeError` **after** the test's assertions had passed, in a callback nothing awaits. Vitest attributes such an error to whichever file is running, and it only appears when the frame fires before teardown, which is why it drifted between files and **never reproduced on demand** (3 consecutive clean runs while hunting it). Found by reading the code path instead of by repetition, then proved: a probe asserted `typeof document.createElement('div').scrollIntoView === 'undefined'`. **The optional chain was a false guard** — `el?.scrollIntoView(...)` tests the element, not the method. Fixed in `src/test/setup.ts` with a no-op installed only when missing, beside the existing `matchMedia`/`ResizeObserver` stubs — a jsdom gap, so the product code stays untouched and correct for browsers. Swept for the same class: `matchMedia` and `ResizeObserver` already stubbed, `IntersectionObserver` already behind a feature check with a fallback in `LandingPage.tsx`, `scrollTo`/`requestIdleCallback` unused. Verified with **four consecutive full runs: 958 passed, 0 errors, 0 failed files**; typecheck and lint unchanged. Docs: `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.152 ✅ | vite 6.4.5 → 8.3.6 with `@vitejs/plugin-react` 4 → 6 (#95) | #95 bumped **vite alone and could not install**: `@vitejs/plugin-react@4.7.0` peers at `vite ^4.2 || ^5 || ^6 || ^7` — and so does 5.1.0 — so vite 8 requires **plugin-react 6**, whose own peer is `vite ^8.0.0`. `vitest@4.1.11` already allowed `^6 || ^7 || ^8`, so it needed no change. **Correcting my own first read:** I described plugin-react 6 as dragging in a *required* rolldown/oxc toolchain (`oxc-transform-react`, `@rolldown/plugin-babel`, `babel-plugin-react-compiler`) and called it a separate project; all three are `peerDependenciesMeta.optional: true`, wanted only by the opt-in `compiler` option, so nothing extra was installed and the change was far smaller than claimed — checking `peerDependenciesMeta` before scoping would have avoided that. vite 8 **does** ship rolldown as its bundler, which the build output confirms (`build.rolldownOptions` in the chunk-size hint). Its `engines` are `^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0`, satisfied by the 22.23.4 pin from 7.140. **No regression — measurably better:** gzip **281.93 kB → 266.05 kB**, dist 1188 → 1180 KB, build **1.53s → 667ms**. A bundler swap is when local-dev scaffolding breaks quietly, so the two at-risk pieces were checked directly rather than inferred from a green build: the **`/__storage` proxy** (the Moto CORS workaround from 7.137) returns **HTTP 200** through `vite dev` carrying Moto's own `x-amzn-requestid`, proving the request reached the backend instead of being swallowed by the dev server; and the `define: { global: 'globalThis' }` shim for `amazon-cognito-identity-js` survives the build, with the auth e2e specs as its runtime check. **Gotcha worth recording:** vite 8's dev server binds **IPv6 `[::1]` only**, so a probe against `127.0.0.1` is refused with a bare `HTTP 000` and no useful error — use `localhost` (`lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN` shows the bind address). Verified: typecheck clean, **958 passed / 145 files**, lint unchanged (0 errors, same 6 warnings), build succeeds. |
| 7.153 ✅ | The 15 e2e skips were **false**, not missing fixtures — a one-shot `isVisible()` inside `test.skip()` | Flagged since 7.137 as "specs mutate seed state"; that diagnosis was **wrong**. On a freshly seeded stack 15 of 101 specs skipped, so they were never testing anything behind a green check. Several guards read `test.skip(!(await btn.isVisible().catch(() => false)), …)`, and `isVisible()` is a **one-shot check with no retry** — "the page has not finished rendering" was silently recorded as "this action does not apply". Proved with a probe rather than inferred: for a `paid` billing as `tenant_admin`, `gen visible immediately: false` → `after 3s: true` at the same URL. Because the **first** test in the release-note chain gave up early, every later test skipped on "run the generation test first" — **one premature check cost eight tests**. **A settle marker is not a fix and was tried first:** the unconditional `Export` button in the same action row becomes visible *before* the conditional actions beside it, so waiting on Export still reported the action missing — that dead end is kept in the PR history deliberately. New `actionAvailable()` helper waits for the action itself; 10 guards across 5 specs converted. **CI, clean environment: 15 skipped / 86 passed → 7 skipped / 94 passed.** **A real product bug found on the way:** `BillingList`'s `rowAction` navigated to `/billing/{id}` **without router state**, so `BillingDetail` always fell back to a `load()` that fetches the **first page** of the billing list and searches by id client-side — because the API has **no `GET /v1/billing/{billing-id}`**. Any older billing opened that way renders *"Billing record not found."*; (**correction, 2026-11-07:** this note said "the default page size of 20 (`shared/pagination.py`)" — `shared/pagination._DEFAULT_LIMIT` is 20, but `billing/handler.py` passes its own default of **50** for `GET /v1/billing`, which is the page size that actually applied here.) `AdminCustomerDetail` already passed state, this list was the outlier. Fixed here, but the complete fix is a by-id endpoint (direct-URL access still scans one page) — **not done, worth its own change**. Also measured the remaining non-idempotency: 15 → 17 skips across two runs without `make stack`, as run 1 consumes `bulk-packing-list-actions` and manifest-verify preconditions. The 7 residual skips are genuine fixture gaps: `scripts/seed.py` has no `create_claim` / `create_refund` / `create_release_note`. **Honest note:** 6 `auth-logout`/`customer-invite` specs failed on this machine after repeated `make stack` cycles and I could not attribute them (a stale-Cognito-env theory was tested and disproved by restarting Vite); they are untouched by this change and **CI passed them**, which is what settled it. Docs: `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.154 ✅ | `GET /v1/billing/{billing-id}/detail` — billing had **no by-id read at all**, and the workaround broke records past page 1 | Closes the "not done, worth its own change" left open by 7.153. The API had no endpoint returning one billing record by its **own** id: the bare `GET /v1/billing/{resource-id}` takes a *customer* id, and both are UUIDs, so the two shapes cannot be told apart — hence the `/detail` suffix rather than overloading that route. **The workaround was worse than "direct links are slow":** both `BillingDetail.tsx` *and* `MyBillingDetail.tsx` (the customer-facing one — a second instance 7.153 did not mention) fetched `GET /v1/billing` with **no `limit`**, so the handler's own default of 50 applied, and searched that page client-side. Record 51+ was simply unreachable — it exists, the user is entitled to it, and the page says it does not. **And it was not only an arrival-time problem:** that same `load()` is the refresh after recording a payment, confirming a payment claim, waiving storage fees and removing a charge (6 call sites), so an off-page record that displayed correctly from router state turned into *"Billing record not found."* the instant someone recorded a payment against it. It also cost ~200 queries to show one invoice (`_enrich_billing` issues 4 per row × 50 rows). Fixed with `service.get_billing` + `repo.get_billing_by_id`, which **needed the `customer` join added** — it selected only `b.*, c.container_number`, so without it the by-id read would have returned `customer_name`/`customer_phone`/`customer_email` as null while the same record carried them when listed. Shape parity is **verified, not assumed**: both responses diffed key-for-key against the live stack — no missing keys, no extra keys, no differing values. **Authorization matters more here than on a list**, since the id is guessable from a URL: the ownership check lives in the service (customer role resolved to its own `customer_id` and compared to the record's, the same force-scoping `list_billing` uses) and **fails closed** — a customer-role token with no `customer` row is denied, not served. Verified live on the seeded stack: owner → 200, a different customer → 403, `china_office_staff` → 403, **another tenant's staff reading this id → 404, not a leak**, unknown id → 404. The off-page mechanism was demonstrated directly rather than asserted (an id absent from a short page still served by `/detail`); it never reproduced locally because the seed holds only 3 billing rows, which is why it went unnoticed. Tests: 5 backend cases in `test_billing.py`, and `BillingDetail.test.tsx` now asserts the page calls `billingApi.get(id)` and **never** `billingApi.list()`. Verified: 1400 backend passed, 958 frontend passed / 145 files, ruff + mypy + typecheck + lint clean. Docs: `11_api_billing.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.155 ✅ | 7.150 slice 1 — customer billing on TanStack Query, and **`npx tsc --noEmit` checks nothing in this repo** | First slice of 7.150, done area-by-area as that entry prescribes. `MyBilling.tsx` now uses the **already-existing, unused-by-it `useBillingList`** hook — the same duplication 7.150 flagged in `Warehouses.tsx` — which fixed a real truncation on the way: the page fetched one page with **no `limit`** and rendered it with no "Load more", so a customer with more than 50 invoices could not reach the older ones; it now uses the shared `ListFooter`, matching the staff list. `MyBillingDetail.tsx` turned out to have **three** hand-rolled fetch effects, not the one that was flagged — the bank-config and payment-claims effects escaped the lint rule only because they set state inside `.then()` callbacks rather than synchronously. All three are queries now; the record sits at `billingKeys.detail(id)`, **the same cache slot the staff `BillingDetail` uses**, so a mutation on either side is seen by both, with router state as `initialData` to keep the instant first paint. New `useBankTransferConfig()` shares `configKeys.tenantConfig()` with `useBranding()`, so the page reuses the config the shell already fetched. `showTransfer` became **derived** (`transferOpened || claims.length > 0`) instead of state an effect pushed to true — correct because the panel only ever opens. **A verification failure of my own, worth recording: `npx tsc --noEmit` in `frontend/` checks ZERO files.** `tsconfig.json` is solution-style (`"files": []` + references to `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.node.json`), so plain `tsc` resolves no inputs, prints nothing and exits 0 — indistinguishable from a clean run. Every "typecheck clean" I reported from that command this session was a **false pass**; `npm run typecheck` (`tsc -b --noEmit`) caught a real dropped type import (`Cannot find name 'BankTransferConfig'`) on the first honest run. **7.154 was unaffected** — CI runs the npm script, and its `build-and-test` passed — but the local signal I cited for it was worthless. Two follow-on traps documented with it: `tsc -b` is incremental and can report success from cache after a fix (`--force` to be sure), and `cmd | tail; echo $?` reports *tail's* exit code, not the command's. Also corrected `LOCAL_TESTING.md`'s long-stale "Should show 356 passed" to 1400. **Test-harness consequence:** a page that uses queries throws `No QueryClient set` without a provider — `MyBillingDetail.test.tsx` and `MyBillingDetailPaymentClaim.test.tsx` both gained one plus stubs for the newly-queried endpoints. Suppressions **25 → 23**. Verified: typecheck clean (`tsc -b --force`, real exit code), lint 0 errors / same 6 warnings, **958 passed / 145 files**, build succeeds. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`. |
| 7.156 ✅ | 7.150 finished — the remaining 23 suppressions, and **two real bugs found on the way** | 21 files, ~10k lines, done simplest-first in five verified batches so the pattern was settled before `BillingDetail` (1804 lines) and `ContainerDetail` (1127). **The migration mostly deleted code rather than moving it**, because `api/client.ts` already calls `invalidateForRequest()` inside `fetchWithAuth` after every successful non-GET (client.ts:216) and `api/cacheInvalidation.ts` already maps every path — so once a page reads through a key *under* the invalidated root, the manual `load()` beside each mutation is exactly what the invalidation already does. `BillingDetail` is the clearest case: all six `await load()` calls each already sat next to an `invalidateBillingCaches()`, and all six simply went. **Bug 1 (user-visible, silently hidden):** `MyShipments` called `billingApi.listForCustomer(session.userId)`, but that endpoint takes a *customer* id and the backend compares it against the customer row resolved from `user_id` — different values, so it returned **403 on every call**, verified live (`/v1/billing/{userId}` → 403, `/{customerId}` → 200, `/v1/billing` → 200). `Promise.allSettled` plus non-fatal handling swallowed it, so `billing` was always `[]`: the greeting's outstanding-balance line never appeared and the per-shipment billing lookup never resolved.
