Fixed to the force-scoped `GET /v1/billing` its own sibling already used, and guarded by a test. **Bug 2 (latent, avoided):** `StaffUserDetail` seeded an *editable* locale select from inside the fetch, so deriving it outright would have discarded a half-made choice on every refetch — the CompanySettings class from 7.149; it now keeps a `localeDraft` that holds only a choice the user actually made. **One genuine exception to the automatic invalidation, found by reading the upload path rather than assuming:** the container document upload is a direct PUT to presigned storage that never passes through the API client, and the presign POST fires the invalidation *before* the bytes land — so `DocumentsTab` invalidates explicitly, and that is now documented as the rule's one known hole. New `src/test/queryWrapper.tsx` (`withQuery`, `retry: false`) adopted by 13 test harnesses; without a provider a migrated page throws `No QueryClient set` with a stack pointing at the component rather than the test. **Four mistakes of my own, every one caught by the compiler or a test, none shipped:** an import-rewriting script that stripped `useEffect` unconditionally from a file with three other effects; a blanket `setError`→`setActionError` rename that twice hit *sub-components' own local state* in the same file (I repeated that one before scoping the rename to the page component); destructuring `useQueries`' mapped array, which trips `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`; and a regression assertion that checked `listForCustomer` was `undefined` when the spread mock keeps it defined, so it could not tell "not called" from "absent". One test assertion legitimately changed: `WarehouseDashboard`'s counts are now `findByText`, since three independent queries let labels paint a tick before their values where one `Promise.all` could not. **`ActivityLog.tsx` is deliberately untouched** — it hand-rolls a loader but was never among the 25 and carries no suppression, because 7.149 fixed its render-purity problems another way. Verified: `tsc -b --force` clean with the real exit code, lint 0 errors / same 6 warnings, **959 passed / 145 files**, build succeeds. Docs: `frontend/09_state_management_and_data_fetching.md` (its "Known limitation" and "Pages refactored" passages both said the opposite of what is now true and were corrected in place), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.157 ✅ | RLS credential switchover — the application now connects as `logistics_app`, so row-level security is **actually enforced** | Migration 059 built every prerequisite and said so; this is the credential half it was waiting for. **What was wrong, measured not assumed:** as `postgres`, `SET app.current_tenant_id` to tenant 1 and `SELECT count(*), count(DISTINCT tenant_id) FROM billing` returned **4 rows across 2 tenants**. `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` makes the table *owner* subject to its policies, but `BYPASSRLS` defeats them, and the local Docker superuser has it — so RLS was inert locally and in CI, and all isolation in effect came from the application's own `tenant_id` predicates. **An important correction to my own first framing:** this was *not* "production is unprotected". A managed RDS master user does not get `BYPASSRLS`, so with 059's FORCE applied the predicate should already have been engaging in AWS — documented RDS behaviour, **not verified against the live cluster**, so recorded as likely rather than confirmed. The real wins are that local and CI now match production (layer 3 is exercisable at all for the first time) and that least privilege no longer rests on the master user *happening* not to have BYPASSRLS. **After:** tenant 1 → 3 rows / 1 tenant; tenant 2 → 1 row / 1 tenant; no tenant set → **0 rows (fails closed)**; `PLATFORM` → 4 rows / 2 tenants, so the deliberate cross-tenant paths 059 called out (public tracking, activation, cross-tenant user lookup) still work — public tracking verified end-to-end through the real HTTP endpoint. **Three design decisions worth keeping:** (1) *migrations keep running as the owner by design, not omission* — DDL cannot be run by a DML-only role, so the migrate Lambda stays on `postgres` and additionally reads the app secret, because migration 060 is what sets the app role's password; (2) *unset `APP_DB_PASSWORD` is not an error* — 060 logs a notice and leaves the role NOLOGIN, so an environment whose secret is not provisioned still migrates instead of blocking its own deploy; (3) *rollback is a config change, not a migration* — repoint `DATABASE_USER` and apply, nothing to undo in the DB under pressure. The password is bound as a parameter into a transaction-local `SET` and quoted inside the DO block with `format('%L', …)`, so it is never interpolated into SQL by Python and never logged. **The RDS Proxy needed a second `auth` block** — without it the proxy refuses a user it holds no secret for, and the failure reads as a bad password rather than a missing entry; that trap is in the runbook. New `shared/db_credentials.py` resolves `DATABASE_ADMIN_*` with a fallback to `DATABASE_*`, so an environment that has not been cut over behaves exactly as before. **CI now exercises the real thing:** the isolation suite runs as `logistics_app` with the owner only in the fixtures, so the three new `TestTheRealApplicationCredentialIsSubjectToRls` cases run rather than skip. Six existing tests errored with *"new row violates row-level security policy"* on first run — the switchover working correctly, and useless for building a two-tenant fixture, so `_admin_conn()` now resolves the owner explicitly. `TestLogisticsAppRoleCannotLogIn`'s `test_the_role_exists_but_has_no_login` asserted the correct *post-059* state and is deliberately inverted by 060; replaced with an assertion of what matters — neither superuser nor `BYPASSRLS`. **Not done: Azure — and that was a scoping error, corrected 2026-11-08 after the merge.** I switched AWS over and deferred Azure as "lower urgency". The post-merge pipeline showed why that was wrong: `vars.DEPLOY_CLOUD` is **`azure`**, every AWS deploy job is gated on `DEPLOY_CLOUD != 'azure'` (and AWS additionally only deploys on a version tag), so the AWS `Deploy Pipeline` and `Terraform` runs **skipped** while `Deploy Pipeline (Azure)` succeeded. The AWS half of this entry is therefore correct but **inert** — staged in `main`, never applied — and the half that governs the live environment is the half I left out. The evidence was available before I decided: I had already read `_deploy-azure-env.yml` and noted "Azure deploys too", and should have checked `DEPLOY_CLOUD` rather than assuming AWS was primary. What *is* live from this entry: local and CI enforce RLS for real, and migration 060 ran in the Azure deploy and **correctly no-opped** (no `APP_DB_SECRET_ARN` there), leaving Azure's behaviour unchanged — the no-op path doing its job for a reason I had not anticipated. See 7.158, which is not a nice-to-have but the item that delivers this change where it counts. Verified: 1400 unit, 88 isolation+integration, **95 passed / 6 skipped e2e against the restricted role** (the 2 `smoke`-project failures are the preview server on :4173 not running locally, unrelated), ruff + mypy + terraform validate clean. Docs: `05c_database_rls_indexes_seed.md`, `docs/runbook.md` (symptoms, causes, rollback), `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.160 ✅ | e2e skips **7 → 1** — and two of the seven had the data they claimed was missing | Filed by 7.153 as "genuine missing fixtures: `seed.py` has no `create_claim`/`create_refund`/`create_release_note`". That was **half wrong**, found by checking the data behind each skip instead of trusting its message. **Two were races.** `customer-refund-request` skipped with "No billing records for the seeded customer identity" while that identity had two, confirmed against `GET /v1/billing`. Its guard was `test.skip((await card.count()) === 0, …)` — and **`count()` has exactly the flaw 7.153 identified in `isVisible()`**, which that entry converted only for `isVisible`. All **15** `count()` skip guards are now `hasRows()`. Ten further one-shot `isVisible()` checks survived inside `if (...)` rather than `test.skip`, where the failure is **silent and worse**: the step is not performed, the test continues, and something later fails for an unrelated reason — precisely how `claims-and-refunds`'s accounts-review step was being skipped, leaving the refund `pending_review` so the next guard blamed "No pending_approval refund found". All ten now use `actionAvailable()` or a new `becomesVisible()`. One carried a comment **correctly describing the one-shot trap and then calling `isVisible()` anyway** — right diagnosis, wrong code, bug live. **One was a spec testing an ineligible record:** it clicked `.first()` ACME billing card, a fully-paid invoice, while "Request refund" is gated on `outstanding > 0 && !isPaid`; it now filters to a card with an outstanding balance. **Four were real seed gaps:** new `create_refund_request` / `create_release_note` helpers plus a draft packing list, two extra `pending_approval` lists (the bulk specs *consume* the single one that existed, starving the next spec and the next run), a `pending_review` refund, and an `active` two-tag release note — `active` because a picked-up note cannot split, two tags because splitting moves one out. **Mistakes recorded rather than tidied:** my first release note went on INV-A-001 and the spec kept skipping, because it navigates to ACME001002 / INV-A-003 — the fixture existed, just not where the spec looks; and I guessed `customer_choice='bank_transfer'`, hit a CHECK violation, and the real enums are now documented on the helper. **The last skip is left open, honestly:** `manifest-verify-and-advance.spec.ts:119` passes in isolation right after `manifest-import` but skips in a full run with the data present in the API — a race with a tail beyond the 7s guard. Suspected cause is `waitForTableSettled()`'s own one-shot `count()` early-out in the *shared* helper, which would explain intermittency across unrelated specs; a bounded wait there **did not fix it and was reverted** rather than kept as an unjustified change. **Variance note:** across four fresh-stack runs I saw 1/100 three times and one run with an extra `billing-payment-and-release` failure that did not reproduce (that file passes 3/3 in isolation on fresh seed) — so one red run here is a re-run, not a verdict, and one green run is weaker evidence than it looks. Verified: 962 frontend unit / 146 files, typecheck + lint clean, `make stack` green. Docs: `17_testing.md`, including correcting 7.153's "remaining 7 are genuine missing fixtures" conclusion in place. |
| 7.161 ✅ | `.env.local.sample` shipped a password mismatch — a regression of mine from 7.156/#126, and the e2e job that would have caught it does not run on backend PRs | **Found by CI on #129, not by me.** The `e2e-local-stack` job came back **25 failed / 54 passed / 22 skipped** across specs #129 never touched (`auth-logout`, `customer-invite`, `smoke-navigation`), with every Flask request answering `FATAL: password authentication failed for user "logistics_app"`. **Cause:** the sample carried `DATABASE_PASSWORD=paste-the-generated-value-here` and `APP_DB_PASSWORD=paste-the-same-generated-value-here` — two *different* literals — so migration 060 set one password and the app connected with the other. Both `make env` (`Makefile:24`) and `frontend-ci.yml` copy that file **unedited**, so this broke every fresh clone, not just CI. The docs were right all along (`LOCAL_TESTING.md` already said the two "must equal"); the sample contradicted them. **Why my local runs were green:** my own `.env.local` has matching values because I hand-edited it while building #126 — the fourth time this session my local environment was not the one everyone else gets. **Why it landed silently:** `frontend-ci.yml` triggered on `frontend/**` only, and #126 touched no frontend file, so the one job that exercises the real backend never ran on it; the break sat on `main` until the next frontend PR inherited it. **Three fixes, at three depths:** (1) the sample pre-fills both with the same local-only literal, so a verbatim copy works; (2) migration 060 now prints a `WARNING:` naming the mismatch when `DATABASE_USER=logistics_app` and the two disagree — a notice, not an error, since an environment awaiting cutover legitimately has no `APP_DB_PASSWORD`; (3) the workflow's path filter gained the files that govern stack bring-up (`.env.local.sample`, `migrations/**`, `scripts/**`, `shared/db*.py`, compose, `Makefile`) — deliberately **not** `services/**`, which would put a ~12-minute e2e run on every backend PR. The two path lists are duplicated by hand because GitHub Actions' parser rejects YAML anchors, which my first attempt used and every local YAML tool accepted. **Verified both directions** against a CI replica (`.env.local` discarded and re-copied from the sample, fresh `make up`/`bootstrap`/`reset`/`migrate`/`seed`): the old placeholder still reproduces the exact `psql` auth failure, the guard fires on a forced mismatch, and the API answers 200 on both a tenant-scoped and a PLATFORM route with zero auth failures in `flask.log`. Docs: `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `docs/runbook.md` (mismatch added as the first and cheapest cause of that `FATAL`). |
| 7.158 ✅ | **Azure credential provisioned (phase 1) — and 7.157's AWS half re-scoped to phase 1 after I found an outage window in it** | Azure is the cloud that deploys (`DEPLOY_CLOUD=azure`), so this is where the switchover has to land. Mirrors the AWS shape exactly: a `random_password` + `postgres-app-password` Key Vault secret in the postgres module, new `application_login`/`application_password` outputs, and `db-app-password` added to the container-app secret set. The migrate Container App Job shares `plain_env`/`secret_env` with the app containers, so it gets `migrate_env`/`migrate_secret_env` overrides — following the existing `claims_env` precedent — pinning it to the owner and giving it `APP_DB_PASSWORD`. **The important finding is why this is phase 1 and not the whole thing.** Reading the Azure deploy workflow rather than assuming it: the order is `terraform apply → build → migrate → roll`, and a Container App env change takes effect *at apply*, creating and activating a new revision. Flipping `DATABASE_USER` in the same change would therefore have started the API as `logistics_app` minutes before migration 060 gave that role a password — a self-inflicted outage for the duration of the image build plus the migration. **Checking AWS for the same flaw turned up something worse:** `deploy.yml` does not run Terraform at all. The env flip (`terraform.yml`) and the migration (`deploy.yml`) are in *separate pipelines*, so a Terraform apply alone would repoint every Lambda at a password-less role with no migration anywhere in that pipeline to repair it — open-ended rather than minutes. 7.157's AWS config, merged but unapplied, had exactly that trap in it, so it is re-scoped here to phase 1 while that is still free to do. **Both clouds now provision the credential and repoint nothing**; the flip is 7.159. The migrate overrides are written as deliberate no-ops in phase 1 so that phase 2 touches only the app-facing defaults and cannot accidentally leave migrations running as a DML-only role. Docs corrected in place where they claimed AWS was already switched over: `05c`, `04e`, and `docs/runbook.md`, which gained a "know which phase you are in" section with the `pg_roles` / `aws lambda get-function-configuration` / `az containerapp show` checks and per-cloud rollback. Verified: `terraform validate` clean on both stacks, `terraform fmt` clean, 1400 unit + 88 isolation/integration unaffected. |
| 7.159 ✅ | Phase 2 of the RLS switchover — `DATABASE_USER` now names `logistics_app`, so migration 059's policies finally bind | The two-line flip phase 1 was built to make safe. **Azure:** `plain_env.DATABASE_USER` → `var.db_app_user` and `secret_env.DATABASE_PASSWORD` → `"db-app-password"`; the `migrate_env` / `migrate_secret_env` overrides written in 7.158 while still a no-op are now load-bearing — without them the migrate job would inherit a DML-only role and every migration would fail on DDL. **AWS:** `common_env_vars.DATABASE_USER` → `"logistics_app"`, `DB_SECRET_ARN` → `var.db_app_secret_arn`, and the service-role IAM `Resource` narrowed to the app secret alone — the least-privilege half, and the half a working app would never have forced. The migrate Lambda keeps both secrets by design. `terraform validate` passes on both stacks, `fmt -check` clean. **What this run exposed, which matters more than the flip:** `Terraform (Azure)` has failed **12 consecutive times since 2026-10-14**, every one of them `azure/login` failing with empty `ARM_CLIENT_ID`/`ARM_TENANT_ID`/`ARM_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`. It is **plan-only** so nothing was silently mis-applied — the real apply lives in `_deploy-azure-env.yml` — but the infra-review gate on every Azure PR has been dead for ~4 weeks, which is why #128's Terraform was never actually reviewed by CI. **Needs repo-secret access to fix; flagged, not fixable from here.** **New hazard, documented rather than assumed away:** with both phases now in `main`, the separation is between two *deploys*, not a property of the code — so a brand-new environment (fresh account, new region, rebuilt stack, or one whose applies have been failing) receives both at once and comes up on a role 060 has not yet given a password. `docs/runbook.md` gains a two-step first-apply procedure gated on `rolcanlogin = t`. Docs: `docs/runbook.md`. |
| 7.162 ✅ | The smoke tests passed a completely broken database credential — found while gating 7.159 | Both suites (`_smoke-test.yml`, and the smoke step in `_deploy-azure-env.yml`) asserted `/health` → 200 and a handful of authenticated routes → 401. **Neither touches the database:** `/health` is a static response and a 401 comes from the auth layer *before* any query runs. **Demonstrated, not reasoned about:** a local server started with `DATABASE_PASSWORD=deliberately_wrong_password` still answered `/health` → **200**, while `/v1/invites/<bogus>` → **500**. This was found by asking what would catch 7.159 going wrong — the whole failure mode of pointing `DATABASE_USER` at a password-less `logistics_app` is "every query fails, nothing else changes", which was invisible to every existing check. Both suites now probe `GET /v1/invites/{token}` with an absent token: **404** = authenticated to Postgres and found nothing (healthy), **5xx** = cannot reach or log in (fail the deploy), with the failure message naming `rolcanlogin` as the thing to check. **Deliberately not `/v1/shipments/{tracking-code}`**, the obvious candidate: it 404s at *routing* without ever reaching the database, so it would have passed just as happily — the two 404s are indistinguishable by status alone and only the body (`"route '...'"` vs `"customer_invite '...'"`) separates them. Probing both locally before choosing is the only reason the right one was picked. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.163 ⬜ | **Phase 2 rolled back — `logistics_app` cannot log in to Azure dev.** Re-attempt once the credential is verified | Merged 7.159 on the inference that phase 1's successful deploy meant migration 060 had set the role's password. **The inference was wrong, and the probe added in 7.162 caught it:** `health: 200` / `db reachability: 500` → deploy failed. Every pre-existing smoke check passed — without 7.162 this would have gone green with an API that could not reach its database. **Root cause not yet identified** (needs Azure access): either 060 took its no-op branch — it logs a notice and *still succeeds* when `APP_DB_PASSWORD` is absent, so a green migration step proves nothing — or the role's password and the `db-app-password` secret have diverged via `random_password.app`. Rolled back by reverting the two Terraform env changes; the probe was kept. **Gate for re-attempt:** `SELECT rolcanlogin FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'logistics_app'` returns `t` **and** a manual `psql` as that role succeeds — observed, not inferred. **Lesson, recorded because it is the second time this session:** phase 1 'succeeding' and the precondition holding are different claims, and I merged on the first while explicitly knowing I had not checked the second. Docs: `docs/runbook.md` (incident + diagnostic order). |
| 7.164 ✅ | `Terraform (Azure)` was permanently red for a month — and being permanently red is what hid it | 12 consecutive failures, 2026-10-14 → 2026-11-09. **Per-job, only `terraform-staging` ever failed:** its GitHub Environment has no `AZURE_*` secrets because staging does not exist yet, so `azure/login` fails and Terraform never receives `ARM_*` (how the `azurerm` provider authenticates to Azure Resource Manager). `terraform-dev` succeeded in **all 12** — the dev plan review was working the whole time, and `terraform-prod` was merely skipped by `needs`. **I misread this myself, which is the point:** while gating 7.159 I reported "the Azure infra-review gate has been dead for a month, #128's Terraform was never reviewed by CI". Wrong — I had read the run-level conclusion, which is the only thing a PR check or run list shows. A check that is always red carries no information: it cannot report that something broke, because it is already broken. Corrected by reading per-job conclusions. **Fix:** `terraform-staging` / `terraform-prod` gated on repo variables `AZURE_STAGING_READY` / `AZURE_PROD_READY` — unset, they skip cleanly and the workflow is green, so the dev plan is a live signal again. Repo *variables*, not a secret-presence check, because a job-level `if` cannot see Environment secrets (the environment is not resolved until the job starts). **`deploy-azure.yml` deliberately left alone:** its staging/prod jobs fire only on an explicit `-rc`/release tag or dispatch, never unprompted, and there failing loudly is correct — silently skipping a *requested* deploy would be worse. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. |
| 7.165 ✅ | Five dead FE-01 placeholder files deleted — each had been implemented somewhere better and the placeholder never removed | `lib/idempotency.ts`, `lib/rfc7807.ts`, `lib/pagination.ts`, `types/api.ts`, `api/paymentRelease.ts`: every one a two-line `// TODO(FE-05)` + `export {}` with **zero importers**. Re-run as `from '...lib/idempotency'` every one was **0**. | 7.167 ✅ | The last e2e skip — a correct guard on a precondition that never existed. **0 skipped / 101 passed** | A `manifest` row is created in exactly one place: `_apply_approval_side_effects`, the only caller of `create_manifest`, which runs when a **packing list is approved**. Importing a forwarder manifest does not create one — it creates the container and a packing list. So the test waited for a row nothing had produced and skipped on every clean serial run, while its message said `run manifest-import.spec.ts first` — that spec HAD run, immediately before, passing. The message named the wrong fixture, which is why an earlier attempt to fix this failed. **Pinned down from a Playwright trace, read rather than reasoned about:** `GET /v1/manifests` fired at `23:39:22.169` returning 200 with two rows, neither this container; the row was written at `23:39:34.024` — **11.9s later, by the next test in the same file**, which approves the packing list as its own setup. **Four hypotheses died on evidence, each of which would have produced a wrong fix:** pagination (4 rows vs `limit=50`, `created_at DESC` — it would have been *first*); tenant mismatch (same Acme tenant; the `…011`/`…012` values I first suspected are *user* ids); worker congestion (CI is `workers: 1` and skips identically); and `waitForTableSettled`'s one-shot `count()` — the theory inherited from 7.160 and the reason that fix attempt was reverted. The mechanism was never involved. **The signal that cost the most time was a pass:** running the file alone succeeded, because the *previous* full run's second test had left a manifest in the DB — isolation was exercising leftover state. A test that passes alone and skips in a suite is not automatically a flake; it can be one whose precondition is supplied by whatever ran before it. **Fix:** the first test approves the packing list over the API before navigating, as the second already did — the file's own docstring said "a test should not depend on a sibling having actually run", applied to the second test and not the first. The guard stays and now means what it says. **This is recovered coverage, not a hidden skip:** test 43 runs and passes, exercising manifest verification through the UI for the first time. Docs: `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.166 ✅ | `terraform.yml` / `terraform-azure.yml` did not trigger on changes to themselves — so 7.164's fix could not be verified | Noticed by checking whether 7.164 actually worked and finding the workflow had **not re-run at all**. Its `paths` filter is `infrastructure/azure/**` + `infrastructure/common/**`; #133 touched only the workflow file, so nothing triggered, and the fix would have stayed unverified until some unrelated infra change happened along. **A CI fix you cannot trigger is a CI fix you have not tested.** `frontend-ci.yml` already self-referenced; both terraform workflows now do too. Found by asking "did the previous fix work?" rather than assuming a merged fix is a working one — the same habit that caught the smoke-test gap in 7.162. |
| 7.10 ✅ | Reports & Notification Log | TenantReports (DonutChart, pipeline bar chart, CBM/FX table, claims summary), NotificationLog (status filter dropdown, message_body preview, chat-bubble modal, suspended rows) |
| 7.124 ✅ | Shipping-company (tenant) onboarding — admin/sales-led | Companies had no public entry point (tenant creation was platform-admin-only; `/register/:slug` is customer-scoped), so the two audiences were blurred. Added a public **lead** flow + platform review/provision, with the two kept unmistakably separate per explicit user requirement. **Backend:** migration 054 `tenant_onboarding_request` (platform-scoped, no RLS); public `POST /v1/onboarding-requests` (no JWT, Pydantic-validated, no PII logged, best-effort `OnboardingRequested` event — **no** account/tenant created); platform `GET/GET{id}/PATCH /v1/platform/onboarding-requests`; `POST /v1/platform/tenants` extended with `onboarding_request_id` to link + flip the lead to `provisioned`. Public route registered in `shared/http_routing.py` **and** `api-gateway` TF. **Frontend:** `RequestOnboardingScreen` (`/get-started`, no password), `OnboardingRequests` platform queue (status filter, mark-contacted/reject/provision) with a live pending-count sidebar badge, `CreateTenantPage` pre-fill from a lead; bare `/register` now a which-are-you chooser; `LoginScreen` dual CTAs. **Tests:** `test_onboarding.py` (10) + 3 frontend suites + a nav test — full backend suite + 861 frontend tests green; ruff/mypy/tsc/eslint clean. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `05b_database_schema_extended.md`, `15_api_notifications.md`, `17_testing.md`, frontend `06_routing_rbac_and_navigation.md` + `07_domain_modules.md`. |
| 7.112 ✅ | Dependency tooling migrated to uv (replaces pip + requirements*.txt) | On its own branch (`chore/uv-migration`). The project was never on Poetry — it was plain pip + four `requirements*.txt`; migrated fully to **uv**. Deps now live in `pyproject.toml` (`[project].dependencies` runtime, `[project.optional-dependencies].azure`, `[dependency-groups]` dev + layer) with a committed, hash-locked **`uv.lock`** (144 pkgs); `[tool.uv] package = false` (deployed app, not a library). Deleted all four requirements files. **CI (`ci.yml`) rewritten** to `astral-sh/setup-uv` + `uv sync`/`uv run`; the Lambda **layer + migrate zips** build via `uv export` → `uv pip install --python-platform aarch64-manylinux_2_28 --python-version 3.13` (the python3.13/AL2023 target — the old `manylinux2014` tag has no arm64 wheel for pillow 12.3). Converted `Makefile`, `frontend-ci.yml` (e2e-local-stack now `uv sync` + `.venv/bin` on `$GITHUB_PATH`), and `scripts/build_lambda_zip.sh`. **Security win:** the safety scan now runs over `uv export` (runtime + transitives + azure = 40 pkgs, vs the old narrow 8-pkg `requirements.txt` scan) — which surfaced a real transitive CVE in `requests==2.31.0` (via azure-core), bumped to **2.34.2**; scan now reports **0**. Docs swept (CLAUDE.md dependency standard, `01_project_setup.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `18_cicd_pipeline.md`, `02_shared_utilities.md`, azure README, excel doc, a runtime error string); historical task-log/findings entries left as dated records. **Verified:** `uv sync` resolves; `uv run pytest` → 1182 pass; `uv run ruff`/`uv run mypy` clean; `uv export --group layer` + arm64 `uv pip install` confirmed locally (pillow/cryptography/psycopg2 arm64 wheels land); `safety` 0 vulns. Docs: `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `CLAUDE.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `18_cicd_pipeline.md`, `01_project_setup.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.111 ✅ | Pipeline fixes — security (cryptography CVEs) + test (SQLAlchemy 2.1 psycopg driver) | First real CI run on the PR surfaced two failures, both fixed. **(1) `security` stage:** `safety` flagged 6 CVEs in `cryptography==46.0.5` (incl. CVE-2026-69247, a Bleichenbacher oracle, affected `<50.0.0`). Bumped the exact pin to `cryptography==50.0.2` in `requirements.txt` + `requirements-layer.txt` (the only version clearing all six); `safety check -r requirements.txt` now reports **0 vulnerabilities**; Fernet + Key Vault wrap/unwrap tests pass on 50.0.2. **(2) `test` stage:** `scripts/migrate.py` died with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'psycopg'` — **SQLAlchemy 2.1 changed the default `postgresql://` driver from psycopg2 → psycopg (v3)**, and SQLAlchemy is unpinned (pulled via alembic), so fresh CI installs 2.1 while the project installs psycopg2-binary. Pinned the driver explicitly in `migrations/env.py` (`postgresql+psycopg2://`) — version-independent, and it also fixes the **production migrate Lambda** (same env.py). Verified: engine resolves to the psycopg2 dialect (no psycopg3 import) and `alembic current` runs end-to-end against local Postgres (`053 (head)`). Updated the runbook Findings Log 0c (crypto now FIXED). Docs: `docs/runbook.md`. |
| 7.123 ✅ | Azure deploy hardening + from-scratch SETUP runbook | Resolved the real-bring-up issues and documented the whole bootstrap so a fresh subscription is reproducible. **Deploy identity stays Contributor-only** (admin does privileged bootstrap out-of-band): the pipelines avoid RBAC role assignments entirely — ACR via admin creds (`grant_acr_pull=false`), and the frontend `$web` upload authenticates with the **storage account key via `listKeys`** (a Contributor permission) rather than `--auth-mode login` (which needs a data-plane role the identity lacks). **`frontend-deploy.yml` (azure)** rewritten to read the storage account / Front Door / API URL from **Terraform state** (not unset `AZURE_FRONTEND_*` secrets — the cause of the `--account-name ""` failure), build the SPA against the Azure `api_url`, upload with `--account-key` (masked via `::add-mask::`), and purge Front Door; needs only the `AZURE_*` OIDC secrets. New **`infrastructure/azure/SETUP.md`** — the ordered, copy-pasteable new-subscription runbook (variables → PAYG upgrade → `az provider register` loop → tfstate account → Contributor-only OIDC app + the only two grants it needs [Contributor + Storage Blob Data Contributor on the tfstate RG] → `gh` DEPLOY_CLOUD + per-env secrets → auth [Cognito-bridge or native Entra] → first deploy + verify) with a **troubleshooting table** covering every gotcha hit during bring-up (provider registration, Front Door Free-Trial block, Redis retirement, ACR 403, `404 WebContentNotFound`, storage-upload 403, Service Bus `-sb` name, migration-poll timeout, `Output not found`). README links to it as the runbook. **Verified:** `terraform fmt -check` + `validate` pass; workflow YAML parses. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/SETUP.md` (new), `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.122 ✅ | Native per-cloud auth — Entra session reload-persistence (backend) | Closes the follow-up from 7.121: the backend httpOnly-cookie session refresh (`services/auth/handler.py`) was Cognito-specific, so an Entra session didn't survive a page reload. Made it **provider-aware** — `handler._refresher()` selects by `shared.providers.AUTH_PROVIDER` (default `CLOUD_PROVIDER`): **`cognito_refresh`** on AWS, new **`entra_refresh.py`** on Azure. `entra_refresh.refresh_id_token` exchanges the cookie's refresh token via the OAuth2 **refresh_token grant** (stdlib urllib, no dep) → fresh id_token; `invalid_grant`→`AuthenticationRequiredError`, misconfig/other→`ExternalServiceError`; `revoke_refresh_token` is a documented no-op (Entra has no revoke-by-value; `global_sign_out` on the identity provider handles per-user revocation). So on Azure a reload silently restores the session exactly as on Cognito. Wired backend env `ENTRA_TOKEN_ENDPOINT`/`ENTRA_CLIENT_ID`/`ENTRA_SCOPE` through the container_app module (+ root `entra_token_endpoint`/`entra_client_id` vars). The handler-route tests were re-pointed from `handler.cognito_refresh.*` (removed top-level import → lazy) to the module path. **Verified:** 1257 unit tests (+6 new `test_entra_refresh.py`: ROPC refresh success/invalid_grant/misconfig/no-op revoke + handler picks entra vs cognito by `AUTH_PROVIDER`), ruff + mypy clean (services/auth), `terraform fmt -check` + `validate` pass. AWS/Cognito path byte-identical. **With this, Entra on Azure is at full session parity with Cognito** (login + reload-persistence + silent refresh); the one remaining caveat is ROPC = local accounts only (swap to MSAL redirect for social/MFA), pending live-tenant validation. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `docs/project docs/frontend/04_auth_and_session.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.121 ✅ | Native per-cloud auth — Entra login path in the SPA (frontend) | User's requirement: Azure deploy → Entra auth, AWS deploy → Cognito auth (native, not the Cognito bridge). The backend was already native per-cloud (`AUTH_PROVIDER` default = `CLOUD_PROVIDER` → Entra verify + claims mapper on Azure); the gap was the **frontend**, which logged in only via Cognito. Added a **cloud-agnostic auth facade** `frontend/src/auth/authClient.ts` selecting the provider by `VITE_AUTH_PROVIDER` (`cognito` default / `entra`). New **`EntraClient.ts`** implements the same `signIn/refreshSession/signOut` surface via the OAuth2 **ROPC** grant (plain `fetch`, no new dep, no MSAL redirect) so the existing email/password `LoginScreen` works unchanged against Entra for local accounts; password-reset is Entra-hosted (challenge fns reject). **`jwt.ts`** `buildSession` now reads both Cognito (`sub`/`custom:tenant_id`/`cognito:groups`/`custom:role`) and Entra (`oid`/`tenant_id`/`groups`/`role`) claim shapes via fallbacks (backward-compatible). The four auth call sites (`SessionProvider`, Forgot/Reset screens) import from the facade; `SessionProvider.test` re-pointed to mock `authClient`. **Verified:** `npm run typecheck` clean, lint 0 errors, 100 auth-suite tests green (+ new `EntraClient.test.ts` ROPC/error tests + `jwt.test.ts` Entra-claims tests). **Known follow-ups (documented, honest):** reload-persistence still rides the Cognito-specific backend httpOnly-cookie refresh (`services/auth`) — extending silent-refresh to Entra refresh tokens is pending, so an Entra session survives the tab but a reload re-prompts; ROPC is local-accounts-only (swap to MSAL redirect behind the same seam for social/MFA); needs validation against a real Entra tenant. Frontend Azure build env (`VITE_AUTH_PROVIDER=entra` + `VITE_ENTRA_*`) + both caveats documented in `infrastructure/azure/README.md` and `frontend/04_auth_and_session.md`. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `docs/project docs/frontend/04_auth_and_session.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.120 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — enable frontend on PAYG + CORS + auth decoupling | User upgrading the dev subscription Free Trial→Pay-As-You-Go (keeps the $200 credit; lifts the Front Door + RBAC policy blocks). Wired the pieces for the **full app on Azure**: (a) `dev.tfvars` `enable_frontend=true` (Blob + Front Door — the prod design; prereq: upgrade before apply). (b) **CORS** — `cors_allow_origins` var on the container_app module → `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` env, sourced in the root from the Front Door URL (`module.frontend[0].frontend_url`) so the SPA origin can call the API from the browser; the http_app already honours it. (c) **CSP** — added `https://*.azurecontainerapps.io` to the SPA connect-src so it can reach the Container Apps API. (d) **`ENTRA_USER_DOMAIN`** passthrough env for the identity provider. (e) **`AUTH_PROVIDER` decoupling** (`shared/providers/__init__.py`) — `get_identity_provider()`/`get_claims_mapper()` now select on `AUTH_PROVIDER` (default `CLOUD_PROVIDER`), the ADR-004 dual-run seam: run compute+data on Azure while auth stays on Cognito (`AUTH_PROVIDER=aws` + `OIDC_*`→Cognito pool), so the Azure API validates Cognito JWTs with the Cognito mapper — **the fastest path to a working full-app login with no frontend change and no Entra tenant**. Documented both auth paths (A: Cognito bridge; B: native Entra — needs net-new frontend MSAL work since the SPA is Cognito-only) in `infrastructure/azure/README.md`. **Verified:** 1251 unit tests (+1 decoupling test), ruff + mypy clean; `terraform fmt -check` + `validate` pass. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md` ("Running the full app on Azure" + auth paths), `02_shared_utilities.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.119 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — enforce Terraform-before-deploy ordering | Per the invariant "Terraform must always run and succeed before the app deploys, so infra/state changes are captured every time." Restructured so `_deploy-azure-env.yml` runs **`terraform apply` as the first step of the deploy job** (`init` + `apply -var-file=environments/<env>.tfvars -auto-approve`, then emits the infra outputs the later steps consume). If the apply fails the job stops there — build/migrate/roll/smoke all depend on its outputs, so the app can never ship on un-applied or failed infra; apply is a no-op when nothing changed, so it's safe every run. To keep a **single Terraform state-owner** (no lock contention), `terraform-azure.yml` is now **plan-only** (fmt + validate + `plan -lock=false` preview; the 4 `terraform apply` steps removed), repositioned as the infra *review* check. Net: infra reconciles → migrations → app roll, in one ordered job; a first-ever environment stands up from the deploy pipeline alone (no separate "apply infra first"). Replaces the earlier preflight guard (the race it detected can no longer happen). **Verified:** both workflows YAML-parse; `terraform-azure.yml` has 0 apply commands, the deploy job applies first. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md` (plan-only note + terraform-first deploy steps), `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.118 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — live-apply hardening (restricted-subscription support) | Fixes from the first real `terraform apply` against a Free Trial/Student subscription, which surfaced provider plan/apply-time constraints that `terraform validate` can't catch. **(a) Service Bus namespace** can't end with the reserved `-sb`/`-mgmt` suffix → renamed `${name_prefix}-sb` → `-bus` (committed earlier as the #24 hotfix). **(b) Resource-provider registration** — documented the one-time `az provider register` loop (Microsoft.App/ContainerRegistry/DBforPostgreSQL/Cache/ServiceBus/EventGrid/Communication/etc.) in the azure README; the apply fails `... not registered for the Microsoft.X resource provider` otherwise. **(c) Capability toggles** for services restricted on Free Trial/Student subs: `enable_frontend` (Front Door forbidden → skip the frontend module), `enable_redis` (classic Redis retiring/blocked → skip; the cache degrades to DB lookups via the existing miss-path fallback), `grant_acr_pull` (Contributor-only deploy identity can't write role assignments → pull from ACR with admin creds instead of a managed-identity AcrPull role). Implemented via module `count` + `one()` outputs + a conditional registry block (identity vs admin creds) in `modules/container_app`; defaults stay `true` (pay-as-you-go), and `dev.tfvars` sets all three `false` for the Free Trial dev account. **(d) Deploy preflight guard** — `_deploy-azure-env.yml` now reads `terraform output -json` and fails fast with a clear "infra not applied yet — run Terraform (Azure) first" message instead of the cryptic `Output "api_url" not found` that the CI-vs-apply race produces. **Verified:** `terraform fmt -check` + `validate` pass; workflow YAML parses. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md` (subscription prerequisites + toggles table + UAA grant), `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.117 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — Phase 6: cost model reconciliation + wrap-up | Final phase. Reconciled `docs/azure_cost_model.md` with the as-built architecture (doc-first — no contradictions): backend compute is **Container Apps** (ADR-005, not "Functions or Container Apps"); private networking is **VNet integration + private DNS** for Postgres (not service endpoints); **Service Bus Standard** carries a ~$10/mo base (queues row 10–15, not 0–5); the portability note now states AWS stays on Lambda while Azure runs the container (ADR-005), selected by `DEPLOY_CLOUD`/`CLOUD_PROVIDER`. Baseline still ≈ $60–175/mo (well within Founders Hub credits). **End-to-end summary — the full application is now deployable to Azure with a single switch:** Phase 1 containerized the backend (one image: api/worker/job/migrate) with a shared auth-verify + routing edge; Phase 2 built the data/messaging Terraform (networking, Postgres, Redis, Service Bus, Event Grid, ACS, monitoring); Phase 3 the Container Apps compute (ACR + env + api + KEDA workers + cron jobs) with the db/redis TLS runtime fixes; Phase 4 the Entra identity adapter + claims mapper + user-migration script; Phase 5 the `deploy-azure.yml` image-build/roll pipeline. AWS (Lambda/Cognito/Aurora) is byte-for-byte untouched throughout. **Remaining is user/settings-side only** (cannot be automated here): set the `DEPLOY_CLOUD` repo variable, wire the Azure OIDC app + `AZURE_*` env secrets, one-time tfstate bootstrap, and the Entra External ID tenant setup + run the user migration — all documented as `az`/`gh` checklists in `infrastructure/azure/README.md` + `18_cicd_pipeline.md`. Docs: `docs/azure_cost_model.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.116 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — Phase 5: Azure backend deploy workflow (`deploy-azure.yml`) | The app-code deploy pipeline for Azure — the analogue of `deploy.yml` (Lambda), gated `vars.DEPLOY_CLOUD == 'azure'`, closing the loop so flipping the switch actually ships the backend to Azure. **`.github/workflows/deploy-azure.yml`** mirrors `deploy.yml`'s promotion (push to main → dev, `v*-rc*` tag → staging, `v*` tag → prod, `workflow_dispatch` → demo); no artifact-resolution job (the image is built here from the CI-passed commit). **`.github/workflows/_deploy-azure-env.yml`** (reusable, per env): `azure/login` (OIDC) + `terraform init` to **read infra outputs from TF state** (RG, ACR name/login-server, migrate-job name, api URL — single source of names, no duplication); `az acr login` + `docker build/push` tagged with the commit SHA; **migrate** (point the migration Container App Job at the new image, `az containerapp job start`, poll to Succeeded — Failed aborts); **roll** every Container App + Job onto the new image via `az containerapp update/job update --image` (Terraform ignores image drift, so CI owns the running tag); **smoke** the live ingress (health 200 + unauthenticated `/v1/*` → 401, read-only/prod-safe). Prereq: infra already applied by `terraform-azure.yml` (ACR + apps exist); the pre-CI first bring-up is the manual sequence in the azure README. **Verified:** all workflows YAML-parse; gating mirrors the proven `deploy.yml` structure (4 env jobs, each `== 'azure'`); `apply`/live deploy need Azure creds + the image (user-run). AWS pipeline untouched. Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md` (DEPLOY_CLOUD table now lists `deploy-azure.yml` + a dedicated "Azure backend deploy" section), `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. **With this the full application is deployable to Azure end-to-end** (Phases 1–5): container → data/messaging infra → Container Apps → Entra auth → CI deploy; remaining is Phase 6 (cost model doc) + the user's settings-side setup (Azure OIDC/secrets, Entra tenant, `DEPLOY_CLOUD` variable). |
| 7.117 ✅ | Entra custom claims provider — all roles resolve on CIAM | Closes the tenant-user gap from 7.116: Entra External ID (CIAM) does not emit app-manifest optional claims for directory extensions, and a tenant user's `tenant_id` is not derivable (only the platform admin's is), so customer/staff/tenant_admin tokens lacked the `tenant_id`/`role` claims the backend needs. Added a **custom authentication extension** (OnTokenIssuanceStart claims provider) that injects them at sign-in. **Code** — `services/entra_token_claims/`: `claims.py` (pure event→response envelope, Graph lookup injected), `security.py` (inbound-caller `aud`/`iss` check, fails closed), `db_lookup.py` (reads `tenant_id`/`role` from `tenant_user` by the Entra `oid` → the globally-unique `cognito_sub`; no Graph), `app.py` (Flask WSGI on the shared image; verifies Entra's inbound bearer against the tenant JWKS then returns the claims). **Provisioning** — `migrate_users_to_entra.py` stamps each provisioned user's Entra `oid` into `tenant_user.cognito_sub` (the key the claims provider resolves by); no directory extension attributes. **Terraform** — its **own Container App** (`*-entra-claims`) in `modules/container_app` on the shared backend image via the `claims` entrypoint (external ingress, always-on `claims_min_replicas`=1), wired from the azure root with `entra_claims_*` tfvars and `entra_claims_{app_name,endpoint_url,principal_id}` outputs. The **API** container also gets `ENTRA_PLATFORM_ADMIN_GROUP_ID` (+ optional `ENTRA_GROUP_ID_MAP`) via the `entra_platform_admin_group_id` tfvar — without it the deployed mapper can't map the group GUID → `platform_admin`, so the platform admin would resolve as a plain customer. **Why not an Azure Function:** App Service VM quota is **0** on this subscription — both Y1 (Consumption) and B1 (Basic) failed `terraform apply` with `401 … Current Limit … 0` — so the original Function-on-App-Service-plan design was replaced by a Container App, which has quota and keeps the one-image model (ADR-005). Always-on because Entra's extension call times out ~2s. **Manual (documented, not Terraformable):** only the `customAuthenticationExtensions`+event binding (no azurerm/azuread resource) — **no Graph `User.Read.All` grant**, since the provider reads the DB, not the directory. **Verified:** full unit suite 1266 passed / 4 skipped (+19 claims-provider cases: pure logic, DB reader, and the Flask app); ruff + mypy clean (`mypy shared/ services/`, strict); `terraform fmt -check -recursive` clean (validate runs in CI — provider download is egress-blocked in the sandbox). **CI deploy:** no dedicated step — the claims Container App shares the backend image, so `_deploy-azure-env.yml`'s existing `az containerapp update --image` loop rolls it alongside the api/workers. Docs: `infrastructure/azure/README.md` ("Token claims on CIAM" runbook) + `SETUP.md`, `docs/adr/004-...md`, `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `18_cicd_pipeline.md`, `docs/runbook.md` ("Azure deployment — required one-time setup"), `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. Remaining manual (one-time, not CI, **required for deploy** — in the runbook): just the `customAuthenticationExtensions`/event binding (the Graph `User.Read.All` grant is no longer needed — DB lookup). |
| 7.116 ✅ | Entra↔Cognito auth compatibility + platform-admin bring-up script | Closed the Cognito↔Entra gap found during the first live platform-admin bring-up: the backend assumed the `groups` claim carries `{tenant_id}:{role}` **display names** (as Cognito does), but Entra emits **object ids (GUIDs)** for cloud-only groups and display-name emission isn't available for them — so a real Entra token resolved **no** roles. **`shared/providers/azure/entra_claims.py`** `EntraClaimsMapper.groups()` now accepts three shapes: (1) canonical names pass through; (2) object ids mapped via `ENTRA_PLATFORM_ADMIN_GROUP_ID` / `ENTRA_GROUP_ID_MAP` (no Graph call on the auth path); (3) the canonical group **synthesized** from the `tenant_id`+`role` optional/extension claims (`ENTRA_TENANT_ID_CLAIM`/`ENTRA_ROLE_CLAIM`) — the primary, claims-mapping-policy-free path. **CIAM finding (live):** Entra External ID (`*.ciamlogin.com`) does not emit app-manifest directory-extension optional claims (only the `groups` GUID arrives), so `EntraClaimsMapper.tenant_id()` **derives** `PLATFORM` from `platform_admin` group membership when no tenant claim is present (a platform admin is tenant-less by invariant) — a real CIAM id_token carrying only the group GUID resolves with just `ENTRA_PLATFORM_ADMIN_GROUP_ID` set; tenant-scoped users still need an explicit tenant claim. `shared/auth.py` stays cloud-agnostic; Cognito's `CognitoClaimsMapper` is untouched. **`scripts/setup_entra_platform_admin.sh`** (new, idempotent): automates the manual bring-up — resolves the verified domain, creates/updates the SPA app as a public (ROPC) client, admin-consents the OIDC scopes, enables the security-group claim, ensures the prefixless `platform_admin` group, creates the admin user (permanent password + contact email + group membership), and prints the exact backend/frontend env. (Originally also registered `tenant_id`/`role` directory extensions + stamped them; those were dropped in 7.117 when the claims provider moved to a DB lookup.) **Verified:** full unit suite 1247 passed / 4 skipped (+23 Entra-claims cases: unmapped GUIDs dropped, synthesis from role/tenant claims, group-id map incl. malformed-JSON ignore, overridable claim names, CIAM `PLATFORM` tenant-id derivation, dual-cloud role resolution) — ruff + mypy clean on changed files; script `bash -n` clean. Live tenant steps run via the script (no Azure creds in CI). Docs: `docs/adr/004-...md` (2026-10-11 addendum), `infrastructure/azure/README.md` + `SETUP.md` (runbook), `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.115 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — Phase 4: Entra auth (adapters + user migration) | Executed ADR-004 Phases 3–4 — the last provider port. **`shared/providers/azure/entra_claims.py`** (`EntraClaimsMapper`): reads `oid`/`sub`, a configurable tenant claim (`ENTRA_TENANT_ID_CLAIM`), the `groups` claim normalized to `{tenant_id}:{role}` (list/JSON/CSV), and a configurable role claim — the downstream interpretation in `shared/auth.py` is unchanged. **`shared/providers/azure/entra.py`** (`EntraIdentityProvider`): the 14 `IdentityProvider` methods over **Microsoft Graph v1.0**, auth'd by an `azure-identity` token and called over **stdlib urllib** (no new runtime dep). Roles are security groups `{tenant_id}:{role}` so `sync_user_groups`'s reconcile is identical to Cognito's; users are created with a `passwordProfile`+`forceChangePasswordNextSignIn` (the invite/reset analogue); 409→`DuplicateResourceError`, else `ExternalServiceError("Entra", …)`. The factory in `shared/providers/__init__.py` already pointed at these paths — they just existed as references until now. The authorizer's verifier was already issuer-driven (`OIDC_JWKS_URL`/`OIDC_AUDIENCE`, Phase 1's `shared/auth_verify.py`), so pointing it at Entra is config. **`scripts/migrate_users_to_entra.py`**: bulk-provisions `tenant_user` rows into Entra via the port (forced reset + full-role group sync, platform admins → `platform_admin` group), idempotent, `--dry-run`/`--tenant`, guarded to `CLOUD_PROVIDER=azure`. Decision recorded (ADR-004): **Entra External ID** + security-group roles. **Verified:** 1250 unit tests (+14 new Entra tests, fully mocked — stubbed Graph/token/urlopen), ruff + mypy clean. Real tenant setup is manual (can't Terraform an External ID tenant) — documented as a checklist in `infrastructure/azure/README.md` ("Auth setup — Entra External ID": app registration, Graph perms, group-name token config, `OIDC_*`/`ENTRA_*` env, migration run, config-flip cutover). AWS/Cognito untouched. Docs: `docs/adr/004-...md` (Phases 3–4 done + decision), `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. Next: Phase 5 (`deploy-azure.yml` — build/push image to ACR + update apps/run migrate, gated `DEPLOY_CLOUD==azure`). |

The root creates one **user-assigned managed identity** (granted AcrPull + Key Vault wrap/unwrap/secret-read via `keyvault.extra_object_ids`) shared by all apps; `CLOUD_PROVIDER=azure` + every Phase-2 output injected as env (connection strings as Container App secrets). | 7.113 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — Phase 2: data + messaging Terraform | Seven new modules under `infrastructure/azure/modules/` mirroring the AWS data/messaging stack, wired into the azure root with outputs mapped 1:1 to the adapter env vars. **`networking`** (VNet 10.1.0.0/16 + three subnets: container-apps + postgres *delegated*, private-endpoints) ← AWS `networking`. **`postgres`** — PostgreSQL Flexible Server, **VNet-integrated/private** (delegated subnet + private DNS zone + link), DB `logistics` + admin user (matches AWS so migrations/seed run unchanged), admin password generated + stored in Key Vault (`postgres-admin-password`, the Secrets-Manager analogue), `require_secure_transport=ON` ← AWS `aurora`. **`redis`** — Azure Cache for Redis (Basic C0 default, TLS-only) ← `elasticache`. **`servicebus`** — namespace + queues sms/whatsapp/email/metering with built-in dead-lettering (`max_delivery_count` = SQS `max_receive_count` 3/3/3/5, P4D TTL) ← `sqs`. **`eventgrid`** — custom topic ← `eventbridge`. **`acs`** — Communication Services + Email with an Azure-managed sending domain ← `ses`. **`monitoring`** — Log Analytics workspace + Application Insights (Container Apps log sink + APM) ← `monitoring`. Root `outputs.tf` exposes every adapter env value (`DATABASE_*`, `REDIS_*`, `AZURE_SERVICE_BUS_CONNECTION_STRING` + `SQS_*_QUEUE_URL` names, `AZURE_EVENT_GRID_ENDPOINT/KEY`, `AZURE_COMMUNICATION_CONNECTION_STRING` + sender, `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING`, workspace id); sensitive ones marked. Prod sizing set in `prod.tfvars` (GP Postgres + Standard Redis); dev/staging/demo use the cheap Burstable/Basic module defaults. Added `vault_id` output to the keyvault module (postgres stores its secret there). **Two runtime portability gaps flagged for Phase 3 (env-wiring, not provisioning):** `shared/db.py` keys `sslmode` on the AWS-only `DB_SECRET_ARN` → add generic `DATABASE_SSLMODE`; `shared/distributed_cache.py` reads only `REDIS_HOST/PORT` → add `REDIS_PASSWORD`/`REDIS_SSL` (both defaults keep AWS unchanged). **Verified:** `terraform fmt -check -recursive` clean + `terraform validate` passes against azurerm 4.81 (schema-checked); `plan`/`apply` need Azure creds (user-run via the README checklist). | 7.112 ✅ | Full-Azure deployability — Phase 1: containerize the backend (ADR-005) | First phase of making the whole app deployable to Azure with `DEPLOY_CLOUD` switching clouds. Decision (ADR-005): **keep AWS on Lambda unchanged; run the same code on Azure Container Apps as a container** — shared core, thin per-cloud edge. **(1) AWS-safe refactor:** extracted the Lambda authorizer's token verification into **`shared/auth_verify.py`** (`build_authorizer_claims` — OIDC RS256/JWKS then ShipX impersonation → the flat Cognito-shaped authorizer context), so the **same** logic backs the AWS authorizer *and* the container's in-process auth middleware; `services/authorizer/handler.py` is now a thin adapter over it (behavior byte-identical — 65 auth tests green). JWKS/audience read from `OIDC_*` with `COGNITO_*` fallback (AWS unchanged; Entra points `OIDC_*` at its JWKS in ADR-004 Phase 4). **(2) Routing DRY:** extracted the HTTP route table + public-route set from `scripts/local_server.py` into **`shared/http_routing.py`** (`resolve_service` + `is_public_route` + `invoke_handler`) — one source for the dev server, the container, and (mirrored) API Gateway; `local_server.py` now imports it. **(3) Container workloads:** **`services/http_app/app.py`** (production gunicorn WSGI app — real token verify, public/authenticated gating, API-GW-v2 event build, dispatch), **`services/queue_worker/worker.py`** (Service Bus consumer, one per queue, wraps messages into SQS-shaped events → the same `notification`/`metering` handlers), **`services/scheduled/run.py`** (Container App Jobs cron runner for the 7 jobs). **(4) `Dockerfile`** (multi-stage, py3.13, `uv sync --extra azure --extra container --group layer`) + `docker-entrypoint.sh` (api/worker/job/migrate) + `.dockerignore`; new `container` extra in `pyproject.toml` (flask/flask-cors/gunicorn==23.0.0; `uv.lock` updated). gunicorn runs processes×1-thread (db-singleton not thread-safe). **Verified:** 1230 unit tests pass (+48 new, fully mocked), ruff + mypy clean (139 files); **`docker build` succeeds and the api container serves `/health` 200 + gates `/v1/*` to 401 without a token; worker/job modules import in-container.** AWS Lambda path untouched. **Remaining phases:** 2 (Azure data/messaging Terraform), 3 (Container Apps + ACR Terraform), 4 (Entra auth + user migration), 5 (`deploy-azure.yml`), 6 (cost model + docs). Docs: `docs/adr/005-compute-portability-lambda-and-container-apps.md`, `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |
| 7.111 ✅ | Single `DEPLOY_CLOUD` switch — one cloud deploys, never both | Per the user's request ("a single DEPLOY_CLOUD, that will either be azure or aws"), gated every deploy/apply job on the `vars.DEPLOY_CLOUD` repository variable so exactly one cloud's pipelines run. **AWS workflows** (`terraform.yml` ×4 jobs, `deploy.yml` ×9 jobs, `frontend-ci.yml` deploy) gated `vars.DEPLOY_CLOUD != 'azure'` — **fail-safe default**: unset/misspelled still deploys AWS (the live cloud), not nothing. **Azure** (`terraform-azure.yml` ×4 jobs) gated `vars.DEPLOY_CLOUD == 'azure'` (opt-in). Multiline `if:` OR-blocks wrapped in parens with the gate `&&`-appended; `always()` smoke/apply jobs got the gate inline. Gate is on **deploy/apply only** — CI (lint/test/build), frontend build+test, and all Terraform `plan`/`validate` still run every push regardless. Switching to `azure` still requires the `AZURE_*` secrets/OIDC wired first (it fails `azure/login`, it does not fall back to AWS). Set once: `gh variable set DEPLOY_CLOUD --body azure|aws`. **Verified:** all 8 workflows YAML-parse; gate counts confirmed (terraform 4 / terraform-azure 4 / deploy 9 / frontend 1). Docs: `18_cicd_pipeline.md` (new "Choosing the cloud — the `DEPLOY_CLOUD` switch" section + value table), `00_MASTER_PLAN.md`. |

| 7.106 ✅ | Azure Key Vault adapters — KeyProvider (crypto) + SecretsProvider | Third Azure increment, covering two ports. `shared/providers/azure/keyvault.py` (`KeyVaultKeyProvider`): Key Vault has no KMS-style `generate_data_key`, so the envelope is the standard Azure pattern — generate a 32-byte AES-256 key locally, **wrap** it with a Key Vault key (RSA-OAEP-256) for the stored `encrypted_key`, **unwrap** on decrypt; the Fernet layer in `shared/encryption.py` is identical to AWS, only the wrapping differs, so the envelope format is unchanged. `shared/providers/azure/keyvault_secrets.py` (`KeyVaultSecretsProvider`): returns the raw secret value (parity with Secrets Manager). Both auth via `DefaultAzureCredential`; `ENCRYPTION_KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` / `AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL` env. **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_azure_keyvault_provider.py` (8) via stubbed Key Vault clients (no official emulator) — key gen/round-trip/32-byte guard, a **real `encrypt_field`/`decrypt_field` round-trip** through the Fernet envelope with only the wrap stubbed, secret retrieval, and both factory selections. `azure-keyvault-keys==4.11.3` + `azure-keyvault-secrets==4.11.3` pinned. **Verified:** full backend suite, ruff + mypy clean (130 files). Docs: `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`. |

| 7.101 ✅ | Cognito→Entra portability — migration plan (ADR-004) | The last touchpoint (Cognito) is the only one that isn't a clean adapter swap, so per the user's call it was scoped as a plan rather than swept. Wrote **ADR-004** (`docs/adr/004-cognito-to-entra-portability.md`): splits the surface into (A) **management ops** — `cognito_client.py`'s 14 methods + token refresh + the GDPR user-purge — which *are* a clean `IdentityProvider` port (method-by-method Cognito→MS-Graph mapping table included), and (B) the **authN boundary** — the authorizer's JWKS/RS256 verify (ports via env) + the `custom:tenant_id`/`cognito:groups`/`custom:role` claim shape in `shared/auth.py` (needs a cloud-keyed claim-mapping layer) — which does **not** port cleanly. Documents the **user migration** (Cognito can't export password hashes → bulk-provision in Entra with forced reset + invite, dual-run authorizer accepting both JWTs during cutover, config-flip rollback), a 5-phase rollout (phase 1 = the mgmt port, safe to do anytime; phases 2–5 = claim adapter → Entra adapter/Terraform → migration → cutover), and open business decisions (Entra External ID vs B2C; MAU cost vs Cognito). | 7.100 ✅ | Backend cloud-portability — X-Ray tracing shim | Replaced the direct `from aws_xray_sdk.core import patch_all; patch_all()` that ran at module load in all **19 Lambda entry points** (every `services/*/handler.py` + the 7 `services/scheduled/*` jobs) with `shared/observability.py::init_tracing()`. On AWS it calls `patch_all()` (unchanged X-Ray instrumentation of boto3/psycopg2/requests); on any other cloud it's a no-op (tracing wired at the platform level, e.g. Azure App Insights), selected by `CLOUD_PROVIDER`. Removes the hard `aws_xray_sdk` import from the handlers' startup path off AWS. **Verified:** 1150 backend tests, ruff clean, mypy clean (hook config). No test changes — `conftest.py` already stubs `aws_xray_sdk`, so the lazy import inside `init_tracing` resolves to the stub exactly as before. With this, **7 of 8 boto3 touchpoints are behind ports/shims**; only Cognito→Entra remains (scoped separately — token claims + user migration, not a drop-in adapter). Docs: `02_shared_utilities.md`, `17_testing.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `docs/runbook.md`. |
| 7.99 ✅ | Backend cloud-portability — message queue (SQS) + transactional email (SES) ports | Continued the ports sweep (7.97/7.98) with the messaging layer. Added **`QueueProvider`** (`send(destination, body)`; `destination` is an opaque queue ref — SQS URL on AWS, Service Bus queue name on Azure; adapter `aws/sqs.py::SqsQueueProvider`) and **`EmailProvider`** (`send(sender, to_email, subject, html_body, text_body)`, raises on hard failure; adapter `aws/ses.py::SesEmailProvider`) to `base.py`, plus `get_queue_provider()` / `get_email_provider()` cached-singleton factories. **Migrated off boto3:** `shared/notifications.py` (both SQS `send_message` sites — `enqueue_notification` + event fan-out), `services/notification/service.py` (SES `send_email`, the SMS-fallback enqueue, and the retry re-enqueue that had inlined its own `boto3.client("sqs")`). Removed the module-level `_get_sqs`/`_get_ses` helpers + their clients; the per-channel `SQS_*_QUEUE_URL` env-var mapping (the caller's config) stays. SQS **consumer** side is unchanged — it's the Lambda event-source trigger (notification + metering handlers); on Azure that's a Functions Service Bus binding handled by the entry adapter, not a boto3 call. **Tests:** the notification suites moved from patching `_get_sqs().send_message(QueueUrl, MessageBody)` to `get_queue_provider().send(destination, body)` — `test_whatsapp_first.py` (SMS-fallback cases patch the *service*-module provider; enqueue cases patch the *shared.notifications* provider), `test_notification_guard.py`, `test_scheduled_jobs.py`; new `test_queue_email_providers.py` (4 — SQS via moto, SES structure via stub, factory/caching). **Verified:** 1150 backend tests, ruff clean, mypy no new real errors. Azure adapters (`azure/servicebus.py`, `azure/acs_email.py`) referenced behind `# type: ignore` until the Azure stack. | 7.97 ✅ | Backend cloud-portability — ports & adapters for crypto (KMS) + object storage (S3) | First code step toward an AWS↔Azure-portable backend (replica roadmap item #2). Introduced a **ports-and-adapters layer** (`shared/providers/`): `base.py` holds `KeyProvider` + `StorageProvider` `Protocol`s; `aws/kms.py` (`KmsKeyProvider`) and `aws/s3.py` (`S3StorageProvider`) are the AWS adapters; `__init__.py` has `get_key_provider()` / `get_storage_provider(bucket=None)` factories selecting on **`CLOUD_PROVIDER`** (`aws` default \| `azure`). **Crypto:** `shared/encryption.py` refactored to `get_key_provider()` — byte-identical envelope (AES-256 data key + Fernet), so existing ciphertext still decrypts; `None` preserves the dev plaintext-fallback. **Storage:** all six boto3-S3 call sites (`manifest`, `excel`, `claims`, `payment_release`, `tracking`, `scheduled/data_cleanup`) migrated off direct `boto3.client("s3")` onto `StorageProvider` (`presigned_get_url`, `presigned_upload`, `put_object`, `get_object`, `object_size`, `delete_prefix` — the last encapsulating the version-aware GDPR purge). **Wire-format change (user-confirmed):** every presigned-upload endpoint now returns a **cloud-neutral PUT ticket** `{ upload_url, upload_method:"PUT", upload_headers }` instead of the S3 presigned-POST `{ upload_url, fields }` — the one upload shape both S3 (presigned PUT) and Azure Blob (SAS PUT) satisfy. Frontend `useFileUpload.ts` / `uploads.ts` / `tracking.ts` switched from multipart `FormData` POST to a raw-body PUT with signed headers. **Trade-off recorded:** the S3 POST `content-length-range` edge size-cap has no PUT equivalent → size now enforced client-side, with `StorageProvider.object_size` available for a future server-side confirm. Azure adapters (`azure/keyvault.py`, `azure/blob.py`) land with the Azure stack (factory references them behind `# type: ignore[import-not-found]`). **Verified:** 1141 backend tests (+9 new `test_storage_provider.py` against moto + `test_encryption.py`), 848 frontend tests, tsc clean, ruff clean, mypy no new real errors (only the pre-existing third-party stub baseline). Docs: `02_shared_utilities.md`, `09_api_manifest.md` (+ cross-ref from `10`/`12`/`13`/`19`), `frontend/10_file_uploads_and_exports.md`, `17_testing.md`, `infrastructure/azure/README.md`, `docs/runbook.md`. |

New `modules/frontend` = **Blob storage `$web` static site behind Front Door Standard** — the Azure analogue of the AWS S3+CloudFront module: HTTPS on the free `*.azurefd.net` managed cert, SPA 404→index fallback, gzip, `IgnoreQueryString` caching, and a security-headers rule set (X-Content-Type-Options / X-Frame-Options / Referrer-Policy / HSTS / CSP) mirroring the CloudFront response-headers policy. Front Door **Standard** (not Premium) keeps cost ~$35/mo and has no WAF — layered later with Cloudflare (cloud-neutral, ~$25) or Front Door Premium. Same `frontend/dist/` artifact deploys to either cloud. Scaffolding: root `providers/versions/locals/variables/main/outputs` (azurerm ~>4.0 + random), resource group, CSP built in locals (connect-src → API + Entra endpoints), and a `README.md` with the `az` deploy+purge commands + a status matrix of the remaining pieces (compute/DB/cache/auth/queues ⏳). `terraform init` + `fmt` + `validate` all pass. Cost model: `docs/azure_cost_model.md`. (AWS stack untouched; backend/DB/auth Azure modules are the next increments, ideally via containerizing the backend so one image runs on both.) | 7.93 ✅ | Frontend go-live runbook + WAF/DDoS protection on CloudFront | User asked how the frontend will be deployed cost-effectively / scalably / resiliently / HA, then to harden it against DDoS. **Verified** the frontend deploy is already architected as the canonical static-SPA pattern — private versioned S3 + CloudFront (OAC, HTTP/2+3, CSP/security-headers, SPA 403/404→index fallback, immutable-asset vs no-cache-index cache policies, `PriceClass_100`), **ACM in us-east-1** via the `aws.us_east_1` provider alias with DNS validation, Route53 apex+wildcard, and an OIDC CI deploy job (immutable sync + index no-cache + `/index.html` invalidation). Module is gated on `route53_zone_id`; not yet `apply`'d. **Wrote** a full `docs/runbook.md` → "Frontend Deployment & Go-Live" section (architecture, why it meets the 4 goals, one-time prerequisites — hosted zone / tfvars / OIDC role perms, `terraform apply` steps, the GitHub secrets table, first+ongoing deploys, rollback). **Added WAFv2** (`modules/cloudfront/main.tf`, scope `CLOUDFRONT`/us-east-1, attached via `web_acl_id`): a per-IP rate-based rule (`var.waf_rate_limit`, default 2000/5min, block) + AWS managed rule groups (IP reputation, common rule set, known-bad-inputs), each with CloudWatch metrics; new `waf_web_acl_arn` output. Documented that CloudFront also gets **Shield Standard** free (L3/L4), and that the **API is an HTTP API (v2) which can't take WAF directly** — protected by Shield Standard + stage throttling (50 req/s, burst 100) + Lambda concurrency + metering; full L7 WAF on the API would need a CloudFront front (noted follow-up). `terraform fmt` + `validate` pass; 26 infra-contract tests pass. Docs: `docs/runbook.md`, `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` (§8.6 edge protection). |

| 7.90 ✅ | Nigeria-ops dashboard overhaul — container pipeline redesign | User: "dashboard of a nigerian office staff needs to be overhauled. Container pipeline (every container by stage — click a card) should be redesigned; current design isn't user friendly and hides a lot of containers because of the design. The old `PipelineKanban` was 8 **horizontally-scrolling** columns, each capped at **6 cards** + a dead "+N more" — so most containers in any busy stage were unreachable. **Redesign** (`DashboardPipeline.tsx` + `.module.css`, new `ContainerPipeline`): a responsive **stage rail** (`auto-fit` grid — wraps, never h-scrolls; 2-col on mobile) with an "All active" lead tile + all 8 stages, each showing the live tenant-wide count (from `containersApi.stats`), a colour dot + label, and a proportional **meter**; selecting a stage drives the **complete, paginated** container table below (`useContainerList` + `ListFooter` load-more) — Container/Route/Status/ETA-Arrival/Packing-lists/View columns — so **no container is hidden** and it scales to tens of thousands. Stage accents reuse the app's status hues (per the dataviz skill: ordered state palette, never colour-alone — every tile labelled). Stat row de-duplicated (per-stage counts now live in the rail): Containers / Ready for pickup / Unpaid billing / Outstanding; `StatCard` labels now wrap instead of truncating on a dense row. Action queues retained (manifest verification, CBM disputes, PL approvals). **Two live follow-ups from the user:** (a) the passive bottom cards "Incoming containers" + "Recent payments" (the first redundant with the rail) were replaced with actionable ones — **Outstanding invoices** (oldest unpaid, chase to collect) + **Awaiting pickup** (active release notes, with an on-credit flag); `.dashGrid2` became `auto-fit` so a lone card spans full width. (b) the pipeline gained a **debounced search box** (`?q=` container/vessel) + an **"Arriving" ETA quick-filter** (≤7d/≤30d/Overdue → `eta_after`/`eta_before`) above the table — stage/search/ETA are independent AND filters. **Verified live** (Playwright, nigeria_office_staff @ 1280px + 390px): rail shows all stages + meters, "At Warehouse" select refetches, search `ACME9057` narrows to one, table collapses to cards on mobile, no horizontal overflow. Tests: new `DashboardPipeline.test.tsx` (6 incl. search/ETA); `Dashboard.test.tsx` updated (outstanding + awaiting-pickup cards). 843 frontend tests pass; tsc/eslint clean; build OK. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.89 ✅ | Carryover apply was 500ing (uuid=text) + billing-detail header enriched | User: "billing details page should also have some vital information too; carry over payment seems not to be working — investigate and resolve." **Root cause (500):** `mark_carryovers_applied()` / `mark_credit_refunds_applied()` compared a `uuid` PK against a bound Python-string array — `id = ANY(%(ids)s)` → `psycopg2 UndefinedFunction: operator does not exist: uuid = text`. Every manual `PATCH /v1/billing/{id}/apply-carryover` 500'd (and the identical fault sat latent on the billing-generation auto-apply path and the CBM-dispute credit-refund path). Fixed with an explicit `::uuid[]` cast. Mocked unit tests can't see it (they stub `execute_write_with_tenant`), so verified live: apply → 200, ledger flips to `applied` with target/applied_at, billing total 139500→195300 / balance 35800→91600. **Status recompute:** applying carried-forward debt raises `total_ngn` (and the generated `balance_ngn`), but `status` was never recomputed — a formerly-`paid` record stayed `paid` while now owing. `apply_carryover` now recomputes the money status from the fresh balance via new `repo.set_billing_status()` (four money statuses only; `released_with_outstanding` untouched). **Picker fix:** the Apply-carryover modal loaded every customer's carryovers but the backend rejects a customer mismatch (BR-CARRYOVER-003), so most picks 422'd — `loadCarryovers()` now scopes to `billing.customer_id`. **Detail header:** the `BillingDetail` hero now leads with the customer name (was the container number) + invoice number + container + the customer's phone/email (reusing the `customer_phone`/`customer_email` fields from 7.88). Tests: backend `test_applying_to_a_paid_billing_recomputes_status_to_partially_paid` (+1, 69 in test_billing); frontend `scopes the carryover picker to the billing's customer` (+1, 13 in BillingDetail). 1124 backend + 837 frontend tests pass; tsc/eslint/ruff clean; build OK. Docs: `11_api_billing.md` (dated note). |

| 7.87 ✅ | Arrival warehouse on packing lists + warehouse admin config page (one surface, de-duplicated) | User: "a shipping company can have multiple warehouses that customer goods can go to… add arrival warehouse option (searchable dropdown) in packing list. And in admin configuration, they can set up multiple warehouse locations, and allow them to set one as default." **Discovery:** a `warehouse` table (migration 041, with `is_default` + one-default-per-tenant partial unique index) and a live `/v1/warehouses` surface (payment_release: list/create/set-default, used by the release-note/pickup flow) already existed — plus a **duplicate, dead** `/v1/tenant/warehouses` CRUD surface in tenant-management that was never wired to the frontend, Terraform, or Postman. So this reused the live table rather than inventing a parallel one. **Arrival warehouse:** migration 053 adds `packing_list.arrival_warehouse_id` (FK → `warehouse`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) on create/update; `service._resolve_arrival_warehouse` validates an explicit id against the tenant (unknown → 404) and otherwise defaults to the tenant's `is_default` warehouse (same fallback release notes use), so a list always records a destination; list/detail/create responses carry `arrival_warehouse_name` (LEFT JOIN).
