| 7.86 ✅ | Robust log tracing — request-scoped context propagation across every Lambda | User: "ensure that we are using a robust logging format and best practices, to ensure that we can trace issues and related things." Audit found the structured-JSON formatter (`shared/logging_config.py`) was sound but correlation was broken in practice: only ~25% of the 267 `logger.*` calls passed `extra={request_id,tenant_id}`, so the other ~75% — **including the two most important lines for tracing, the `logger.warning("Business error…")` and `logger.exception("Unhandled error")` in every handler's error path** — emitted `request_id:null, tenant_id:null`; service/repository logs never carried context at all (the IDs weren't threaded down); `duration_ms` and other `extra=` fields were **silently dropped** by the fixed-schema formatter; and the security-critical **authorizer Lambda called no `setup_logging()` at all** (plaintext, unstructured). **Fix = request-scoped `contextvars` + a `logging.Filter`:** `bind_log_context()`/`clear_log_context()` bind `request_id`/`tenant_id`/`user_id` once per invocation and `ContextFilter` injects them onto *every* record automatically — so a deep `services.billing.service` log with **no `extra=`** now carries all three IDs (proven live). Explicit `extra=` still wins per-line. The formatter now **passes through** any custom `extra` field (duration_ms, event_type…) as a top-level queryable key, and adds `user_id` to the core schema. **Wiring contract:** API handlers `clear`+bind `request_id` at entry (clear at *entry*, not `finally`, so reused Lambda execution environments can't leak the previous request and the closing `←` line keeps context); `get_tenant_context()` binds `tenant_id`+`user_id` from the single auth chokepoint the moment identity is known (covers all 13 handlers in one edit); scheduled jobs bind `request_id` from `context.aws_request_id` + per-tenant `tenant_id` in their loop; SQS consumers (notification sms/whatsapp/email, metering) bind `request_id` from the SQS `messageId` per record + `tenant_id` from the parsed body; authorizer + migrate Lambdas gained `setup_logging()`. **Verified live**: formatter emits the new schema; a no-`extra=` service log carried `request_id`/`tenant_id`/`user_id`; `duration_ms`/`event_type` survive as top-level keys. Tests: new `tests/unit/test_logging_config.py` (9 — schema, extra passthrough, Decimal serialisation, exception capture, bind/refine/clear, explicit-extra precedence, cross-invocation isolation). 1113 backend tests pass (+9); ruff clean; mypy only pre-existing `[import-untyped]` stub noise. Docs: `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` §3 (rewritten with §3.1 context-propagation contract + §3.2 custom-field passthrough + no-PII rule), `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.85 ✅ | Packing-list item entry: input validation, package type, bale sizing, minimum CBM, shipment dates, draft editing | User: numeric fields accepted negatives/alphabets; add package type (carton/bags/pallets/bales/sacks); bale <0.3 CBM = small bale, ≥0.3 = big bale (abbreviations ok); line-item CBM <0.1 rounds up to a tenant-configurable minimum; drafts should be editable; align the design. **Input hardening** is frontend-only (the Pydantic models already enforced `ge=0`/`gt=0`) — number cells block negatives + `e/+/-` and sanitize onChange. **Package type** = per-row select reusing `packing_list_item.package_unit`; feeds CBM-rate bucketing. **Bale sizing** = backend computes `bale_type` (`BIG BALE` ≥0.3 CBM else `SMALL BALE`) into the existing column; wizard shows a live `BIG/SM BALE` badge. **Min CBM** = `shared/cbm_rate.apply_min_cbm`/`get_min_cbm_per_item` floors each line item's stored/billed CBM at the tenant's `min_cbm_per_item` (`tenant_config`, default 0.1), editable on the CBM-rate admin page (GET/PUT `/v1/tenant/cbm-rate` now carry it); wizard shows the floored value + a `min` tag. **Dates** = `loading_date`/`delivery_date` per packing list (migration 052), captured in step 1. **Draft editing** = the create wizard doubles as an editor at `/packing-lists/:id/edit` (reuses the existing draft/rejected-only `PUT`), customer/container fixed, items+dates editable; new Edit button on draft detail. Step-2 table realigned with a Type column + compact CBM cell. **Verified live**: 0.06 bale → `0.100 · min · SM BALE`, 0.4 bale → `BIG BALE`, dates persisted, negatives 422, draft opens prefilled. Tests: `TestMinCbmAndBaleSizing` (3) + live e2e, `CreatePackingList.test.tsx` (+3 incl. draft edit), `TestGetCbmRate`/`TestUpdateCbmRate` + `CbmRateConfig.test.tsx` updated for `min_cbm_per_item`. 1104 backend + 828 frontend tests pass; tsc/eslint/ruff/mypy clean; terraform valid. | 7.83 ✅ | Light / dark theme | User: "implement Theme for this, dark mode and day light." Full app-wide theming driven by the `tokens.css` CSS custom properties. The active scheme resolves to an explicit `data-theme="light\|dark"` on `<html>` in JS (`src/design/colorScheme.tsx`), with a pre-paint script in `index.html` to avoid a light flash; 'system' (default) follows `prefers-color-scheme` and re-resolves live. New `ThemeToggle` (System → Light → Dark) in both the staff `TopBar` and customer `CustomerTopNav`; preference persisted to localStorage. A single `:root[data-theme="dark"]` block flips surfaces/ink/lines/shadows and the soft/tint fills, while brand/status/role hues stay recognisable (soft backgrounds darken, strong badge-text variants lighten). Two new tokens made the surface-flip safe: `--color-on-brand` (always white, for on-colored text — replaced all 23 `color: var(--color-surface)` usages) and `--color-primary-on-soft` (dark→bright green, for stat-card icon chips + consent pills so they aren't green-on-green). Fixed hardcoded light colors dark mode exposed (`TopBar` hover, `MyShipments` card gradient). **Verified live in dark mode**: staff dashboard/billing/customers, customer portal, and login screen all legible. **Follow-up fix (reported live):** a hovered/selected table row showed a blinding near-white band in dark mode — the tenant brand's derived *light* `soft`/`tint` shades were applied inline on `[data-tenant-theme]`, overriding the dark tokens. Made `deriveBrandShades(hex, scheme)` scheme-aware (dark soft/tint for dark mode) and had `ThemeProvider` re-derive on scheme change; verified the dark hover is now a subtle dark tint. **Dark-mode contrast audit (reported live):** a WCAG pass fixed the hard-to-read dark combinations, all dark-only (new tokens' light values equal the old). Biggest: `<Mono>` defaulted to the static `colors.ink` (#1c1b1c) from `theme.ts`, rendering container numbers/totals/CBM near-invisible dark-on-dark across every table — now defaults to `var(--color-ink)`. Also lightened the accent badge/monogram, accent eyebrow labels, and platform/purple badge (`--color-accent-on-soft` / `--color-accent-text` / `--color-role-platform-text`). Tests: `colorScheme.test.tsx` (5), `ThemeToggle.test.tsx` (1), `deriveBrandShades.test.tsx` (+1 dark-branch), `matchMedia` mock added to the test setup. 813 frontend unit tests pass; tsc + eslint clean; `npm run build` succeeds. | 7.82 ✅ | Production-readiness pass: header consistency, bounded load-more lists, and server-side stat cards that stay correct at scale | User: "look into the layouts and structuring of all the pages, refine all to be production ready… consistent, production-ready responsive", with the scale constraint "data and figures can grow to 10's of thousands and the design should accommodate it" (incl. "listing things, scrolling"), scoped via AskUserQuestion to "Both — heroes + backend stats" and "Bounded load-more + strong filters". Three structural changes applied app-wide. **(1) Header consistency:** list-page titles moved into the shared `TopBar` slot (new `subtitle` plumbing through `TopBarContext`/`StaffLayout`/`TopBar`); 14 list pages migrated, 7 detail heroes unified onto one `.hero`/`.heroTitle`/`.heroActions` spec. **(2) Bounded load-more:** new shared `ListFooter` ("Showing X of N {noun}" + "Load more (N remaining)", capped at 500 rows with a refine-search nudge) adopted across the migrated list pages — tables already page (DOM bounded); this makes accumulation explicit and capped. **(3) Server-side stat cards:** stat figures no longer aggregate a capped client sample — they come from SQL `COUNT(*) FILTER (…)` endpoints. Built/verified `GET /v1/billing/stats` + `/report`, `/v1/claims/stats`, `/v1/containers/stats`, and `GET /v1/customers/stats` (`CustomerStats` = total/active/anonymised/with_whatsapp/shipments, both queries RLS-scoped via `execute_with_tenant`), each with repo→service→handler layers, a Terraform route, a Postman request, and backend tests. Pages rewired to the endpoints: `BillingList`, `ClaimList`, `ContainerList`, `TenantReports`, `AdminCustomerList`, and **`Dashboard`** — whose `customerCount` had a real bug (read `meta.pagination.total_count ?? data.length`, but list endpoints return no `total_count`, so it silently showed the loaded-row count ≈1); now `customerStats.active`. Dashboard's container count, greeting summary line, pipeline-Kanban per-stage badges (cards stay a bounded visual sample; badge + "+N more" use the true count), unpaid/outstanding, and open-claims all read stats endpoints; "Incoming containers" became a dedicated server query (`status=sealed,in_transit`, `sort=estimated_arrival_date asc`, `limit=5`) instead of filtering a capped list. **Audited clean, no change needed:** ActivityLog, StaffUserList, Configuration pages. Gates: backend `TestCustomerStats` added (service delegation + two-query repo mapping + empty-tenant); `GET /v1/customers/stats` verified live vs direct SQL (70 total / 372 shipments); 1145 backend + 799 frontend unit tests pass, mypy/ruff/eslint/tsc clean, terraform validate OK. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.81 ✅ | Full-project debugging pass — SSRF in tenant webhooks, ~80 routes missing from API Gateway, notification Lambdas that could never run, a broken production build, and rotted tests | User: "debug the whole project and fix any issue you find along the line." Started from the health gates (`make check`, `tsc -b`, `vitest`, `terraform validate`) and followed each signal to its root. **Security (CRITICAL):** a live **SQL injection** in `?sort=` on `GET /v1/billing|claims|containers` (reachable by the customer role) — `build_paginated_query` validated only the segment after the last `.` while interpolating the whole string into `ORDER BY`, so `sort=id,(select pg_sleep(5))::text,b.created_at` executed (reproduced against Postgres, 2.2s vs 0.1s); fixed at the single choke point (`fullmatch` of `identifier[.identifier]` + allowlist), 51 regression tests driving the real handler→service→repo chain, confirmed to fail on the pre-fix code, and all 46 other dynamic-SQL sites audited clean. **Security (LOW–MEDIUM):** upload file names were embedded raw in S3 keys and `Content-Disposition` headers in four presign flows (quote breakout, extension spoofing via bidi override) — `safe_filename()` at upload and download, Unicode/CJK names preserved. **Security (HIGH):** `shared/url_safety.py` closes an SSRF in the tenant webhook (`^https://` was the only guard; the fetch runs in the VPC beside Aurora/Redis, so `https://169.254.169.254/…`, RFC1918, localhost, hostnames resolving to those and redirects to them were all reachable, and the `/webhook/test` endpoint echoed raw connect errors) — save-time validator + delivery-time DNS check + pinned-IP connection + no redirects, verified against real TLS. **Infrastructure drift (works locally, fails only in AWS):** an audit of served routes vs Terraform found ~80 routes never declared in API Gateway (including every `/v1/notifications/*`), all three notification Lambdas configured with a handler (`lambda_handler`) that doesn't exist, producers with no queue-URL env vars and no `sqs:SendMessage` (every notification silently dropped), `metering` never exposed to API Gateway (its route silently dropped by `active_routes`), missing S3/Cognito/KMS grants derived from the code's actual boto3 calls, and a permissions boundary whose `kms:RequestAlias` condition could never match the code's key-ARN/keyless usage (would have denied all PII field encryption). Added VPC `notif-api`/`notif-webhook` Lambdas, wired queue URLs, corrected boundary + role policies, `batch_size = 1` on the notification consumers. **Guard:** `tests/unit/test_infrastructure_contracts.py` (frontend call → route, route → deployed service, handler → real function) — confirmed by running it against the pre-fix Terraform, where it reports 22 unroutable calls, the orphaned metering route and all three bad handlers. **Correctness:** 4 rotted tenant-isolation tests repaired (mocks had drifted: 4-tuple return, later-added unmocked repo/config calls); frontend `npm run build` was failing on 4 type errors that `vitest` can't see; a `TenantReports` stale-response race; a `None` dereference in the payment-verify race path. **Gates:** ruff 89→0, mypy 31→0, `tsc -b` clean, ESLint 0 errors. **Left open, deliberately (need an owner — see `docs/runbook.md` → Open infrastructure decisions):** whether RLS is actually enforced in AWS (Lambdas connect as `postgres`, which on RDS typically has `BYPASSRLS`, not the `logistics_app` role migration 005 provisions), NAT/egress for Paystack/Bybit/SMS/WhatsApp ("no NAT in v1" contradicts features built since), the unadopted Lambda authorizer (impersonation cannot work in AWS), unprovisioned provider credentials, and the 937 kB single-chunk frontend. **Dependencies:** `safety` flags 6 advisories on the pinned `cryptography==46.0.5` (low exposure — Fernet + RS256 verify only); a bump to `50.0.1` was verified in a clean venv (full suite green, `pip check` clean) but is left for its own PR per `CLAUDE.md`; `npm audit` shows 2 moderate `react-router` advisories that aren't reachable here. **Verified live** against the seeded local stack: injection payloads → 422 with nothing executed (incl. customer role), SSRF targets refused, a real webhook delivers through the hardened opener, all 50 migrations run from scratch. **Not verified:** no `terraform plan`/`apply` has been run — `terraform validate` and the contract tests only. 1141 backend + 799 frontend unit tests pass. Docs: `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` (§2.2 sort-expression rule, §8.3 boundary note, new §8.4 SSRF, §8.5 contracts), `10_api_tracking.md` / `11_api_billing.md` / `12_api_claims.md` (`sort` → 422), `02_shared_utilities.md` + `06_shared_layer_implementation.md` (`shared/url_safety.py`, `safe_filename`), `09_api_manifest.md` / `10_api_tracking.md` / `12_api_claims.md` / `13_api_payment_release.md` (filename sanitisation), `04c_terraform_api_gateway.md`, `04d_terraform_storage_messaging.md`, `04e_terraform_lambda_monitoring.md`, `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `15_api_notifications.md`, `17_testing.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `docs/runbook.md`. |
| 7.80 ✅ | App-wide responsive pass — every domain, not just the areas already touched | User: "Responsiveness of the website is not working properly. Make sure that it is responsive 100% across all pages", scoped via follow-up questions to a comprehensive all-domain pass prioritizing UX quality over speed ("we are still in development phase, it is not live, so nothing to rush"), and an explicit choice of the real documented `Table` card-list fallback over extending the existing horizontal-scroll pattern when both options were presented. A full audit found coverage very uneven: 22 nigeria-domain page stylesheets had zero responsive CSS; the customer-portal shell had no mobile nav at all; the long-documented "`Table` collapses to a card list on mobile" rule (`frontend/07_domain_modules.md`'s Cross-Domain UX Conventions) had never actually been built — the real component just scrolled horizontally; the 44×44px/16px-input/full-screen-modal mobile rules were implemented on exactly one screen (`WarehouseDashboard`) and not even mirrored in its own near-duplicate (`ReleaseNoteDetail`'s `RecordPickupModal`); ~18 hand-rolled modals and ~15 fixed-column stat grids overflowed or clipped below ~480–860px, several with a literal fixed `width: 480px` on the modal panel — genuinely wider than any phone, not just unoptimized. **Built the real `Table` card view** (`src/design/components/Table.tsx`): a `ResizeObserver`-driven JS switch between two structurally different DOM trees (table vs. card list), not a CSS-only restyle — chosen deliberately over the plan's originally-proposed "render both markups, let `@container` CSS pick" approach once it became clear that would make every existing `getByText()` row assertion across all 29 `<Table>`-consuming pages ambiguous (both markups present in the DOM at once). Convention (column 0 → card header, a trailing empty-title column → the action row, everything else → a label:value pair) held with zero exceptions across all 29 existing call sites, so the whole feature needed zero prop changes at any of them; one new optional `TableColumn.hideInCard` escape hatch added. **Extracted `ShellDrawer`** (`src/shell/ShellDrawer.tsx`) out of `PageShell.tsx` so the previously drawer-less customer shell (`CustShell`/`CustomerTopNav`) could get the same mobile hamburger nav the staff shell already had. **Applied one canonical mobile-first bottom-sheet modal pattern** (mirrored from the one screen that already had it right, `WarehouseDashboard`'s pickup sheet) across ~18 hand-rolled modals spanning nigeria/china/tenant-admin/platform/customer (3 different local class-naming conventions, so this could not be a single global selector) plus 4 more inline-styled modals with no CSS module at all. **Standardized fixed-column stat grids onto `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(150px, 1fr))`** across ~15 files, including converting several inline `style={{gridTemplateColumns: '1fr 1fr'}}` occurrences (`customer/Account.tsx`, `tenant-admin/ActivityLog.tsx`, `platform/CreateTenantPage.tsx`) that a `@container` rule could never have targeted in the first place. Added `flex-wrap` to several hero/toolbar rows found missing it during a final sweep (`BillingDetail`/`ClaimDetail` hero action rows, `PackingListList`'s bulk-selection toolbar, `CreatePackingList`/`ImportPackingLists`'s multi-step form footers) — genuine overflow risk once more than 2 buttons render. Fixed the `Field` component's 14px input font-size (below iOS Safari's 16px auto-zoom threshold) app-wide in one place. **Live-browser verification (Playwright, 375×800) caught two overflow bugs unit tests structurally cannot** — jsdom has no layout engine and never evaluates `@container`/`@media` at all: `BillingList`'s stats grid stayed wider than the phone viewport even after the `Table` card view landed, and `TopBar`'s injected page-action buttons didn't fit at the existing 860px breakpoint because `.topbar` had a fixed height with no way for a second action row to wrap. This coverage is now permanent in `e2e/flows/responsive_smoke.spec.ts` (5 cases: `BillingList` card view, `BillingDetail` hero actions, `PackingListList`'s live bulk-selection toolbar, all three tabs of the customer `Account` page, and a full open→navigate→close round-trip of the customer-portal drawer). 799 frontend unit tests pass (+32 over the pre-pass baseline: new `Table.test.tsx` card-view cases, new `CustomerTopNav.test.tsx` drawer cases), zero regressions; only the same pre-existing, unrelated tsc errors remain (none introduced by this pass). Docs: `frontend/02_design_tokens_and_theming.md` (860px breakpoint convention written down for the first time), `frontend/03_component_library.md` (Table/Modal rows updated, full addendum), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (Cross-Domain UX Conventions corrected + new dated section), `17_testing.md` (new test-coverage section). |
| 7.79 ✅ | CBM rate: tenant admins can now choose a currency (USD/NGN) and price by package type (carton/bag/bale) instead of one flat USD rate | User: "For CMB rate, let's allow the tenant admin to have multiple options for the rate: 1. One Single rate with options of either Naira or Dollar. 2. Rates targeted to Bags, Cartons and Bales (for e.g carton and bags can have the same rate while bale can have a different rate)." Confirmed with the user beforehand: unrecognized package types (pallet/item/mixed/unspecified/anything else) fall back to the carton rate; currency is one tenant-wide choice applying regardless of mode, not selectable per package type. **Key design simplification**: "flat" isn't a separate code path anywhere — it's just the state where `carton_rate == bag_rate == bale_rate`, enforced server-side by `UpdateCbmRateRequest`'s validator; `compute_cbm_charge` never branches on `rate_mode`, only on the actual rate values and each item's `package_unit`. New `shared/cbm_rate.py` (mirrors `shared/fx_rate.py`'s role, direct SQL against `tenant_config` rather than importing `services.tenant_management`'s repository) centralizes reading/normalizing the config (backward-compatible with the pre-2026-09-20 single-rate shape, no data migration needed) and the pure pricing function. `services/billing/service.py::handle_payment_due` now groups a customer's CBM by package type (`repo.get_cbm_by_package_type`, new) before pricing, instead of one flat `SUM(cbm)`. New `billing` columns (migration 050): `cbm_rate_currency`, `cbm_rate_mode` (both descriptive/audit only), `cbm_breakdown` (JSONB, always populated, the actual per-bucket detail in the tenant's configured currency). The pre-existing `cbm_rate_usd` column stays exact (no new rounding drift) whenever the tenant's three rates are genuinely equal, only falling back to a blended average (`subtotal_usd / total_cbm`) for a real multi-rate mix — this construction means `resolve_dispute` (CBM dispute resolution) needed **zero code changes**, verified live against a real per-package-type invoice. **Two real bugs caught live, both invisible to mocked unit tests**: (1) `get_cbm_rate`'s response returned raw `Decimal` values, which `shared/responses.py`'s `success()` serializes via `json.dumps(..., default=str)` — silently a JSON *string*, while `update_cbm_rate`'s response (already `float`-cast) returned a JSON *number* for the same field; loading the real `CbmRateConfig.tsx` page crashed (`config.carton_rate.toFixed is not a function`) since every Vitest test for this page mocks the API to return a plain number literal, never exercising real JSON serialization — caught only by a new e2e test loading the real page against the real backend. Fixed by casting `get_cbm_rate`'s response to `float`, matching `update_cbm_rate`. (2) `update_cbm_rate`'s return statement was a leftover `{**new_value, "previous_rate_usd": current.get("rate_usd")}` from the pre-2026-09-20 code — `current` no longer has a `"rate_usd"` key, so this was always `None` and unused by the frontend; fixed to return `new_value` directly. **Frontend**: `CbmRateConfig.tsx` rewritten (currency + rate-mode toggles, both styled like `DollarRateConfig.tsx`'s existing toggle pattern); `CreatePackingList.tsx`'s live pricing card made currency-aware; `BillingDetail.tsx` (staff) and `MyBillingDetail.tsx` (customer) both gained a conditional per-package-type breakdown shown only when `cbm_rate_mode === 'per_package_type'` — added specifically to close a real trust/audit gap flagged during design review (a customer on a per-type or NGN-priced invoice would otherwise see only a back-derived blended USD number corresponding to nothing they were actually billed against). Also fixed a pre-existing, unrelated `rate_history` bug found along the way: the backend has always written `rate_type="cbm_rate"`, but the frontend queried `"cbm_rate_per_unit"` (the `tenant_config` key, not the `rate_history` rate_type) — the CBM Rate History table/chart silently never matched any row. **Verified live against the real running stack**: all four currency/mode combinations via direct `PUT`; a genuine mixed carton+bale billing record generated via `handle_payment_due` against real seeded data (carton 1.85 CBM × $45 = $83.25, bale 3.2 CBM × $60 = $192.00, blended rate $54.5050 exactly) confirmed correct via both a direct DB read and the real `GET /v1/billing/{customer-id}` response; a CBM dispute filed and resolved against that invoice confirming the zero-code-change claim; the `rate_history` round-trip across 4 consecutive changes including the tenant's real pre-existing history row. Test data cleaned up afterward; tenant rate reset to its original flat/USD/$45 state. Used this task to also restore three earlier-session Postman collection updates (Initialize/Verify Online Payment, warehouse CRUD routes, the dollar-rate markup PUT body, and migration-041 release-note/pickup body fields) that a stray `git checkout` on that one file had accidentally reverted mid-task — caught immediately via `git diff --stat` showing an unexpectedly large diff after a routine JSON fix attempt, and recovered by hand from this session's own knowledge of the correct current API contracts rather than from any backup. 922 backend unit tests pass (+28: `test_cbm_rate.py` new, plus `TestUpdateCbmRateRequestValidator`/`TestGetCbmRate`/`TestUpdateCbmRate`/`TestHandlePaymentDueCbmPricing` additions); 786 frontend unit tests pass (+10: `CbmRateConfig.test.tsx` new, `CreatePackingList.test.tsx` +1); only the 3 pre-existing unrelated tsc errors remain. New e2e: `cbm-rate-config.spec.ts`. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md` (CBM Rate section rewritten), `11_api_billing.md` (Billing Calculation section rewritten), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (migration 050), `02_shared_utilities.md` + `06_shared_layer_implementation.md` (new `shared/cbm_rate.py`), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section), `17_testing.md` (new e2e coverage section), `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json` (new CBM-rate routes + the restored items above). |
| 7.78 ✅ | Release flow audit: count-based release unreachable from the UI, a real duplicate-release bug, a stale release-notes list, and zero e2e coverage for the migration-041 fields | User: "double check release flow, we need to make sure that everything is alinged and wokring well and E2e is tested from front end." Started by running the one pre-existing release-note e2e spec (`billing-payment-and-release.spec.ts`) — 2 of 3 tests skipped, confirmed benign (its shared fixture had already been driven to a terminal state earlier this session), but it also confirmed that file gives zero coverage of any migration-041 field (warehouse, document/invoice numbers, count-based release, officer pickers) since it predates that work entirely. **Found three real bugs while building that coverage, none from code review:** (1) count-based release (`packages_released`/`package_unit`) was a fully-working backend feature with no UI path to it at all — `GenerateReleaseNoteModal` never rendered a field for it and hard-required carton tags to submit; (2) `generate_release_note` had no duplicate/over-release guard — reproduced live by re-POSTing carton tags already on a `picked_up` release note, which silently created a second, fully valid `active` note authorising the same cartons for pickup a second time (a real double-release/fraud path); (3) none of `ReleaseNoteDetail.tsx`'s three mutations (pickup, split, reissue) nor `BillingDetail.tsx`'s generate-success handler ever invalidated the release-notes list's query cache, so the list served up to 3 minutes of stale data (e.g. a freshly-split child note missing from the list) despite `lib/queryClient.ts`'s own documented "mutations invalidate immediately" contract. **Fixes**: added a Carton-tags/Count toggle to `GenerateReleaseNoteModal` (mirrors `DollarRateConfig.tsx`'s existing toggle pattern); added `get_active_release_note_carton_tags`/`get_active_release_note_packages_released` (scoped to `active`/`picked_up` notes only — reissue/split are separate, unaffected code paths) and a check in `generate_release_note` raising `BusinessRuleViolationError` (`BR-RN-DUPLICATE-001`/`BR-RN-OVERRELEASE-001`) before the payment-status branch; added `qc.invalidateQueries({queryKey: releaseNoteKeys.all})` to all four success handlers. Also fixed a real a11y/testability gap found along the way (4 `<select>`s with a visible `<label>` but no `htmlFor`/`id` association — added matching `aria-label`s). **New e2e coverage**: `release-note-migration-fields.spec.ts` (count-based generation with warehouse/document/invoice fields, detail-page rendering, pickup with the officer pickers, print view, PDF export) and `release-note-split.spec.ts` (generate two tags, split one into a child, confirm it lists as a second "Partial release" row) — both found and fixed a real, repeatable race along the way (a one-shot `.count()` immediately after navigating to the release-notes list can catch the `Table` component's loading skeleton instead of the real rows; fixed with an auto-retrying `toHaveCount` everywhere this pattern appears, including in the pre-existing spec file). Also hit an unrelated environmental snag mid-task: a Docker container restart earlier in the session had wiped Moto's in-memory EventBridge bus, so `generate_release_note` started 500ing on the publish-event step *after* its DB write had already (incorrectly, pre-fix) succeeded — `make bootstrap` restores this (`make up` alone does not); resolved and clearly separated from the actual duplicate-release bug before concluding the fix was correct. 894 backend unit tests pass (+4: `TestGenerateReleaseNoteDuplicateGuard`); 776 frontend unit tests pass (no frontend unit changes needed — the fixes are UI/query-cache only, covered by the new e2e specs). Docs: `13_api_payment_release.md` (new "Duplicate / over-release guard" section), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section), `17_testing.md` (new e2e coverage section). |
| 7.77 ✅ | Online Paystack payments never actually got recorded — webhook-only confirmation is structurally unreachable in local dev, and unreliable even in prod | User: "when i pay a bill as a customer through paystack, it showed successful, view invoice and when i clicked view invoice, it took be back to the same billing page and on that page the payment is not made. if i click pay it attempts making the payment again." Root cause: `billing.status` was updated *exclusively* by `POST /v1/webhooks/payment`, an HMAC-verified server-to-server callback from Paystack — but Paystack's servers can never reach a `localhost` URL at all, so in local dev that webhook simply never fires, no matter how many times a payment genuinely succeeds; even in production a webhook can be delayed, retried, or dropped. `PaymentSuccess.tsx` (the page Paystack's hosted checkout redirects back to) compounded this by unconditionally rendering a static "being confirmed automatically" success message with zero actual verification. **Fix**: new `POST /v1/payments/{billing-id}/verify` (customer, own billing) reconciles a payment directly against Paystack's own `GET /transaction/verify/:reference` API (`paystack.verify_transaction()`, new) instead of only ever trusting a webhook — the reference is checked to actually belong to the given billing id first (`BR-PAY-VERIFY-001`), then fed into the *same* `process_webhook_payment()` the real webhook already uses (one confirmation code path, not two), whose existing `gateway_transaction_id` `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (migration 018) makes running both this and a webhook that eventually also fires completely safe. `PaymentSuccess.tsx` now reads `reference`/`trxref` from the URL and calls this on mount, showing a real verifying/success/pending/error state instead of a guaranteed-cheerful one. **Verified live against the real Paystack test API, not mocked** — the practice that's caught a real bug in every pass this session: initialized a real transaction for the *exact* billing record from the bug report and confirmed it read as "abandoned" (unpaid); completed it for real via Paystack's server-to-server test Charge API (`4084084084084081`, no OTP in test mode — sidesteps needing to browser-automate Paystack's own hosted checkout UI) to get a genuinely successful reference with no real money involved; called the new verify path and confirmed the exact stuck billing record flipped to `status: "paid"`, `balance_ngn: "0.00"` — checked via the real `GET /v1/billing/{customer_id}` HTTP endpoint the frontend itself calls, not just a direct DB read; called it a second time with the same reference and confirmed it stayed idempotent (still reports success, no duplicate `payment_record` row). 890 backend unit tests pass (+11: `TestVerifyTransaction` for the raw Paystack HTTP call mocked at `urllib.request.urlopen`, `TestConfirmOnlinePayment` for the service function's ownership/reference-matching/dedup/not-yet-successful logic); 776 frontend unit tests pass (+6, new `PaymentSuccess.test.tsx`); only the 3 pre-existing unrelated tsc errors remain. Docs: `13_api_payment_release.md` (new route + reconciliation section, corrected the now-stale "source of truth is the webhook alone" claim), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section). |
| 7.76 ✅ | Reloading any page reset the active dev role to platform_admin, bouncing every page to the wrong dashboard | User: "when i am on tab e.g customer or any other tab and i refresh, it goes to the dashboard. this is experienced in the tenant admin page. i believe this issue will be persistent on other pages as well, check all the pages and ensure that we handle it properly." Root cause, found by reading `LocalAuthProvider.tsx`'s `identity` state then confirming live with Playwright before writing any fix: `identity` (which of the 7 dev roles `RoleSwitcher` has active) was plain `useState<DevIdentityKey>(initialRole)` with nothing persisting it, and `App.tsx` never passes an `initialRole` — so every reload silently restarted it at the hardcoded `'platform_admin'` default, discarding whatever role was actually active. `RoleGuard` then saw the current route's `allow` list no longer matched and redirected to that (now wrong) role's home page — `platform_admin` → `/platform/dashboard`, a "dashboard"-titled page, matching the report exactly. Live-reproduced systemically across 4 different route families (an `/admin` sub-page, a `ConfigLayout` config sub-nav page, a China-office page, a customer-portal page) — confirming the user's "this will be persistent on other pages" prediction, since the bug lives in the shared identity-restoration step, not any individual page. Also investigated a theoretically similar race for a *real* Cognito login tested against local dev (`LocalAuthProvider` has no `'loading'` status window during its async cookie restore the way `SessionProvider` does) — tried to reproduce it live (real invite claim → login → reload) and could not; the local cookie round-trip resolves fast enough that React never commits the stale redirect, and production's actual code path (`SessionProvider`) was already independently confirmed immune to this class of race entirely. Left unfixed rather than adding an unproven ~20-test migration and a loading flash to every dev-shim page load for a race with no evidence it's ever hit — documented for future reference instead. **Fix**: the dev-shim identity is now persisted across a reload via new `getPersistedDevIdentity`/`setPersistedDevIdentity`/`clearPersistedDevIdentity` functions in `src/lib/localDevSession.ts` (sessionStorage-backed, kept outside `src/auth/` per that module's existing pattern from the earlier logout fix, since `frontend-ci.yml`'s token-storage grep scans that directory literally) — `LocalAuthProvider` now restores the last-picked role on mount instead of always falling back to the hardcoded default, and every `RoleSwitcher` pick persists going forward. **Verified two ways**: 3 new unit cases in `LocalAuthProvider.test.tsx` (a picked role surviving a simulated reload via unmount+remount, a persisted identity taking priority over `initialRole`, the existing default/initialRole cases now also proving the fallback still works when nothing's persisted) — 769 frontend unit tests pass. Live e2e: new `describe('dev role survives a reload')` block in `e2e/flows/auth.spec.ts` (4 cases, one per major role family) reproduces the exact bug on the unfixed code and passes cleanly after the fix; re-ran alongside the existing `auth.spec.ts`/`auth-logout.spec.ts` cases (14 total) to confirm no regression to the two logout fixes from earlier the same week. **Same-day follow-up:** user reported "the dev role switcher is not working," persisting after a dev-server restart and hard browser refresh — ruled out stale HMR state, pointing at a real code bug. Root cause: `session = impSession ?? cognitoSession ?? (loggedOut ? null : roleSession)` — a lingering real Cognito session (routine in this session's testing history, e.g. left over from earlier real-login flows in the same browser tab) unconditionally outranks the dev shim, so picking a role updated the `<select>`/`identity` exactly as designed but had zero visible effect on the actual session, with no indication why — indistinguishable from "does nothing." Reproduced live before any fix: log in as a real customer, pick `tenant_admin` in the switcher, the app stayed on the customer's own `/my-shipments` page. Fixed by extracting `logout()`'s Cognito-ending logic into a shared `endCognitoSession()` that `setIdentity()` now also calls whenever a real session is active — picking a dev role is an unambiguous "use the shim now" signal, so it ends any real session in its way rather than being silently outranked. This also closes the specific reload race noted above as "left unfixed" for the case where the switcher is used while a real session is active, since there's no longer a lingering valid cookie to race against afterward. New unit case in `LocalAuthProvider.test.tsx` (log in for real, pick a dev role, assert `signOut`/`revokeServerSession` fire and the session fully reflects the picked role); re-reproduced the bug live with Playwright before the fix and confirmed it resolved after. 770 frontend unit tests pass total. Docs: `frontend/04_auth_and_session.md` (new ground-truth entry), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (pointer entry). |
| 7.75 ✅ | Dollar rate switched to a Bybit P2P black-market base + per-tenant markup (percentage or flat ₦) | User: "for the dollar rate i want to use the black market price, using the bybit p2p naira rate, then on the frontend we can add a markup for the tenant admins, such that when they update the markup, it when the api is fetched, it adds the markup and that's the dollar rate use for their customer. markup can be in percentage (0.1%, 2%, 3%...) or in dollars (1, 2, 4, 10...)." Confirmed with the user beforehand: no manual rate-override escape hatch (Bybit + a persisted fallback is the only safety net), the flat markup is a plain ₦ amount added to the NGN/USD rate, and the Bybit fetch is cached ~10 minutes. **Root design**: previously `dollar_rate` was a number typed into `tenant_config`, read independently by 4 separate call sites (tenant_management, billing, claims, refunds) with no external source, no caching (despite `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` claiming a 60s in-memory one that was verified to not actually exist anywhere — corrected in that doc now) and no scheduled job. Live-curled Bybit's real, undocumented public P2P endpoint (`api2.bybit.com/fiat/otc/item/online`) while designing this to nail the exact request/response shape and confirm which `side` value (sell-USDT ads, `side="1"`, sorted cheapest-first) gives the correct "cost to acquire USD" rate — no guessing at an unverified external API. **New `shared/fx_rate.py`** centralizes all 4 call sites into one `get_effective_dollar_rate(tenant_id)`: fetches Bybit (mean of the 5 cheapest ads, circuit-breaker guarded), caches in Redis (`DistributedCache`, 10 min TTL, platform-wide since the black-market rate isn't tenant data), falls back to a `platform_settings`-persisted last-known-good rate on fetch failure, and a hardcoded constant as a final safety net so billing/claims/refunds can never hard-block on a missing rate. Per-tenant markup lives in a new `tenant_config['dollar_rate_markup']` key (`{markup_type: "percentage"|"flat", markup_value}`, default zero so no tenant's pricing jumps on rollout) — no migration needed, both `tenant_config` and `platform_settings` already existed as flexible JSONB stores. `billing/service.py::handle_payment_due`, `claims/service.py` (2 call sites), and `refunds/service.py` (2 call sites) all now call the shared function instead of their own independent `tenant_config` reads; `refunds/repository.py::get_refund_threshold_config` and `claims/repository.py::get_dollar_rate` (the latter deleted outright) no longer touch the dollar rate at all, keeping those repository modules "pure SQL" as their own docstrings claim. `PUT /v1/tenant/dollar-rate`'s contract changed from `{rate, currency_pair}` to `{markup_type, markup_value}` (deliberate breaking change — single frontend in this repo); `GET` now returns `base_rate`/`markup_type`/`markup_value`/`source`/`fetched_at` alongside the existing `rate`/`currency_pair`/`effective_from`. Small justified extraction alongside this: `paystack.py`'s private `_ssl_context()` became shared `shared/ssl_context.py::verified_ssl_context()`, now used by both external-HTTP integrations rather than duplicated a second time. **Frontend**: `DollarRateConfig.tsx` rewritten — Base rate / Your markup / Effective rate `Kvp` rows replace the old single editable number; the update form became a Percentage/Flat toggle + one input whose label switches with it. `RateHistoryTable`/`RateTrendChart` needed zero changes since `rate_history` still stores a plain numeric `rate` (now the effective rate at the moment a markup changed, not a typed-in value). **Live-verified end-to-end** against the real running stack: `GET` returned a genuine live Bybit rate (₦1368.80/$); a repeat call within the cache window returned the identical `fetched_at` (proving the cache); `PUT` with a flat and a percentage markup produced exactly correct arithmetic (`1368.80+15=1383.80`; `1368.80×1.05=1437.24`); a fresh `handle_payment_due` billing record froze `exchange_rate_ngn` at the new computed rate. **Caught one real bug live**: `rate_history` silently stopped recording anything the moment this shipped — `repo.append_rate_history`'s `json.dumps()` has no `default=str` (unlike `shared/audit.py`'s) and raised on the raw `Decimal` values `get_effective_dollar_rate` returns, swallowed by `update_dollar_rate`'s own try/except; fixed by casting to `float` before that call, re-verified live (history rows now append correctly) and locked in with a unit test that actually round-trips the values through `json.dumps` rather than mocking the function that would have raised. 879 backend unit tests pass (+26: `test_fx_rate.py` new, plus threshold-enforcement coverage added to `test_refunds.py` that didn't exist before); 767 frontend unit tests pass (+8, new `DollarRateConfig.test.tsx`); only the 3 pre-existing unrelated tsc errors remain. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md` (Dollar Rate section rewritten), `02_shared_utilities.md` + `06_shared_layer_implementation.md` (new `shared/fx_rate.py`/`shared/ssl_context.py`), `05a_database_schema_core.md` (`dollar_rate` marked superseded, new `dollar_rate_markup` shape), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (new `platform_settings` key, `rate_history` shape note), `11_api_billing.md`, `14_api_refunds.md`, `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` (corrected the stale cache claim), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section). |
| 7.74 ✅ | Explicit logout in local dev didn't actually log out — fixed for immediate logout, page reload, and a follow-up dev-role-switcher paper cut | User: "i logged in as ifesinachi (customer), when i logout and refresh the page it still logged in. Double check all the state management and fix all." Root-caused to `LocalAuthProvider` (mounted for the whole app whenever `VITE_ENVIRONMENT=local`, i.e. always in this dev setup) rather than `SessionProvider` (audited and confirmed already correct — its `logout()` properly sets `session: null`/`status: 'unauthenticated'`, no fallback). Two compounding bugs, both latent since the 2026-09-06 fix that first gave `LocalAuthProvider` a real Cognito `login()`/`logout()`: (1) `status` was hardcoded to `'authenticated'` unconditionally, so `RoleGuard`'s unauthenticated-redirect check could never fire regardless of session state; (2) `session` derived as `impSession ?? cognitoSession ?? roleSession` with no way to express "logged out" at all — clearing `cognitoSession` on logout just revealed the always-on dev-identity fallback (`roleSession`, e.g. `platform_admin` if the switcher was never touched) as a fully live, re-authenticated session (its header-setting `useEffect` fired too, not just the display), and since none of that is state that survives a reload, refreshing reproduced the exact same outcome — matching the "even after refresh" half of the report precisely. Fixed with a `loggedOut` flag (`LocalAuthProvider.tsx`) cleared by `login()`/`startImpersonation()`/picking a role via `RoleSwitcher`/a genuinely-restored cookie session, persisted across reload via a new `frontend/src/lib/localDevSession.ts` — deliberately outside `src/auth/`, since `frontend-ci.yml`'s literal grep for `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` in that directory would otherwise flag it (holds no token/PII, dev-only). Also fixed a related paper cut found while testing: re-selecting the *same* dev role from `RoleSwitcher` post-logout fired no `<select>` `onChange` at all (value unchanged) leaving the switcher looking inert — fixed with a distinct disabled "— logged out —" placeholder option whenever `status === 'unauthenticated'`. **Verified two ways**: 12 new unit tests in `LocalAuthProvider.test.tsx` (immediate post-logout, simulated reload via unmount+remount, a stale-cookie-wins-over-the-marker edge case, re-entering via the switcher, logging back in afterward) — 758 frontend unit tests pass total. Live, end-to-end, against the real running stack with an actual Cognito-via-Moto login: new `frontend/e2e/flows/auth-logout.spec.ts` claims a real invite, logs in for real, logs out, asserts `/login` (not a dev-shim home page), reloads and confirms it stays on `/login`, navigates straight to a protected route and confirms the bounce-back, then logs in again successfully — all 4 pass run serially (the file's default 4-way parallelism intermittently fails at the shared, pre-existing `mint_test_invite.py` setup step under concurrent DB/Moto load — a known flakiness class also latent in `customer-invite.spec.ts`, not a regression from this fix). **Same-day follow-up:** user reported "The Dev selection bar for roles is no longer working" immediately after this shipped. Root cause: `api/client.ts`'s `recoverFrom401` calls this same `logout()` on any unrecovered 401 — already wired up, routine in local dev (any endpoint the current dev role can't call), and previously harmless since the old `logout()` had no lasting effect with no real Cognito session active. The new sticky `loggedOut` flag turned every such stray 401 into a permanent (reload-surviving) lockout of the switcher. Fixed by making `logout()` a true no-op — skip `clearActiveTokens()` and the flag both — whenever `cognitoSession` is null, so only a real session's own logout can ever set it. New regression test confirms a no-session `logout()` call leaves status/session/headers untouched; re-verified `auth-logout.spec.ts` (real login) and `auth.spec.ts` (all 6 dev roles) both pass together in the same serial run. Docs: `frontend/04_auth_and_session.md` (corrected a now-stale claim + new ground-truth entry), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (pointer entry). |
| 7.73 ✅ | Release note expanded to match a real paper "Goods Release Note" form; multi-tenant warehouse table; PDF export field-completeness + QR crash fix | User shared 4 photos of a real "Goods Release Note" / "Cash Receipt" pair from a Nigerian logistics company and asked to "expan on the release note that we already have... ensure that we sure make that it is a multitanat app... it is [NOT] hardcorded... something that will be emcompassing for all other tanants." Migration 041 had already added the schema (warehouse table; release_note.warehouse_id/physical_document_number/packing_list_invoice_numbers/packages_released/package_unit/packages_balance/is_on_credit/credit_granted_by(_name); pickup_record officer columns) but the code reading/writing it had multiple silent-drop bugs. **Backend**: `get_release_note_by_id`/`list_release_notes` now join `warehouse.phone` and `carryover_ledger.due_date`; `create_split_release_note` was silently dropping every migration-041 column on every split *and* reissue (only carried migration-003 fields) — rewritten to carry all of them, with split vs. reissue given deliberately different carry-forward semantics (split is tag-based and does not copy packages_released/package_unit/packages_balance; reissue is a full clone and copies everything). New `_first_present()` helper resolves the two different key-naming schemes across call sites (caught its own bug: `Decimal("0.00")` is falsy, so `_first_present(...) or "0"` wrongly rendered a real zero balance as `"0"` — fixed with explicit `is not None` checks). New `warehouse` CRUD (`GET/POST /v1/warehouses`, `PATCH .../set-default`) with "tenant's first warehouse auto-becomes-default" logic (found live: a freshly created warehouse had `is_default: false` with no other mechanism to ever flip it). `record_pickup` now resolves and returns `customer_service_officer_name`/`accounting_officer_name` by ID. Second gap found live: the current local dev tenant had zero warehouse rows despite migration 041's backfill, because that backfill only ever ran once against tenants that existed at that moment — fixed by creating a default warehouse as a step of `tenant_management.create_tenant`. Widened `GET /v1/tenant/users` to `nigeria_office_staff`/`warehouse_attendant` (not `china_office_staff`) so `RecordPickupModal`'s officer pickers can populate, following this codebase's existing least-privilege carve-out pattern. **Frontend**: `GenerateReleaseNoteModal` gained warehouse/document-number/invoice-numbers/credit-granted-by/due-date fields; `RecordPickupModal` gained warehouse/CSO/accounting-officer pickers; `ReleaseNoteDetail`'s Kvp section now shows count-based carton progress, warehouse, credit/receipt status, and a combined payment line; `PrintReleaseNote.tsx` rewritten — the core bug found live: it always printed the one tenant-wide branding address regardless of which warehouse actually issued the note, now reads `note.warehouse_address` first — plus a conditional payment block and 4 named signature lines (Customer's Rep / CSO / Accounting Officer / Warehouse Attendant) with the paper form's exact liability disclaimer, replacing 2 generic signature lines. New `api/warehouses.ts` + `hooks/useWarehouses.ts`. **PDF export** (`export_release_note_pdf.py`): same silent-drop bug class — `get_release_note_for_pdf`'s query already joined `customer_phone`/`warehouse_*`/`billing_*_ngn`/`receipt_number`, but the generator only ever read 7 of ~20 available fields; rewrote its meta-block construction into a pure, independently-tested `build_meta_pairs()` and brought the signature block to the same 4-named-line parity as the print HTML. While fixing it, found a second, more serious pre-existing bug in `_qr_image()` (shared with `export_receipt_pdf.py`, the cash-receipt PDF): it set `.transform` on the `QrCodeWidget` itself, which reportlab's `AttrMap` rejects — **every call to either PDF generator had always raised `AttributeError`, meaning both PDF exports have never worked**, invisible because no prior test exercised past the repository mock. Fixed by setting `.transform` on the enclosing `Drawing`; added `tests/unit/test_export_release_note_pdf.py` (13 tests) and `tests/unit/test_export_receipt_pdf.py` (2 tests), both rendering a real PDF and asserting on the `%PDF` magic bytes. **Also discovered undocumented**: `export_receipt_pdf.py` and its `GET /v1/payments/{id}/receipt/pdf` route existed from earlier in this session but had never been added to `19_excel_import_export.md` — added now. **Live end-to-end verification** against the local stack (real dev server, not mocks) surfaced one more gap: a fresh `POST /v1/release-notes` with an explicit `warehouse_id` came back with `warehouse_name`/`customer_name`/`container_number` all null on the *immediate* response (only a follow-up GET showed them correctly) — `create_release_note`'s RETURNING row carries no joins, so `_fmt_release_note` had nothing to read. Fixed by backfilling those fields from data already fetched (`billing`, and the warehouse row resolved via the now-added `get_warehouse_by_id`) before formatting the response; both release-note and receipt PDF exports confirmed working end-to-end against the live server afterward (previously-crashing endpoints now return real, valid `%PDF`-prefixed documents). 850 backend unit tests pass (+16 net this task). Docs: `19_excel_import_export.md` (PDF field-completeness + QR bug + missing receipt-route entry), `13_api_payment_release.md` (new "Immediate-response gap fixed" note), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (migration 041 section added — was previously undocumented entirely). |
| 7.72 ✅ | `BillingDetail.tsx` never invalidated the billing query cache — fixed for every mutation on the page, not just the one reported | User: "i approved a payment, but it did not record the payment as paid, until i refreshed the page. check the state." Investigation confirmed the backend (`confirm_payment_claim`/`confirm_payment()`) writes `billing.status`/`amount_paid_ngn` correctly and synchronously — this was a pure frontend caching bug. Root cause, found to be systemic across the whole file: every billing-mutating handler (confirm/reject a payment claim, record a payment, waive storage fees, remove a misc charge, apply a carryover, cancel billing, acknowledge/resolve/reject a CBM dispute, generate a release note) only ever refreshed this component's own local `billing` React state (`load()`/`setBilling()`) — none called `queryClient.invalidateQueries`, so `BillingList.tsx`'s list query (5-minute `staleTime`) kept serving the stale row on every other screen until a hard refresh wiped the whole `QueryClient` — exactly "until I refreshed the page." Every comparable mutation elsewhere in this codebase (`useRecordPayment`/`useFileDispute`/`useResolveDispute` in `useBilling.ts`) already invalidates correctly; this page's confirm/reject/record-payment/etc. handlers were hand-rolled instead of using those hooks and the invalidation step was never added. Fixed with one shared `invalidateBillingCaches()` helper wired into all nine mutation call sites. **Verified**: new tests spy on `queryClient.invalidateQueries` for confirm-claim, reject-claim, and record-payment, each asserting the call happens. 744 frontend tests pass (+3), no backend change needed (confirmed correct by reading the write path, not modifying it). Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section). |
| 7.71 ✅ | Payment-claim staff notification now identifies the customer and payment source | User: "i sbmitted a payment, but on the staff's notification, it did not tell the staff the customer who submitted the payment, i think we should add it in the notification, so that the staff can quick know who has made the payment, and any other metadata that will help the staff quickly identify the payment source." `submit_payment_claim`'s bell notification (title="New payment claim submitted", body="A payment claim for NGN {amount} is awaiting review.") carried nothing to identify who paid. Fixed: title is now `"New payment claim — {customer_name}"` (unclamped in the notification panel, so the name is always fully visible); body is `"NGN {amount} · Invoice {invoice_number} · Container {container_number} · {phone}"`, omitting container/phone when the billing has none rather than rendering a literal "None," and falling back to "A customer" in the title if the customer join comes back empty. All four fields were already returned by `repo.get_billing_by_id()`'s existing customer/container join — only `customer_phone` needed adding to that one query (`cu.phone AS customer_phone`), no second query. Body text deliberately omits the customer's name (already in the title) since the notification panel's body is CSS-clamped to 2 lines and every character was competing for very little space. Verified live: ran the exact title/body construction against a real billing/customer record on the local Postgres stack — produced `"New payment claim — Ifesinachi Ugwuoke"` / `"NGN 367237.50 · Invoice ACM-INV-20260906-0004 · Container E2E-TEST-001 · +2348169760253"` (87 chars, comfortably inside the 2-line budget). 808 backend tests pass (+3: identifies-the-customer, omits-missing-fields, falls-back-when-name-missing). Docs: `13_api_payment_release.md` (new ground-truth entry). |
| 7.70 ✅ | Announcements moved to a dedicated page with full CRUD, "Publish"/"Unpublish" replaced the toggle | Two direct follow-ups rejecting the just-shipped popup/toggle approach in 7.69. First: "no, i wan the annoucement management to be on it's own page, not on the notification popup... Annoucement is managed in that page (CRUD), and they can enable only one announcement to be visible per time. for admin they can toggle staffs and customers. no approval is required for them to publish it. For staff they can only sent to customers, but it must be approved by the admin before it can become visible to the customer. The banner should have a colour that draws attention too. look at the color pallet." Second, reviewing that first pass: "Instead of toggle to enable an announcement, let it be "publish". For the admin. For staff, it will be submit announcement (it will show the state) once it is approved, it will show approved on the staff's end." **Route restructuring:** `announcement_request.id` and `staff_notification.announcement_group_id` previously shared one ambiguous `/v1/notifications/announcements/{id}/...` path; every request-scoped route moved under `.../requests/{id}/...` (approve/reject moved there; new PATCH/DELETE for a staff member editing/withdrawing their own still-pending request) while a bare `/v1/notifications/announcements/{id}` now unambiguously means a group (new tenant_admin-only PATCH to edit, DELETE to permanently remove — cascades to every recipient's bell via the existing FK, not just the banner). New `GET .../announcements/mine` (nigeria_office_staff's own submissions, every status). The dispatcher's one blanket `assert_role(TENANT_ADMIN)` became per-branch role checks since staff now legitimately owns some of these routes. No new migration — every new operation reuses existing columns behind the same ownership (`requested_by`)/status (`'pending'`) guards already established. **Frontend:** deleted `ManageAnnouncementsModal.tsx` outright; its content became `pages/staff/AnnouncementsPage.tsx` at a real route (`/announcements`, wired into `routeTable.ts`/`navConfig.ts`/`routes.tsx` for both roles) that branches its whole body by role — tenant_admin gets "Pending approval" + "All announcements" (each row: a **Publish**/**Unpublish** `Button` replacing the `ToggleSwitch`, a "Draft"/"Published" `Badge`, inline Edit and click-to-arm Delete); nigeria_office_staff gets "My announcements" (every submission with a status `Badge` — "Pending approval"/**"Approved"**/"Rejected", rejection reason shown inline, Edit/Withdraw only while pending). `StaffNotificationPanel.tsx` reverted to notification-only (no `useSession()`, no compose/manage triggers). `ComposeAnnouncementModal.tsx`'s staff button now reads "Submit announcement". `AnnouncementBanner.tsx` switched from `variant="info"` (blue) to `variant="warning"` (amber, existing `--color-warning` palette, same "pay attention" language `QuotaWarningBanner` already uses) per the colour request — no new design tokens added. **Verified live**: staff submits → shows in "My announcements" as pending → edits it → a genuinely different user (fabricated UUID) 404s trying to edit it (local dev's non-UUID `X-User-ID` header resolves by role to one seeded account, so two different-looking header strings for the same role can collide — a real gotcha hit and documented while verifying this) → tenant_admin approves via the new path → staff's row flips to "Approved". tenant_admin sends `all_staff`, clicks Publish (Draft→Published), edits the title (visible on a staff bell), deletes it (gone from that bell entirely, not just unpublished; repeat delete 404s). 805 backend (+18) and 741 frontend (+6 net, after deleting 5 modal tests and adding 15 page tests) tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (new section + corrections to the two 7.69 subsections' now-stale route tables), `05b_database_schema_extended.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.69 ✅ | Staff announcements now need tenant_admin approval; announcements gained a page banner | Two direct follow-ups sent in quick succession while reviewing 7.68. First: "Tenant admin can send announcement to all staffs and all customers. But the staff can only send to customers, which should be approved by the tenant admins, this is to ensure that a compromised staff does not misinformation the customers." **Approval:** `nigeria_office_staff` can no longer target `all_staff` at all (403, tenant_admin-only audience now); an `all_customers` request from staff creates a new `announcement_request` row (migration 048, RLS on) instead of an immediate notification — `status` pending/approved/rejected, `WHERE status='pending'` guards against double-review. New tenant_admin-only queue: `GET /v1/notifications/announcements/pending`, `PATCH .../{id}/approve` (creates the real notification via the same fan-out helper), `PATCH .../{id}/reject` (optional reason). `tenant_admin` sends (either audience) are unaffected — still immediate. Second follow-up, sent mid-review of the first: "annoucement should be a banner on the target user's web page, with this they won't miss it. It can be enabled or disabled, more can be created but only one can be visible per time... we can add amination to it, so that it is obvious to the target user." **Banner:** migration 049 adds `staff_notification.announcement_group_id` (ties an `all_staff` send's 4 fanned-out role-rows into one manageable unit) and `banner_active`. Creating any banner-eligible announcement first deactivates whatever was active for the *same audience* (staff and customer banners are fully independent — sending one never retires the other), enforcing "only one visible per time" per audience. New `GET /v1/notifications/{staff,customer}/banner` (the single active one, or null), `GET /v1/notifications/announcements` (tenant_admin history list, one entry per send), `PATCH .../{group_id}/activate|deactivate`. Frontend: `ComposeAnnouncementModal.tsx` hides the "All staff" chip entirely for `nigeria_office_staff` and relabels its button "Submit for approval"; new `ManageAnnouncementsModal.tsx` (tenant_admin-only, opened from a new "Manage announcements" trigger) holds the approval queue plus a `ToggleSwitch`-driven banner history list; new `AnnouncementBanner.tsx` — a presentational, CSS-animated (slide-down + pulse, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected) banner mounted once each in `StaffLayout.tsx`/`CustomerLayout.tsx` alongside `ImpersonationBanner`, backed by new 60s-polling `useStaffBanner`/`useCustomerBanner` hooks; dismissing it is tab-session-only state, deliberately never persisted, so it reappears on reload until an admin actually deactivates it. **Verified live**: staff→all_staff 403s before any row exists; staff→all_customers creates a pending request invisible to customers until a tenant_admin approves it, at which point the customer banner picks it up and the original requester's bell shows the approval; two sequential customer sends showed only the newer one as the active banner, and manually re-activating the older one via the toggle correctly retired the newer one; an all_staff banner had zero effect on the customer banner endpoint. 787 backend (+21) and 735 frontend (+21) tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (two new subsections), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (migrations 048/049), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section), `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.68 ✅ | Notification clearing (per-viewer) + manual staff announcements | Direct user follow-up to 7.66/7.67: "The notification should allow clearing. Secondly tenant admin and nigeria staff should be able to send a announcements, for example during celebration sessions, or import announcements." Clarified with the user that an announcement targets one broad audience per send — every staff member, or every customer — not a role/person picker. **Clearing:** new migration 047 adds `staff_notification_read.dismissed_at`; clearing lives on the existing per-viewer read-state row (not the shared notification row), so clearing a role-broadcast notification for one recipient never removes it from another recipient's list — clearing always implies read, and `list_staff_notifications` excludes dismissed rows from its query entirely. New `DELETE /v1/notifications/{staff,customer}/{id}` (clear one) and `.../clear-all` routes. **Announcements:** new `POST /v1/notifications/announce` (`tenant_admin`/`nigeria_office_staff` only), backed by a new, deliberately **error-propagating** `repository.create_staff_notification()` — not the existing fire-and-forget `notify_staff()`, since an announcement is the entire user-initiated action and a failed send must surface as a real error rather than silently no-op. `all_staff` fans out to one row per staff role (never a bare NULL/NULL broadcast row, which the shared visibility filter would also leak to customers); `all_customers` uses `target_role="customer"`. Frontend: new `ComposeAnnouncementModal.tsx` (Title/Message/audience-Chip form); both notification panels' rows split into a `rowMain` (click-to-mark-read) + separate `clearBtn` (×), plus a new "Clear all" header button; `StaffNotificationPanel.tsx` gained a `useSession()`-gated "New announcement" trigger (`ANNOUNCE_ROLES` mirroring the backend's `_ANNOUNCE_ROLES`) — required updating its test file's render helper to wrap in `SessionContext.Provider` for the first time. **Verified live**: `all_staff` send created 4 rows (one per role), visible to all staff roles and invisible to a customer session, `china_office_staff` 403s on the route itself; `all_customers` send reached two different customer sessions and no staff; dismissed one notification as one recipient of a broadcast row and confirmed a different recipient's copy was untouched; `clear-all` returned the correct count without touching other recipients, on both the staff and customer routes. 766 backend (+11) and 714 frontend (+18) tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (new section), `05b_database_schema_extended.md` (migration 047), `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` (new section, plus the previously-undocumented 7.67 customer-bell frontend entry added retroactively), `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.67 ✅ | Customer notification bell + local-dev delivery plumbing fixed, from one live bug report | User, right after the 7.66 staff bell shipped: "i rejected ifexalex2@gmail[.com]'s submitted payment with a comment that we did not receive the payment, and i did not see the notification when i logged in as ifexalex2@gmail.com." Investigating turned up three separate, real problems, all confirmed against the actual local stack before fixing anything: **(1)** the notification code was correct — the request log showed a real WhatsApp+email enqueue at the exact rejection timestamp; **(2)** nothing in local dev ever drains that queue (AWS wires SQS→Lambda automatically; Moto doesn't), so the message just sat there — this alone explained "saw nothing at all," independent of the other two; **(3)** the customer portal's own bell icon (`CustomerTopNav.tsx`) existed but was never wired to any data, the same gap the staff bell had before 7.66. Fixed all three, as the user chose. **Auto-drain:** `local_server.py` now spawns `scripts/drain_notifications.py --watch` as a background **process** (not thread — the server's `threaded=False` exists precisely because `shared/db.py`'s connection singleton isn't thread-safe, and a poller thread sharing it would reintroduce that exact corruption risk a subprocess avoids entirely). **Allowlist:** added `ifexalex2@gmail.com` to `.env.local`'s `NOTIFICATION_ALLOWLIST` (the phone was already there) — verified live the email attempt changed from "blocked by allowlist" to "attempted, failed on missing local SES credentials," i.e. genuinely progressed. **Customer bell:** reuses the exact same `staff_notification`/`staff_notification_read` tables and service functions from 7.66 — zero schema change — targeted via `target_user_id` (`customer.user_id`, added to the `get_customer_contact()`/`get_customers_for_container()` SELECTs in all 5 repos that have one) instead of `target_role`. New `/v1/notifications/customer[/unread-count]` + mark-read/mark-all-read routes, role-gated to `CUSTOMER` only, reusing the identical service layer. Wired into the same 6 customer-facing events already sending SMS/WhatsApp/email: `payment_claim_confirmed`/`rejected` (**the exact reported case**), `payment_due`, `shipment_update`, `container_arrived`, `damage_claim_update`, `packing_list_approved` — each guarded on the customer actually having a portal account. Frontend: `CustomerTopNav.tsx` gained the same `notificationsPanel`/`bellWrap` pattern as the staff `TopBar.tsx`; `CustomerLayout.tsx` wires a new `CustomerNotificationPanel.tsx` (small, separate component reusing the staff panel's CSS module rather than one shared component — the two portals never share a render tree). **Verified live, reproducing the exact report**: submitted a fresh claim as `ifexalex2@gmail.com`, rejected it as staff — without running anything by hand, `notification_log` showed all three channel attempts within seconds, and the customer's own unread count went 0→1 with the exact title/body of the rejection. 755 backend (+18) and 696 frontend (+10) tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (new section), `05b_database_schema_extended.md`, `13_api_payment_release.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `12_api_claims.md`, `08_api_packing_list.md`, `10_api_tracking.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`. |
| 7.66 ✅ | Staff notification bell built for real — the item 7.65 deferred | Direct user follow-up: "let's go through the whole notification including the frontend notification to resolve all." Asked to choose depth: a cheaper "live actionable-item count" (no new table) vs. a full per-user read/unread feed — user chose the full feed. New migration 046 (`staff_notification` + `staff_notification_read`, RLS on both): one row per event, broadcast to a role or targeted at a person, read state tracked per-viewer so a role-broadcast notification is independently readable by each recipient. New `shared/staff_notifications.py::notify_staff()`, modeled on `log_audit` — never raises. New routes on the existing notification Lambda: `GET /v1/notifications/staff[/unread-count]`, `PATCH /v1/notifications/staff/{id}/read`, `PATCH .../mark-all-read` — a broader role set (any staff role) than the compliance-scoped notification log. Wired into 6 trigger sites, each broadcasting to the relevant reviewer role(s): `payment_claim_submitted`, `damage_claim_filed` (customer-filed only), `packing_list_submitted` (single + bulk), `cbm_dispute_filed`, `cbm_dispute_sla_breach`, `cbm_dispute_escalated` (the last two alongside their pre-existing email-only notification). **Bug avoided, not just fixed:** `bulk_submit_packing_lists`' new notification call uses `entity_id=None` rather than a comma-joined id list — `entity_id` is a UUID column, and a comma-joined string would silently fail the cast exactly the way `log_audit`'s own pre-existing `BULK_APPROVE` call already does for its `entity_id` (found while writing this one, left as-is elsewhere since fixing a co-located bug was out of scope). Frontend: `TopBar.tsx` gained a `notificationsPanel` slot inside the bell's own positioning wrapper so a dropdown anchors correctly regardless of surrounding layout; `StaffLayout.tsx` wires it to a new polling hook + a new `StaffNotificationPanel.tsx` (mark-one-read, mark-all-read, empty state), gated by the same `!isPlatformShell` check `useBranding` already uses. **Verified live**: submitted a payment claim as a real customer → staff unread count 0→1 with the correct title; marking it read for one `nigeria_office_staff` session left a separate `tenant_admin` session's count untouched (independent per-viewer state, the core design claim, actually confirmed); `mark-all-read` cleared it; a `customer`-role request 403s; filed a damage claim and confirmed the second event type too. 737 backend (+23) and 686 frontend (+12) tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (new "Staff notification bell" section), `05b_database_schema_extended.md`, `13_api_payment_release.md`, `12_api_claims.md`, `08_api_packing_list.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `16_scheduled_jobs.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`. |
| 7.65 ✅ | Notifications audited app-wide: 6 of 8 customer notification events were defined but never triggered, including the exact bug reported | User: "a staff rejected a payment marked as paid, but no notification was showing when I logged into the staff account. Check all notifications within the app and ensure all are working." Investigation found two problems, one confirmed out of scope by the user at the time (see 7.66 — later reversed): **(1) no in-app staff notification bell exists** — `TopBar.tsx`'s bell icon and unread badge are fully wired, but `StaffLayout.tsx` never passes them data, so it always shows 0 and does nothing; its own doc-comment says "wired to GET /v1/notifications/log in Task 07" but never was. User chose "wire up customer notifications only" — the staff bell is a real feature (unread state, backing model, UI), not a bug fix, and stays deferred. **(2) 6 of 8 `NotificationEvent` values had complete SMS/WhatsApp/email templates but zero call sites** — every one only ever published an EventBridge event that was never wired into any routing rule (confirmed: even the routed ones don't reach the SQS-based notification pipeline at all — EventBridge and `shared/notifications.py` are two disconnected systems). Wired all 6 directly via `enqueue_notifications`, best-effort (try/except, never able to fail the underlying action): `payment_claim_confirmed`/`payment_claim_rejected` (new event types — **the exact reported bug**, `payment_release/service.py`), `payment_due` (`billing/service.py::handle_payment_due`, corrects a stale doc claim from 2026-07-18 that this already worked), `shipment_update` (`tracking/service.py`, the `in_transit`/`arrived`/`customs_clearance`/`fully_released` transitions), `damage_claim_update` (`claims/service.py`, both `start_inspection` and `resolve_claim`'s two internal paths), `packing_list_approved` (`packing_list/service.py`, shared by single and bulk approve), `tenant_onboarded`/`tenant_suspended` (`tenant_management/service.py`). Each new business function needed a `get_customer_contact()` repo helper — added identically per-service (billing/claims/packing_list/payment_release), matching this codebase's existing deliberate per-service `get_customer_by_user_id` duplication rather than centralizing into `shared/`. **Verified live end-to-end, reproducing the user's exact scenario**: submitted a real payment claim as a real customer, rejected it as staff, confirmed WhatsApp+email `payment_claim_rejected` messages were enqueued to the real (Moto) SQS queues, then drained them through `scripts/drain_notifications.py` — WhatsApp correctly fell back to SMS when Meta rejected the unregistered template, and all three attempts landed in `notification_log` with the correct amount/reason, visible on the staff Notification Log page. The two send failures (unregistered Meta template, no local SES/Africa's Talking credentials) are expected local-dev limitations, not code bugs. 714 backend tests pass (up from 690). Docs: `15_api_notifications.md` (full audit table), `13_api_payment_release.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `12_api_claims.md`, `08_api_packing_list.md`, `10_api_tracking.md`, `07_api_tenant_management.md`. |
| 7.64 ✅ | Every "calendar" filter tenant-wide made real, plus custom date ranges added to the two that already worked | Direct user follow-up to 7.63: "I want all the filters that use calendar to allow date range. Check all pages where we are using calendar filter. Include date filter to them." Audited every `icon="calendar"` button. `ClaimList.tsx` and `NotificationLog.tsx` had a static "Last 30 days" label with no `onClick` at all, and neither backend (`list_damage_claims`, `notification/repository.py::list_logs`) supported a date filter — added `created_after`/`created_before` to both. `TenantReports.tsx`'s "Last 3 months" button was the same dead pattern, and worse: the whole report always fetched **unscoped** billing/container/claims data regardless of what the label implied ("Total revenue" never actually meant "this period") — wired the date range into all three underlying list calls; the registered-customer count stays unscoped since it isn't a per-period metric. `ContainerList.tsx` and `BillingList.tsx` already had a working "last N days" preset but no way to pick an exact day or range — added a custom From/To pair to both, matching what `ActivityLog.tsx` already had. Extracted the preset+custom-date pairing logic (a custom date always overrides and clears the preset) into a new shared `frontend/src/hooks/useDateRangeFilter.ts`, since hand-rolling it a 4th/5th time crossed into real duplication — now used by all five pages, each still rendering its own `<DateRangeFilter>` + From/To inputs in its own filter-bar markup. Verified live against the real local stack: `created_after=2099-01-01` correctly returned 0 of 12 claims and 0 of 8 notification-log rows; `created_after=2020-01-01` returned the full count for both. Tests: `TestListClaimsDateRange`, `TestListLogsDateRangeRepository` + `TestNotificationLogHandlerDateRangeQueryParams` (backend); `ClaimList.test.tsx`/`ContainerList.test.tsx`/`BillingList.test.tsx` each +1 case, new `NotificationLog.test.tsx` (2 cases) and `TenantReports.test.tsx` (2 cases). 690 backend + 674 frontend tests pass. Docs: `12_api_claims.md`, `15_api_notifications.md`, `10_api_tracking.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.63 ✅ | Billing list's date-range and Filters buttons were dead UI, and a Postman-collection recovery incident | User asked to double-check "billing details filters and date ranges search and other filters are correctly doing what they should do." Found `nigeria/BillingList.tsx`'s "calendar" and "Filters" buttons rendered with no `onClick` at all, and neither `BillingListParams` nor the backend query-param parsing nor `list_billing_all()` supported a date range or multi-status filter — the same "looks like a filter, does nothing" shape as the Activity Log's original gaps.
