Fixed: `list_billing_all()` now supports `status` as `ANY(%(statuses)s)` (multi-select, same idiom as `tracking.list_containers`) and `created_after`/`created_before` against `billed_at` (not `created_at` — matches the column the existing sort already uses); `BillingList.tsx`'s calendar button now drives the shared `DateRangeFilter`, and its Filters button opens a real granular multi-select panel, following `china/ContainerList.tsx`'s established pattern exactly (`filtersWrap`/`toggleStatus`/`clearAllFilters`). Verified live against the real local stack: a 2-of-4-status filter and a future-dated `created_after` both returned exactly the expected rows (including correctly excluding a `billed_at IS NULL` record from `>=`, per SQL NULL semantics). **Incident, disclosed to the user directly:** while fixing an unrelated indentation mismatch in `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, ran `git checkout -- ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, which reverted that file to the repo's very first commit — discarding every uncommitted Postman-collection addition from this entire session (payment_claim routes, the Activity Log route, bank_transfer_config, this task's own filter widening). No local or Time Machine backup of the pre-checkout version existed. Application code, migrations, and tests were unaffected — only this manual-testing artifact. Reconstructed the missing entries by reading the actual current route handlers/models (source of truth, unaffected by the checkout) rather than from memory: all 6 payment-claim routes, `GET /v1/tenant/activity` with its full filter set, and the widened billing list query params. Tests: `TestListBillingAllRepositoryFilters` (5 cases) + `TestBillingListHandlerQueryParams` (3 cases) added to `tests/unit/test_billing.py`; `BillingList.test.tsx` gained 3 cases. 685 backend + 667 frontend tests pass. Docs: `11_api_billing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.62 ✅ | Activity Log UX + information-boundary fixes: misaligned search box, "Details" opening in the wrong place, impersonation leaking into a tenant's own view, and a systemic audit-write failure found along the way | Third same-day round of direct feedback. (1) Search box misaligned against the filter dropdowns — root cause was mixing the `Field` component's own label/margin convention with plain `<select>` elements; replaced with a plain input sharing one style, explicit height matching `Button`'s default so every control (including the date-range button) sits on the same baseline. (2) "Details" opened as a block after the entire table, so clicking any row but the last meant scrolling past the rest of the list; replaced with a `Modal` that always opens in the same place. (3) Reported directly: "impersonation... is irrelevant" for a tenant admin's own filter, and more broadly "platform admin does not see sensitive information of a tenant e.g financial information and logs." Traced `start_impersonation`/`end_impersonation` writing into the **tenant's own** `audit_log` — moved to `repository.insert_platform_audit()` (the same mechanism every other platform-level action already uses, visible only via `PlatformAuditLog.tsx`), plus a hard, unconditional `NOT IN ('impersonation_session')` clause in `list_tenant_activity()` so a tenant admin cannot see one even by asking for it by name. **Investigating that surfaced the day's largest finding:** a grep across every `log_audit()` call site for action values outside the CHECK-constrained set (`CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`STATUS_CHANGE`) found **20** across 7 services — `EXPORT`, `CANCEL`, `RETRY`, `BULK_APPROVE`, `IMPERSONATION_START/END`, and 15 more — that had been silently failing to write since each was authored. Fixed with migration 045 (drops the constraint, widens to VARCHAR(100), matching the already-unconstrained `platform_audit_log`) — a permanent fix, not a backfill of today's 20. (4) Added custom From/To date inputs alongside the day-preset filter, an explicit date always overriding and clearing the preset. **The broader financial-visibility-during-impersonation policy question was flagged back to the user, not assumed** — blocking Billing/Refunds/Claims outright during impersonation is a real functional change to existing support tooling that needs their call on scope. Verified live: filter bar aligned (screenshot-confirmed), modal opens centered regardless of row, custom range correctly narrows results, impersonation absent from both dropdowns, and the constraint fix confirmed by reproducing the exact failure then confirming the fix writes a real row. 677 backend + 664 frontend tests pass. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `02_shared_utilities.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.61 ✅ | Activity Log's filtering was superficial — one dropdown, no search, no sort, no categorization | Same-day follow-up to 7.60, reported directly by the user: "activity logs lacks fully functional filtering, sorting and search. we need it to be properly categorized for early [easy] filtering." `GET /v1/tenant/activity` gained a real filter set, all combinable with `AND`: `entity_type` now accepts a comma-separated list via `= ANY(...)` (same contract as `GET /v1/containers`'s multi-status filter) — this is what makes categorization real, since the frontend groups every `entity_type` into six categories (Money, Goods & shipments, Customers, Staff & access, Settings, System) and sends every type in a category as one request; `action` (CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE/STATUS_CHANGE); `q` — matches the actor's name **or** the record's id, so pasting a `billing_id`/`payment_claim.id` finds every action ever taken on that one specific record; `created_after`/`created_before` (the shared `DateRangeFilter` component, 7/30/90-day presets); `sort=asc|desc` (reuses `build_paginated_query`'s existing `sort_direction` support — already flips the cursor comparison operator correctly, so ascending pagination needed no new code). Frontend: Category narrows Record-type's options and clears a selection that no longer applies when switched; a debounced search box; a sort-order select; "Clear filters" resets everything together. Verified live: Category→"Money" returned exactly the 7 money-related types and narrowed Record-type to match; search isolated one actor's entries; "Oldest first" correctly flipped the first visible row. Tests: `TestListTenantActivityFiltersRepository` (asserts the real SQL each filter produces, including that combining filters is always `AND`, never a widening `OR`) + expanded `TestTenantActivityFeed` (backend), rewritten `ActivityLog.test.tsx` (10 cases, frontend). 672 backend + 661 frontend tests pass. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.61 ✅ | Operator-provisioned users (tenant admin + staff) could never log in — created in the IdP with a random password and no welcome email | User feedback: onboarding a tenant sent no link to set a password, and "ensure everyone/role can login." Customers self-registering set their own password (`create_user_with_password`); only operator-provisioned users were stranded — created with a random IdP password and no email, so they could authenticate but never had a usable session (surfaced as `JWT is missing required claims`). Built a ShipX-branded **account-activation** flow (option C, best-for-business — not the "use Forgot password" workaround): `tenant_user_activation` table (migration 055, RLS, single-use 48h SHA-256-hashed token, `shared/activation.py`, mirrors `customer_invite`); `IdentityProvider.set_password` (Entra via the Graph provisioning app, `forceChange=false`; Cognito `admin_set_user_password Permanent`); `service.issue_activation` (best-effort, called from `create_tenant` for the admin and `create_user` for all staff/roles) emails an `account_activation` notification with a `{APP_BASE_URL}/activate/{token}` CTA link; public `GET`/`POST /v1/activate/{token}` set the chosen password and consume the token; frontend `/activate/:token` (`ActivateScreen.tsx`) + `activationApi`. Email renderer now renders `*_link` template data as a real button (improves the customer invite too). This gives every role a first-login path, so all roles can sign in. Tests: `test_activation.py` (new), `test_entra_identity_provider.py::set_password`, existing suites updated — 1343 backend + 407 frontend (auth/shell/api) pass, typecheck + lint clean. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `05a_database_schema_core.md`, `frontend/04_auth_and_session.md`. |
| 7.60 ✅ | No full audit-trail page for tenant admins, and several money/goods writes had no audit log at all — one silently, completely broken | User feedback: "we need to ensure that we have log trail for everything, since it has to do with people's goods and money, so that the tenant admin can see every activity going on." Two problems. **(1) No page existed** — only an 8-row dashboard widget, no filtering, no way to see what changed; platform admins already had a full audit-log page, tenant admins didn't. Added `tenant-admin/ActivityLog.tsx` (`/admin/activity-log`): record-type filter, pagination, expandable before/after diff — mirrors the platform page. Backend `GET /v1/tenant/activity` gained `?entity_type=` and now returns `before_state`/`after_state` (previously excluded as a "lean projection"). **(2) Auditing actual log coverage across every service** (dispatched as a background investigation) found real gaps: `billing.handle_payment_due` (creates the invoice — zero trail), `billing.reject_dispute` (every sibling transition logs; reject didn't), `claims`' missing/seized-item report+resolve+the refund each can trigger (four functions, zero audit calls), both excel bulk-importers (containers/customers/packing lists created with zero trail — now one summary entry per import, not one per row), and `tenant_management.deactivate_charge_type`. All now log. **The most serious find:** `shared.audit.log_audit()` never raises on failure (by design), and two system-triggered call sites passed a non-UUID placeholder (`actor_id="gateway"`/`"system"`) into a `UUID` column — every webhook-confirmed payment had **silently never been audit-logged**, ever, with nothing surfacing it. Reproduced the exact failure live against the real DB, fixed by passing `None` (a real, already-supported value) instead of a placeholder string. Verified live: `GET /v1/tenant/activity?entity_type=payment_claim` returned this session's real claim history with correct before/after states; the new page rendered it correctly in a real browser. 660 backend + 655 frontend tests pass. Docs: `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `02_shared_utilities.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `12_api_claims.md`, `13_api_payment_release.md`, `19_excel_import_export.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.59 ✅ | Payment claims had no staff-side UI at all, and the customer side allowed a double payment while one was pending | Same-day follow-up to 7.58: the user started actually using the "I've made this payment" feature and asked how staff confirm it — the backend confirm/reject endpoints existed but no page could reach them, so nothing could actually be confirmed. Added a "Payment claims" table to `nigeria/BillingDetail.tsx` for `nigeria_office_staff`/`tenant_admin`: amount, date, a "View evidence" button (new `GET /v1/payments/claims/{id}/evidence-url` presigned S3 GET — staff need to see the actual receipt, not trust a filename), status, Confirm/Reject for pending ones. While explaining this the user separately caught a real gap themselves: "Pay online" stayed fully clickable and the page showed no sign a payment was in flight after submitting a claim — a genuine double-payment risk. Fixed: a pending claim now replaces the whole "Pay now" section (both Pay online and bank transfer) with a single "awaiting confirmation" banner; a rejected claim surfaces its reason above the restored options instead of buried in an accordion; `MyShipmentDetail.tsx`'s compact CTA got the matching fix. **Bug caught by a test, not by eye:** the rejection-reason banner was rendering twice (new top-level one + the pre-existing panel's own, which auto-expands whenever there's claim history) — `findByText` failed with "found multiple elements." Verified live against the user's own real pending claims (submitted through the UI while testing the feature themselves) — real evidence in S3, real rows, correct staff visibility. 651 backend + 651 frontend tests pass. Docs: `13_api_payment_release.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`. |
| 7.58 ✅ | Billing money-clarity pass ("misc fees" ambiguity + a broader check) plus a missing manual-payment confirmation step | User feedback: "misc fees" in the billing breakdown doesn't say what it's for, and — since it's money — asked for a wider check of the same surface. That turned up more than wording: `MyBillingDetail.tsx`'s "Invoice breakdown" silently omitted misc fees/storage fees/carryover/adjustments from the customer's own bill entirely (only CBM/Rate/Exchange rate/Total due showed, so `total_due_ngn` didn't reconcile with anything visible); the customer's downloadable PDF/CSV invoice (`_BILLING_COLUMNS`) was a bare 7 columns with **zero** breakdown, the one actually stripped-down path among the three export formats; and the "Manual bank transfer" panel hardcoded one tenant's bank account for every tenant's customers — a real money-misdirection risk, not just ambiguity. Fixed all three: itemized `billing.misc_fees[]` by real description everywhere (customer breakdown, customer shipment-detail summary, staff breakdown — never a lump "Misc fees" line again), widened `_BILLING_COLUMNS` to full parity with the xlsx export plus a new itemized-fee string column, and added a real per-tenant `bank_transfer_config` (new tenant-config key, admin settings card, `GET /v1/tenant/config` widened to `CUSTOMER`). Mid-session the user separately asked for a manual-transfer confirmation step: added `payment_claim` (migration 044) — customer's "I've made this payment" submission with **mandatory** evidence upload (rejected at the model level without it), reviewed by staff (`confirm`/`reject`), where confirming calls the *existing* `confirm_payment()` rather than creating a second ledger. **Bug found live, not by unit tests:** the first version of the claim INSERT had no `ON CONFLICT` clause, so a second pending claim 500'd instead of the intended clean 422 — fixed with `ON CONFLICT (billing_id) WHERE status='pending' DO NOTHING`, verified live. Verified end-to-end through a real browser session (not just curl): real tenant bank details rendered, evidence upload gated the submit button, and the resulting "pending review" banner showed the correct amount/date/filename; separately confirmed the claim via the API and verified `payment_record`/`billing.status` updated correctly, and downloaded the real invoice PDF via `pdftotext` to confirm the full breakdown now renders. 647 backend + 643 frontend tests pass. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `13_api_payment_release.md`, `19_excel_import_export.md`, `05b_database_schema_extended.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.57 ✅ | A damage claim could be filed against cargo that hadn't arrived yet | Reported directly by the user. Neither the backend (`file_claim()`) nor the frontend (`MyClaimsNew.tsx`'s free-text container field) checked container arrival status — a customer could file, and staff would then investigate, a claim against a container still `loading` or at sea. Fixed at both layers: `file_claim()` now rejects (`422 CONTAINER_NOT_ARRIVED`) unless the container's status is `arrived`/`customs_clearance`/`cleared`/`at_warehouse`/`fully_released`; the frontend replaced the free-text field with a dropdown populated from the customer's own shipments, filtered to arrived ones only, so a non-arrived shipment is never offered rather than being submitted and rejected after the fact. Verified live: a `loading` container correctly 422s, an `at_warehouse` one still succeeds. Tests: parametrized over all pre-arrival and all arrived-or-later statuses (backend), plus a new `MyClaimsNew.test.tsx`. Docs: `12_api_claims.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.56 ✅ | Customer tracking UI showed only the most recent shipment — every other active shipment was invisible | Found live testing the customer-invite login flow with a real account holding 3 simultaneously active shipments: `MyShipments.tsx` computed the full `activeShipments` list correctly but then only ever rendered `activeShipments[0]` as a single feature card — every other in-transit shipment was silently dropped from the page (not even listed in a table; "Past shipments" only covers delivered/cancelled). Fixed by rendering the same rich tracking card once per active shipment in a stack instead of indexing into the array; heading/greeting now reflect the real count ("Active shipments (3)"). Verified live via screenshot against a real 3-shipment account. Tests: `MyShipments.test.tsx` (new), covering both the plural and singular heading cases. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.52 ✅ | Manifest-imported customers had no way to log in — plus tenant-scoped self-signup was genuinely broken | 68 customers from an STL409 import carried only name + phone (all a manifest gives), with no path to a portal account. Built an expiring (48h), single-use account-creation invite: `shared/invites.py` (random `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`, stored only as a SHA-256 hash — never a JWT, since single-use revocation needs a server-side row regardless), `customer_invite` table (migration 042, RLS-enabled), `POST /v1/customers/{id}/invite` (staff issue/resend — resend deletes the prior unconsumed row first), `GET /v1/invites/{token}` + `POST /v1/invites/{token}/accept` (public claim). Manifest import auto-issues an invite for every customer it *creates* (never matched ones), best-effort, same posture as `record_usage`. **Investigating turned up `self_register_customer` had never actually worked**: it wrote the Cognito sub straight into `customer.user_id`, a column with `FOREIGN KEY REFERENCES tenant_user(id)` — verified against the live schema, that write could never succeed. Fixed by creating the missing `tenant_user` row and linking *its* id, same fix applied to `accept_customer_invite`'s Cognito-login path. Also fixed tenant resolution: self-register took the tenant from the `Host` header, unreachable in practice since the frontend calls a shared `VITE_API_BASE_URL` — now `POST /v1/register/{slug}` resolves from an explicit path param (`POST /v1/register` kept, body-resolved, for backward compatibility). **Phone normalisation prerequisite**: moved `_normalize_phone` out of `services/excel/service.py` into `shared/phone.py`, canonicalising to E.164 via the tenant's destination-country dial code so `08012345678` and `+2348012345678` converge on one customer; applied to every customer write path (tenant_management previously stored phones verbatim). Migration 042 adds `UNIQUE INDEX ... ON customer(tenant_id, phone) WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`, with a pre-flight check that **reports duplicates and aborts** rather than merging records — verified live by inserting a deliberate duplicate pair pre-migration and confirming the guard catches it. **Two bugs caught only by live verification, invisible to the mocked unit suite**: (1) both new `create_tenant_user` call sites omitted the `tenant_id` key its SQL requires (`%(tenant_id)s`) — a bare `KeyError` at the DB layer that a mocked `repo.create_tenant_user` never exercises; (2) the new unique index collided by name with migration 002's pre-existing plain `idx_customer_tenant_phone` — renamed to `idx_customer_tenant_phone_unique`. **RLS gap found live**: `customer_invite`'s policy (copied from the established pattern in migrations 022/024/025/041) checked `current_setting('app.tenant_id', ...)`, but `shared/db.py` only ever sets `app.current_tenant_id` — the GUC name never matched, and combined with local dev's DB connection owning the table (RLS is bypassed for table owners), cross-tenant reads were not actually blocked. Fixed for this table's policy (`app.current_tenant_id`); confirmed with a throwaway non-owner role that it now correctly returns 0 rows cross-tenant and the real row count same-tenant. **The other four migrations carry the same latent GUC-name bug — flagged, not fixed (out of scope here, no evidence it's been exploited since local dev's owner-role connection masks it everywhere).** Docker Desktop could not be started in this environment (`docker ps` hung indefinitely even after launching it); live verification ran against a native Homebrew PostgreSQL already listening on :5432 and a natively started `moto_server` (:5100, same package the Docker image wraps) after bootstrapping fresh Cognito/S3/SQS resources. **Verified live end-to-end**: re-imported a synthetic STL409-shaped manifest, 3 invites issued (1 row matched an existing customer and correctly got no invite), confirmed in the SQS queue (`notification_log` itself only populates via the Lambda's SQS consumer, which local dev has no persistent process for — a pre-existing local-dev limitation, not new). Claimed an invite via the real endpoint: `tenant_user` created, `customer.user_id` linked, `consumed_at` set. **Logged in as that customer in the browser and confirmed they can see their shipment's transit status** — screenshot shows `PL-20260901-007` / container `STL377` at the Received stage, matching the DB exactly. Reused the same token → 404. Expired one by moving `expires_at` back → 404 with the friendly "contact your shipping company" page (screenshot confirmed, not a raw error). Resent → old token dead (404), new one resolved and rendered the claim page correctly. Self-signup via `/register/{slug}` end to end through the actual browser UI, confirming the FK bug is gone (`customer.user_id` → real `tenant_user.id` → real `cognito_sub`, all equal by construction). **A third bug found only by browser verification**: `scripts/local_server.py`'s dev-only JWT-claims shim resolved a real Cognito-login session to the wrong customer — Moto's ID token carries no `email` claim, so the shim's email-lookup fell through to a role-based fallback that arbitrarily picked the *first*-created customer for that tenant, silently showing one customer's shipments under another's session. Fixed by trying the token's `sub` directly against `tenant_user.id` first (true by construction for every self-registered/invite-accepted account) before the email/role fallback. Dev-only file; not a production code path. **Flagged, not fixed**: `MyShipments.tsx` calls `billingApi.listForCustomer(session.userId)` using the Cognito sub as a path param where the endpoint expects a customer id, 403/500ing on every customer-portal billing widget load — pre-existing, unrelated to this feature, caught incidentally during live verification. Tests: `test_phone.py`, `test_invites.py` (new), `TestSelfRegisterCustomer`/`TestIssueCustomerInvite`/`TestAcceptCustomerInvite` (the FK regression test explicitly asserts `customer.user_id` resolves to a real `tenant_user`), `TestCreateUserWithPassword` (moto-backed), `TestProcessManifestImport` extended — 576 backend + 632 frontend tests pass, typecheck clean. Docs: `05a_database_schema_core.md`, `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `15_api_notifications.md`, `19_excel_import_export.md`, `04c_terraform_api_gateway.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`. |
| 7.55 ✅ | WhatsApp template parameters were positional on dict order — an added key broke a template, a reordered key sent the wrong message | Preparing to create the Meta templates exposed the coupling: `build_wa_components` sent `template_data.values()` in **dict insertion order**, and Meta matches template parameters positionally. So adding a key to any `template_data` dict silently broke that event's WhatsApp send (`132000 number of parameters does not match`) while SMS and email kept working — a one-channel failure — and reordering keys silently swapped values between placeholders, delivering a wrong message to a customer with no error at all. Anything past the 5th value was dropped unannounced. Replaced with `WA_TEMPLATE_PARAMS`, which names the ordered `template_data` keys per event, so the parameter count is a property of the template rather than of the caller; a missing key becomes blank to preserve the count (Meta rejects a short message outright) and undeclared events keep the old behaviour. Tests: declared order beats insertion order, an extra key does not change the count, a missing key blanks rather than shortens, plus two guards — every `WA_TEMPLATE_MAP` entry must declare parameters, and none may exceed Meta's 5-parameter budget, so a new event cannot silently regress to ordering. 601 backend tests pass. Also corrected an earlier claim of mine: the Meta access token is **not** a 24-hour token — `debug_token` shows type USER with ~1–2h life (this one had 1.3h remaining), which is why the first one died mid-session; a System User token is what production needs. Docs: new `whatsapp_templates.md` (all ten templates with code-derived parameter counts, body text, and the create/change procedure), `15_api_notifications.md`. |
| 7.54 ✅ | Meta WhatsApp wired up live — and the template language was hardcoded, making Meta's own templates and all Chinese templates unusable | Configured Meta Cloud API against the user's test number and sent a real WhatsApp end to end (`wamid.HBgNMjM0ODE2OTc2MDI1Mx…`). Three things had to be fixed to get there. **(1) Hardcoded template language:** the payload always sent `"language": {"code": "en"}`, but Meta approves templates *per language* and the request must match — Meta's own `hello_world` (registered `en_US`) returned `132001 Template name does not exist in the translation`, and `WA_TEMPLATE_MAP_ZH` was **unreachable entirely** since Chinese templates were always requested in English. Now derived from locale via `wa_language_code()` (`en→en_US`, `zh→zh_CN`), overridable with `META_WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_LANG`. **(2) `.env.local` was tracked in git** — adding the Meta token would have committed a live credential, violating the repo's own "no secrets committed" SSDLC check. Untracked it and added `.env.*` to `.gitignore` while keeping `.env.example` tracked; the already-committed version held only local placeholders, so nothing real had leaked. **(3) macOS TLS:** python.org builds ship without root certs, so the Graph API call failed `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`; fixed by pointing `SSL_CERT_FILE` at certifi rather than weakening verification in application code. Also added a **second, provider-level recipient guard** in `send_whatsapp`/`send_sms`/`send_email` — the existing producer-level guard only covers messages queued now, so a replayed queue item or a direct call could still have reached a real customer, and this database holds genuine numbers imported from client manifests. **Verified against the live database**: every real customer number blocked, only the allowlisted number permitted, with no network call attempted for blocked recipients. Tests: 3 new language cases (including asserting the language code in the actual payload and that Meta's message id is returned rather than discarded) plus 3 provider-guard cases — 595 backend tests pass. Docs: `15_api_notifications.md`. |
| 7.53 ✅ | WhatsApp-first notifications with SMS fallback — plus WhatsApp delivery status never worked | Requested: most customers are reached on WhatsApp, so send there first and only fall back to SMS on failure. Previously every enabled channel fanned out in parallel, so a customer got an SMS *and* a WhatsApp for the same event — double cost, no benefit. Producer now queues `whatsapp`+`email` when WhatsApp can be attempted (phone present, channel enabled, queue configured) and `sms`+`email` only when it cannot; email is deliberately untouched, being a different medium rather than a worse SMS. Fallback (`trigger_sms_fallback`) covers both real failure modes — Meta rejecting the send (consumer exception path) and Meta accepting then reporting `failed` later (delivery-status webhook) — and is **idempotent** via the existing `retry_of` column, since both paths can fire for one message and would otherwise text the customer twice. The phone is read back from the `customer` record because `notification_log` stores only a masked contact by design, and the non-production recipient guard (7.52) applies to fallbacks too. **Ground-truth bug found while building it:** `send_whatsapp` returned `True` and discarded Meta's message id, so `log_sent` wrote an empty `provider_message_id` — the column the delivery-status webhook matches on. Every `delivered`/`read`/`failed` callback had therefore been silently matching nothing: WhatsApp delivery status was never recorded, and the undelivered-fallback path could not have worked at all. Now returned and stored. Tests: `test_whatsapp_first.py` (7 cases — routing prefers WhatsApp, falls back when WhatsApp is unconfigured or tenant-muted, fallback queues SMS with `retry_of` set, does not double-send when both paths fire, respects the non-prod guard, handles a customer with no phone). 589 backend tests pass. **Provider recommendation recorded** in `15_api_notifications.md`: stay on Meta Cloud API direct for WhatsApp (no BSP margin on the primary channel; the codebase is already Meta-shaped) and Africa's Talking for now-low-volume SMS fallback; Termii is the Nigeria-specific alternative if fallback deliverability disappoints; Twilio ruled out on Nigerian SMS cost. |
| 7.51 ✅ | Bulk approve skipped every side effect of approval — no manifest, no tracking records | Surfaced by a user report that a container with 68 approved packing lists could not be sealed ("Container must have a manifest"). Root cause: `approve_packing_list` creates the container's manifest, links the list to it, recalculates totals, creates a `shipment_tracking` row per tracking code and publishes `PackingListApproved` — while `bulk_approve_packing_lists` only called `bulk_set_status`. Confirmed on real data: 68 approved lists, **0 linked to a manifest, 0 shipment_tracking rows**. The unsealable container was the visible symptom; the serious one was silent — **customers could not track cargo the system considered fully approved**, and later container transitions had no tracking rows to bulk-update. The two paths drifted because the side effects were inline in the single-item function, so the fix extracts `_apply_approval_side_effects()` and calls it from both. Repaired the live data by re-running the fixed bulk approve over the existing container: 1 manifest (68 customers, 81 rows, 72.55 CBM), 68/68 linked, 68 tracking rows. **Verified live end-to-end**: sealing was correctly refused before verification ("The manifest must be verified…"), the manifest verified, then the container sealed. Tests: `TestBulkApproveSideEffects` asserting manifest create/link/recalc/tracking/event counts, with a stateful manifest mock so the second list finds the manifest the first created (exactly as against a real DB) rather than asserting a duplicate-creating behaviour. 542 backend tests pass. |
| 7.50 ✅ | A full page of results read as the complete result — "50 packing lists" for a container holding 68 | Reported live: searching `STL409` showed 50 rows, but the "Pending approval" filter revealed more under the same container. Not data loss — 50 approved + 18 pending = 68, and the endpoint pages at 50. The failure was that nothing said so: `meta.pagination.total_count` was never populated (the `paginated()` helper accepted it, no packing-list route passed it), and "Load more" sat below 50 rows of scroll, so a full page looked like the whole answer and the status filter then looked inconsistent with it. Backend: new `repository.count_packing_lists`, sharing its predicate with `list_packing_lists` through an extracted `_packing_list_predicates` so the count can never describe a different filter than the rows — and only run when `has_more`, since otherwise the page length is the total. Threaded through service (now returning a 4-tuple) and both list routes. Frontend: `PackingListList` states "Showing 50 of 68 packing lists" and the button reads "Load more · 18 remaining"; a complete page claims no total and renders no button. **Verified live in the browser**: search STL409 → "Showing 50 of 68", click Load more → "Showing 68", 68 rows. Tests: 2 new `PackingListList` cases (partial page states the total, complete page does not), existing handler tests updated for the 4-tuple — 541 backend + 632 frontend pass, typecheck clean. |
| 7.49 ✅ | Packing-list search did nothing — `q` never left the handler | Reported live: searching packing lists by customer name returned everything. Confirmed before fixing by issuing a real name and a nonsense string, both returning 76 rows. Two defects on one feature: **(1)** `services/packing_list/handler.py` built its `filters` dict from `status`/`container_id`/`customer_id`/`created_after`/`created_before` and never read `q` — the frontend had always sent it and the repository SQL had always supported it, so the predicate was simply never applied; the container-scoped sub-route (`_container_packing_lists`) had the same omission. **(2)** the search box's placeholder promises "Reference, customer name, container…" but the SQL matched only `cu.name` and `c.container_number`, so a PL reference would still have missed once the filter was wired. Both fixed. **Verified live**: `q=Ngozi` → 3, `q=ZZZ_NO_MATCH` → 0, `q=STL409` → 68, an exact reference → 1, and `q=CYNTHIA` scoped to a container → 2; in the browser the list narrows from 50 rows to 2. Tests: `TestPackingListSearchFilter` (3 cases — handler passes `q` on both routes, and the SQL matches all three promised fields), following `test_tracking.py`'s handler-level pattern of patching `get_tenant_context`. 541 backend + 630 frontend pass. **Pattern flagged:** this is the third search bug of the same shape (cf. row 7.36's container `q` filter, which was malformed SQL) — wired at the UI and the SQL but broken in the layer between; the remaining list endpoints with search boxes are worth an audit. Docs: `08_api_packing_list.md`. |
| 7.48 ✅ | Bales and cartons were indistinguishable in the UI — and every count was rows, not packages | User asked to show which items are bales vs cartons. Investigating found the display gap sat on top of a counting bug: since migration 040 made a manifest row a *bulk quantity* ("16BAGS" is one row), `carton_count = COUNT(*)` stopped being a package count, and every table still labelled it "Cartons". A list of 2 bales + 16 bags rendered as "3 cartons"; container STL409 showed "Total cartons 81" while physically holding **437 packages**. Backend: the packing-list list query now also returns `package_count` (sums bulk quantities) and `package_mix` (packages keyed by unit), aggregated **two-level** — per `(packing_list_id, unit)` first, then folded — because a single-level `jsonb_object_agg` collapses duplicate keys and would report two `bale` rows as one. Rows with no unit group as `unspecified` so they still count. `carton_count` retained unchanged (still row count) to avoid disturbing existing consumers. Frontend: new `formatPackageMix`/`formatPackageUnit`/`dominantPackageUnit` in `packingListRules.ts`; **Packages** column replaces "Cartons" on `PackingListList` and both `ContainerDetail` tables; item-level `PackageCell` on `PackingListDetail` shows "16 bags" or "1 × BIG BALE · No. 2" (verbatim `bale_type`/`bale_no`, plus `quantity_raw` for compound cells); container tile becomes **Total packages** with the mix as sub-label; hardcoded "Carton items"/"1 cartons" copy replaced with the derived mix. **Two bugs I introduced and caught by looking at the rendered page:** (1) the SQL comment contained `{"bale": 2}` inside an f-string, so Python read the braces as a format specifier → 500 on every list call; (2) `SUM()` over numeric serialises as a **string** where `COUNT()` returned a JSON number, so the frontend concatenated counts — the container total rendered as `01113131641111...` and per-customer packages as `019`/`054`. Fixed with `::int` casts in SQL rather than coercion scattered through TS. **Verified live in the browser** against the real STL409 import: Total packages **437** = "342 cartons · 76 bags · 18 bales · 1 pallet", reconciling exactly with the source manifest's declared `CTNS/PKGS: 419` (CTN sheet) plus 18 bales (BALES sheet); per-customer and per-item cells all name their unit. Tests: 8 new cases across `packingListRules.test.ts`, updated `ContainerDetail`/`PackingListDetail` assertions — 538 backend + 630 frontend pass, typecheck and lint clean. **Flagged, not changed:** `billing.total_cartons` and per-carton storage-fee accrual still count rows, so the carton figure on invoices and storage charges under-count for bulk rows. Billing charges on CBM so invoice *amounts* are unaffected, but this moves money and needs an explicit decision — documented in `08_api_packing_list.md`. |
| 7.47 ✅ | Bulk-action failures were unreadable: 50 rows of `<uuid> self_approval_forbidden` | User feedback after 7.46: "as a developer I understand the error, as a user it is not understandable — and should we be having errors in line items like this?" Both fair. **Prevent first:** `canApproveOrReject`/`isSelfApproval` already existed in `api/packingListRules.ts` but nothing consulted them before sending the request. The bulk bar now counts what the user can actually approve — button reads "Bulk approve · N" from that count, disables at zero, and states the reason inline before any click ("you created all of these, so someone else must approve them"). **Then explain:** new `explainBulkFailure()` maps each reason code to a plain-language title + next step, and `groupBulkFailures()` collapses failures sharing a cause, so 50 identical failures render as **one** block with a count instead of 50 rows; affected lists show as `reference · customer` chips resolved from already-loaded page data, never UUIDs. Unknown codes still surface verbatim rather than being swallowed. **Ground-truth gap found while verifying in the browser:** the prevention appeared to do nothing locally because `LocalAuthProvider`'s `ROLE_USER_IDS` used placeholder slugs (`local-china-staff`) while production `session.userId` is the Cognito sub (= `tenant_user.id` for seeded users) — so *every* UI ownership check comparing session user to `created_by` silently never matched under the dev shim, hiding exactly the class of bug it exists to catch. Shim now uses the real fixed seed UUIDs. **Verified live in the browser:** as the importer, "Bulk approve · 0" disabled with the inline warning; as tenant_admin, "Bulk approve · 50" enabled; the grouped failure panel renders one explained block with reference·customer chips. Tests: `explainBulkFailure`/`groupBulkFailures` (6 cases), a panel test asserting no code or UUID leaks to screen, a prevention test, plus updated `LocalAuthProvider` header assertions — 538 backend + 625 frontend pass, typecheck and lint clean. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`. |
| 7.46 ✅ | Bulk approve returned `wrong_status:draft` for every imported packing list — imported cargo could not be approved at all | Reported live after a manifest import. Two independent walls. **(1)** The state machine is `draft -> pending_approval -> approved`; bulk approve only accepts `pending_approval`, but **no bulk submit existed** — `submit` was single-item only, so 68 imported lists required 68 individual submits. Added `POST /v1/packing-lists/bulk-submit` (mirrors bulk-approve/reject) *and* changed imports to land in `pending_approval` rather than `draft`, since `draft` means "still being typed up" and an imported file is complete on arrival. **(2)** Even once submitted, the importer could not approve their own import (`bulk_approve` rejects `created_by == actor`). Kept deliberately after confirming with the user — imported cargo drives billing, so a second person signs it off; the working flow is staff imports → tenant_admin (or another china staff) bulk approves. **Latent data-corruption bug found and fixed while building this:** `repository.bulk_set_status` branched `if approved ... else: rejected`, silently coercing *any* other status to `rejected` — the first `bulk_set_status(..., "pending_approval", ...)` call marked five real packing lists **rejected** instead of submitted. Caught immediately because the live check showed `rejected=5` where `pending_approval=5` was expected; the five rows were repaired. It now dispatches explicitly on the three supported statuses and raises `ValueError` on anything else rather than guessing — with a regression test pinning that. **Verified live end-to-end**: bulk submit moved 5 drafts to `pending_approval` (not rejected); approve as the importer correctly returned `self_approval_forbidden`; approve as tenant_admin succeeded (5 approved); and a fresh re-import of the real STL409 file landed all 68 lists directly in `pending_approval`. Frontend: "Submit for approval · N" added to the bulk action bar. Tests: `TestBulkSubmit` (2), `TestBulkSetStatusGuards` (1), plus a `PackingListList.test.tsx` case — 538 backend + 618 frontend pass, typecheck and lint clean. Docs: `08_api_packing_list.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`. |
| 7.45 ✅ | Customer contact missing from every operational table + container cargo totals only counted page one | The imported manifest carries a phone for every row (the source document pairs **NAME** with **CONTACT**, because staff ring the customer when cargo lands), but `customer_phone` was selected by no query and shown by no table — the number was only reachable by clicking through to the customer record. Added `c.phone AS customer_phone` to `services/manifest/repository.py::get_customer_summaries` (+ `GROUP BY`) and to both packing-list queries in `services/packing_list/repository.py`; the `customer` joins already existed. Surfaced as a **Contact** column on Container→Overview customers, Container→Packing lists, and Manifest→customers. Removed the now-duplicated phone from the customer `UserCell` subtitle (which had previously duplicated the carton count instead). **Ground-truth bug found while verifying in the browser:** `ContainerDetail` fetched packing lists with no paging (endpoint default 50) but aggregated that single page as the whole container — so STL409 (68 packing lists, 81 cartons, **72.55 CBM**) displayed **50 customers, 55 cartons, 35.77 CBM**, and the tab header read "50 list(s)". Real manifests routinely exceed 50 customers, so this silently misreported cargo on exactly the containers that matter most. Fixed with `fetchAllPackingLists()` following `next_cursor` to the end. **Verified live in a browser** against the real imported STL409 data: Contact column populated with genuine numbers, and the cargo summary now reads 68 / 81 / 72.55 — matching the source document's own declared totals. Tests: contact-column assertions in `ManifestDetail.test.tsx` and `ContainerDetail.test.tsx`, plus a two-page pagination regression test; 617 frontend + 535 backend tests pass, typecheck and lint clean. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `08_api_packing_list.md`, `09_api_manifest.md`. |
| 7.44 ✅ | Frontend for manifest import — the backend importer (7.41/7.43) had no UI at all | Extended `pages/china/ImportPackingLists.tsx` into a two-mode wizard rather than adding a second page: **Forwarder manifest** (default — `POST /v1/manifests/import`, container read from the file's `PARTIAL NO`, no container picker shown, customers auto-created) vs **ShipX packing-list template** (the existing `POST /v1/packing-lists/import` flow, unchanged). One page keeps the shared drag/drop, stepper and results table rather than duplicating them. Results are mode-aware: manifest imports split into row outcomes (`Rows read`/`Items added`/`Already imported`/`Skipped`) and a separate "Created for you" group (`Containers`/`Customers`/`Packing lists`), with an explicit "Nothing new to add" banner when a re-import is fully duplicate so idempotency doesn't read as failure, and a **Sheet** column on skipped rows since real exports span several worksheets. New `excelApi.importManifest` + `ManifestImportResult` in `api/excel.ts`. **Ground-truth gap found:** the import wizard had a route but **no nav entry** — reachable only via a small button buried inside *Create packing list* — so the primary data-entry path was essentially hidden; added to `china_office_staff`'s "Packing lists" nav group. **Bug caught only by looking at a browser screenshot:** curly quotes/em dashes written as `\uXXXX` escapes render **literally** in JSX text nodes (`\u2019` visible on screen) — invisible to typecheck, lint and all 614 tests. **Verified live in a real browser** (Playwright, local stack, the user's genuine `STL409.xlsx`): manifest mode default with the container picker correctly absent → upload → confirm → **81 rows read, 81 items added, 0 errors, 1 container + 68 customers + 68 packing lists created**; `psql` confirms the stored data still matches the source document's own declared totals (`SUM(cbm) = 72.55`, carton qty `= 419`); an immediate re-import through the same UI showed **0 added / 81 already imported** with the duplicate banner, and created zero extra customers (matched, not duplicated). Tests: 5 new manifest cases in `ImportPackingLists.test.tsx` (mode default + no picker, Next enabled without a container, manifest endpoint used and packing-list endpoint not called, duplicate-only banner, sheet column) — 614 frontend tests pass, typecheck and lint clean. Docs: `frontend/07_domain_modules.md` §7.2b. |
| 7.43 ✅ | Manifest importer failed completely on the user's first real export — 0 of 87 rows | The user supplied two genuine exports from their forwarder's platform (`STL409.xlsx`, plus a flat search-table export). Run against the real file, the 7.41 importer parsed **0 rows, 64 errors, and garbage metadata** (`destination: 'NAME'`), despite all 10 synthetic unit tests passing — the fixtures had been built from my reading of a PDF, not from a real machine export. Seven distinct defects, each now pinned by a regression test in `TestRealExportShape`/`TestContainerMetadataDates`: **(1)** parser read `wb.active` only, but real exports split the two layouts into **separate sheets** (`Master-BALES` + `Master-CTN GOOD`) under one PARTIAL NO rather than stacking them — an entire class of goods was silently dropped; now every sheet is parsed and rows tagged with `sheet_name`. **(2)** metadata values sit several columns right of their labels (label col 2, value col 5), not adjacent — now scans forward, stopping at the next known label. **(3)** the metadata scan ran into the header row and read its `PORT` column, taking the neighbouring `NAME` header as the destination — now bounded to stop before the header row. **(4)** source misspells `ARRIVEL DATE` — added verbatim (not fuzzy-matched, so only spellings actually seen are accepted). **(5)** a two-row header (`CTNS/PKG` under `QTY`) matched as a fresh header row and **replaced** the real column map with a one-column map, killing `customer_name` and failing every data row — header rows now require ≥3 recognised columns. **(6)** `DLVY  NO.` double-spaced missed the alias table — all alias lookups now whitespace-normalised. **(7)** Excel date cells stringified to `2026-07-04 00:00:00`, matching no format, so **every container date imported as NULL** — native `date`/`datetime` cells are now used directly. Also: `TOTAL:`/`CLOSE CONTAINER ITEM:` footer rows surfaced as bogus "customer name required" errors (now skipped silently — reporting them told staff records had failed when nothing was wrong); compound quantity cells `1PALLET(61items)` and `3bags2ctns1pallet` failed the single-pair regex (now scans every `<count><unit>` run, strips parentheticals, and yields `6 / mixed` for the compound case). **Dedupe bug found only by importing real data:** the 7.41 key `delivery_note_number` alone dropped 4 real bales (2,900 kg) on the *first* import — one delivery note routinely covers several physical bales, each its own row with its own bale number and weight (`HM(UNIQUEWEARS)` has bales 5/4/3 under note `0079283`). Key is now `(delivery_note_number, bale_no)`, matched with `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` so carton/bag rows without a bale number still dedupe. Two columns added to migration 040 (still uncommitted, edited in place + local `ALTER`): `quantity_raw` keeps the verbatim quantity cell so a mixed parcel normalising to `6/mixed` doesn't lose what was written, and `bale_type` keeps `BIG BALE`/`LONG BALE` while `package_unit` collapses to `bale` so "count all bales" stays correct without discarding a distinction the trade prices differently. **Verified live end-to-end against the real file**: 81/81 rows imported, 0 errors, container dates and destination correct, and the stored data matches the source document's **own declared totals** exactly — `SUM(cbm) = 72.55` vs the sheet's `17.65 + 54.9`, and carton/package quantity `= 419` vs its `CTNS/PKGS: 419` — an independent checksum no unit test can fake. Re-import is idempotent (`items_created: 0`, `items_skipped_duplicate: 81`). 533 unit tests pass, lint clean. Docs: `19_excel_import_export.md`, `05a_database_schema_core.md`, `17_testing.md`. **Flagged, not fixed:** the second export (`.xls`) is not an Excel file at all — it is **tab-separated UTF-8 text with an `.xls` extension**, a flat multi-container table (one row per parcel, `Container_No` per row, no header metadata block) with its own quirks (quantity in a `Detail` column, a corrupted `Arrival_date` of `22026-05-31`). `openpyxl` cannot open it, so it needs a separate parser — scoped as its own pass rather than bolted onto the manifest importer. |
| 7.42 ✅ | Manifest import field naming — `supplier_reference` renamed to `original_receipt_number` | User flagged that new field names should align with real shipping/logistics terminology from the source documents, for consistency. Auditing the manifest-import fields (7.41) against the source turned up more than a naming mismatch: the "OG NO." column had been mapped to `supplier_reference`, but the source packing-list document spells this out as "ORIGINAL RECEIPT NUMBER" — a per-parcel receipt reference, not a supplier concept at all. Renamed `packing_list_item.supplier_reference` → `.original_receipt_number` (migration 040 edited in place — it was still uncommitted/local-only, never applied outside this session, so no separate rename migration was needed) across the migration, `import_manifest.py`'s alias table, `service.py`, `repository.py`'s INSERT, and tests. `package_unit`/`delivery_zone` were deliberately kept as-is rather than literally renamed to the source's `bale_type`/`port`: `package_unit` also holds `carton`/`bag` values unrelated to bales (parsed from the other column layout), and `delivery_zone` would collide with `container.destination` if called `port` too — both source terms are themselves overloaded across layouts/granularities. `container.ship_date` → `loading_date` was considered and explicitly declined by the user — that field is pre-existing, unrelated to this feature, and used throughout container creation/tracking/the frontend already. Verified: 527/527 unit tests pass, live re-import against the local stack confirmed the renamed column carries data correctly with no loss from the manual `ALTER TABLE RENAME COLUMN` used to sync the already-migrated local DB. Docs: `19_excel_import_export.md` (alias table + new naming note), `05a_database_schema_core.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.41 ✅ | Manifest import — flexible, auto-creating `POST /v1/manifests/import` for China-side freight-forwarder manifest exports (real PDF: "PARTIAL NO" container manifest, mixed bale/carton column layouts) | New, parallel sibling to the existing exact-header `POST /v1/packing-lists/import` (unchanged, still for staff filling ShipX's own template). Migration 040: `container.departure_date` (distinct from `ship_date`="loading date"); `packing_list_item` gains a bulk-quantity model (`quantity`/`package_unit` instead of one row per physical carton) plus `delivery_note_number` (dedupe key), `bale_no`, `original_receipt_number`, `delivery_zone`, `on_hold`/`hold_reason` — all nullable/defaulted, zero impact on existing rows/importer/tests (517 unit tests pass unchanged before and after). New `services/excel/import_manifest.py`: scans the first ~10 rows for label:value container metadata (PARTIAL NO/LOADING DATE/PORT/ARRIVAL DATE/DEPARTURE DATE) sitting above the tabular data rather than as columns; resolves column headers via an alias table instead of an exact match, and — a real bug caught mid-build — re-checks *every* row against that alias table rather than assuming one static header for the whole sheet, since the real source file switches column layout again partway down (bale-type goods vs. carton/bag goods); parses bulk quantity cells (`"17CTNS"` → `quantity=17, package_unit='carton'`); detects on-hold rows via red font color. `services/excel/service.py::process_manifest_import` auto-creates/matches the container (by `container_number`, fields set **only at creation** — re-import never overwrites hand-edited fields, verified live), customers (by normalized phone, falling back to name), and packing lists (`draft` status); dedupes items by `delivery_note_number` within a packing list so re-uploading the same file is idempotent. Extracted `_parse_positive_decimal`/`_parse_date`/`_compute_cbm` out of `import_packing_list.py` into shared `services/excel/_import_helpers.py`, used by both importers. **Ground-truth gap found and fixed:** `services/excel/repository.py::create_packing_list` (shared by both importers) never supplied `packing_list.reference`, which migration 031 made `NOT NULL` — every real run of the *existing* importer would have 500'd; masked entirely by the unit suite mocking the repo layer. Found live on this task's first end-to-end run; fixed with a new `get_next_pl_reference()` helper (mirrors `services/packing_list/repository.py`), confirmed via a live re-run of the existing importer post-fix. **Verified live end-to-end** against the local stack: hand-built `.xlsx` (openpyxl) transcribing real rows in both layouts under one `PARTIAL NO: STL377` container, imported via the real presigned-url → S3 → `POST /v1/manifests/import` flow; `psql`-confirmed container dates/destination, 4 customers created/matched by phone, packing lists in `draft` with valid `reference`s, items with correct `quantity`/`package_unit`/`delivery_note_number`/`bale_no`/`on_hold`. Hand-edited the container's `destination`/`notes` in the DB, re-uploaded the identical file — hand-edit survived, `items_skipped_duplicate=4`/`items_created=0` in the response, zero new DB rows. Tests: `tests/unit/test_import_manifest.py` (new, 10 cases — both layouts in one combined workbook per the plan, header-metadata scan, quantity/unit parsing, on-hold detection, auto-create-vs-match for container/customer, dedupe). Docs: `19_excel_import_export.md` (new "Manifest Import" section + ground-truth-gaps note), `05a_database_schema_core.md` (migration 040 section + updated DDL), `09_api_manifest.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.40 ✅ | Role-switch selector reverted to the primary role on reload, disagreeing with the still-warehouse page underneath | The active role is UI-only state and is deliberately never persisted — `src/auth/` has a hard CI grep check banning `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` (session-theft risk, `11_security_and_compliance.md`), and it isn't a security boundary worth an exception (authorization is always governed by the full `session.roles` set server-side). So a reload rebuilds `session.role` from the JWT's primary role while the URL — and the rendered page — stays put. Reported live: switch to Warehouse, reload, selector snaps back to "Nigeria Office Staff" while the page is still `/warehouse`. Fixed without any storage mechanism: new `navConfig.ts::roleForPath()` reverse-looks-up which of the user's assigned roles' nav trees owns the current path (prefers the already-active role when it owns the path too, so shared routes like `/billing` don't flip-flop); `StaffLayout.tsx` runs it in a `useEffect` and self-corrects `session.role` whenever it disagrees with the URL — covers reload, direct links, bookmarks, and back/forward with nothing to keep in sync or go stale. Tests: `navConfig.test.ts` (5 new `roleForPath` cases), `StaffLayout.test.tsx` (+2). Docs: `frontend/06_routing_rbac_and_navigation.md`. |
| 7.39 ✅ | Role-switch toggle (7.38) left the page mounted at the old role's route | `StaffLayout.tsx`'s `onRoleChange` handler called only `setActiveRole` — sidebar/breadcrumb correctly updated (they're `session.role`-driven) but the URL never changed, so the page stayed on whatever route the *previous* role was viewing until the user separately clicked a nav link. Reported live: switching Nigeria staff → Warehouse showed a stale/broken view until manually clicking "Warehouse" in the sidebar. Fixed by also calling `navigate(homePathForRole(nextRole))` in the same handler, landing the user on the new role's home page — same pattern already used by login and impersonation-exit. Test: `StaffLayout.test.tsx` (new case, mocks `useNavigate` to assert the call). Docs: `frontend/06_routing_rbac_and_navigation.md`. |
| 7.38 ✅ | Multi-role staff support — a `tenant_user` couldn't hold more than one role (e.g. `nigeria_office_staff` + `warehouse_attendant`); investigating that surfaced a real bug: role changes never actually updated Cognito group membership | Migration 039 adds `tenant_user_role` (join table; `tenant_user.role` kept as primary role, mirrored transactionally). `TenantContext.assert_role(*roles)` keeps its exact call signature — only its internal check changed to a set-intersection against the new `roles: frozenset[UserRole]` field (verified: all 113 existing `assert_role`/`require_roles` call sites unchanged, count and shapes identical before/after). `shared/auth.py::_parse_role` → `_parse_roles` (collects every matching Cognito group, not just the first) + new `_parse_primary_role` (reads `custom:role`, deterministic fallback). **Breaking change (no production tenants yet, no dual-path back-compat):** `POST`/`PATCH /v1/tenant/users` — `role` (singular) → `roles: TenantUserRole[]` + `primary_role?: TenantUserRole`. **Real bug fixed:** `CognitoClient.sync_user_groups()` (new — plus `remove_user_from_group`/`list_groups_for_user`, neither of which existed) actually diffs and reconciles Cognito group membership on every role change; before this, `update_user_attributes(role=...)` only ever touched the `custom:role` *attribute*, never the group, so a demoted/promoted user re-derived their **old** role from their **old**, untouched group after re-login — role changes had zero real effect on permissions. Frontend: `Session.roles: UserRole[]` (new) alongside `role` (now explicitly "active/display role, user-switchable"); `RoleGuard` checks `allow.some(r => session.roles.includes(r))`; new `setActiveRole()` on `SessionContextValue` — a lightweight, client-side-only active-role toggle in `TopBar`'s user menu (no network call, no new token — authorization is always governed by the full `roles` set server-side); `StaffUserList`/`StaffUserDetail` role pickers replaced with a shared `RoleCheckboxList` (checkbox per role + radio for primary). Local dev: `X-Roles` header (`local_server.py`), one multi-role `LocalAuthProvider` dev identity (`nigeria_warehouse`), one seeded multi-role Acme user (`amaka@acmeshipping.com`, `scripts/seed.py` + `bootstrap_local.sh`). **Verified live end-to-end** against the local stack (real Postgres + Moto Cognito): migration backfill produced exactly 28/28 `tenant_user` rows with exactly one `is_primary=TRUE` `tenant_user_role` row each; the partial unique index rejected a second primary; `GET /v1/tenant/users/{id}` returned both roles for the seeded multi-role user; a request gated to a non-active-but-assigned role (`warehouse_attendant`) succeeded while the same request with only the primary role sent 403'd (positive + negative control); `PATCH .../roles` dropping `nigeria_office_staff` actually removed that Cognito group (confirmed via `admin-list-groups-for-user` before/after) and updated `custom:role`, and a subsequent request gated to the dropped role correctly 403'd while the retained role's endpoint still succeeded — the direct regression test for the group-sync bug. Tests: `test_auth.py` (`TestParseRoles`, `TestParsePrimaryRole`, `TestAssertRole` extended), `test_cognito_client.py` (new, moto-backed — add/remove/no-op/cross-tenant-isolation diff cases), `test_tenant_management.py` (`TestCreateUser` extended, `TestUpdateUserRoles` new, `TestSetUserRoles` new) — 517 backend unit tests pass. `RoleGuard.test.tsx`/`StaffUserList.test.tsx` extended, `StaffUserDetail.test.tsx` (new — no prior test file existed), `jwt.test.ts`/`SessionProvider.test.tsx`/`LocalAuthProvider.test.tsx`/`tokenStore.test.ts` extended — 601 frontend tests pass, typecheck and lint clean. Docs: `05a_database_schema_core.md`, `07_api_tenant_management.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `frontend/06_routing_rbac_and_navigation.md`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `LOCAL_TESTING.md`, `17_testing.md`. **Out of scope (flagged, not fixed):** navigation is not merged/unioned across roles (the active-role toggle covers this); the impersonation token-minting flow was not reused/extended for the role toggle; the pre-existing `TenantUserRole`/`UserRole` duplicate-enum tech debt was not unified; `cognito_client.py::enable_user` remains unused/unwired (pre-existing gap, unrelated). |
| 7.37 ✅ | Table sort UI (`sortable: true`) was dead on all 4 tables that declare it; ContainerList Filters panel duplicated the date-range button and had no other real filter | The shared `Table` component's sort chevrons/`onSort` and the backend's `build_paginated_query(sort_field=...)` were both fully real, working code — nothing ever connected them. Wired up all 4: `ContainerList` ('Container'/'ETA'), `BillingList` ('Reference'/'Billed'), `ClaimList` ('Claim ID'/'Filed') via a new shared `frontend/src/hooks/useTableSort.ts` hook + new `sort`/`direction` query params on `GET /v1/containers`\|`/v1/billing`\|`/v1/claims` (parameterized, validated against `ALLOWED_SORT_FIELDS`); `ContainerDetail`'s packing-lists sub-table 'Ref #' is pure client-side (already-loaded array, no backend change). **Ground-truth allowlist fix:** `shared/pagination.py::ALLOWED_SORT_FIELDS` had `arrival_date`/`departure_date`/`total_amount_ngn` — confirmed via grep against every migration that none match any real column on any table — replaced with `estimated_arrival_date`/`invoice_number`/`billed_at`/`reference`, the columns the new sort UI actually needs. **Ground-truth bug found and fixed while wiring the Filters panel's status multi-select:** `GET /v1/containers?status=` only ever accepted one value, but `ContainerList.tsx`'s grouped chips (e.g. "Cleared" = `cleared`+`at_warehouse`+`fully_released`) were already computing a 3-status array and silently truncating it to `[0]` before the request — clicking "Cleared" only ever fetched `status=cleared`, dropping the other two statuses from every result set since this chip has existed. Fixed both ends: `status` now takes a comma-separated list matched via parameterized `status = ANY(%(statuses)s)`. Filters panel rework: removed the duplicate date-range control flagged in 7.36 (now lives only in the standalone calendar button); added a granular 9-status multi-select (independent of the grouped chips — selecting here overrides, never silently ANDs with, the chip selection) and an "Arriving" ETA-range filter (new `eta_after`/`eta_before` params) as the second real filter dimension. **Judgment call:** the task's suggested second-dimension candidate, `origin`, was checked via direct `psql` against the seeded dev tenant (`seed_stress.py`'s 200+ Acme containers) and found to have **zero variance** (all 208 rows are `origin = 'China'` — the stress-seed script never varies it) — `vessel_name`/`shipping_line`/`container_type` were checked too and are likewise NULL/near-constant. `estimated_arrival_date` was the one column with genuine variance (62 distinct dates over ~4 months), so ETA-range replaced origin as the second filter. Verified live against the local stack (`GET /v1/containers`/`/v1/billing`/`/v1/claims` via direct HTTP, real DB, both seed tenants): asc/desc sort confirmed by row order for all 5 sortable fields, multi-status `ANY()` confirmed against real data, `eta_after`/`eta_before` range confirmed, invalid `sort` value correctly 422s (not 500), combined sort+status+search probed together, malformed status list (`sealed,,cleared`) and garbage `direction` values fail safe. Caught a real environment trap along the way: `scripts/local_server.py` (Flask, `debug=False`, no autoreload) had been running since before this change and was silently serving the pre-fix code — sort/status/eta params were accepted but had zero effect until the process was restarted; a reminder that "server was already running" isn't sufficient for live verification of backend code changes. Tests: `TestListContainersRepository`/`TestContainersHandlerQueryParams` (`test_tracking.py`), `TestListBilling`/`TestListBillingAllRepositorySort` (`test_billing.py`), `TestListClaimsSort`/`TestListDamageClaimsRepositorySort` (`test_claims.py`), `ContainerList.test.tsx` (+6), `BillingList.test.tsx`/`ClaimList.test.tsx` (+2 each), `ContainerDetail.test.tsx` (+1). Docs: `10_api_tracking.md`, `11_api_billing.md`, `12_api_claims.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.36 ✅ | China Containers list — search box 500'd server-side, header search was a no-op app-wide, calendar/Filters buttons were decorative | Four bugs found while root-causing a user report that "filters don't work" on `ContainerList`. **(1)** `services/tracking/repository.py::list_containers`'s `q` free-text filter was folded into the blanket `f"c.{cond}"`-prefixed `conditions` list, producing invalid SQL (`c.(c.container_number ILIKE ...)`) — every keystroke in the table search box actually 500'd server-side (confirmed via direct `psql`), not just "needed debouncing" as initially assumed. Fixed by appending it as a separate `q_clause` (same pattern as `packing_list/repository.py::list_packing_lists`). **(2)** Table search had no debounce — added 300ms debounce (`AdminCustomerList`'s existing pattern). **(3)** `TopBar`'s header search box (`onSearch` prop) was declared but never wired by any caller anywhere in the app — extended `TopBarContext`/`useTopBarSlot` with an `onSearch` slot (same shape as `actions`), threaded through `StaffLayout` → `TopBar`; `ContainerList` now passes its own `setSearch` so the header box and the table's own search box share one state (not two independent search implementations). **(4)** "Last 30 days" and "Filters" buttons had no `onClick` at all. Added `created_after`/`created_before` query params to `GET /v1/containers` (parameterized SQL, same naming convention as packing-list's existing `created_after`/`created_before`), backed by a new reusable `DateRangeFilter` design component (7/30/90-day presets + All time, defaults to All time — never silently scopes the list, since this dev tenant alone has 200+ containers via `seed_stress.py`); "Filters" opens a panel with the same date-created control plus "Clear all filters". Also fixed the "Cleared (30d)" stat card, which previously counted all-time cleared containers under a "(30d)" label — now scoped to `created_at` within 30 days, matching its label. Verified live via Playwright against the local stack (200+-container seed tenant): search, header-search sync, date-range dropdown, and Filters panel all screenshot-confirmed working, zero 4xx/5xx from `GET /v1/containers` across all interactions. Tests: `TestListContainersRepository` (3 cases, `tests/unit/test_tracking.py`) + 4 new cases in `ContainerList.test.tsx`. `BillingList.tsx`/`ClaimList.tsx`/etc. have the identical dead calendar/filter button pattern — noted, not fixed (out of scope for this pass). Docs: `10_api_tracking.md`, `ShipX_API.postman_collection.json`, `frontend/07_domain_modules.md`, `17_testing.md`. |
| 7.35 ✅ | Volume-scale dev seed data + `billing.status` truncation bug | Added `scripts/seed_stress.py` (`make seed-stress`) — ~200 containers / ~50 customers across the full container lifecycle and every billing status, added to the Acme dev tenant so the container/customer/billing list UIs can be exercised under realistic volume (the curated `seed.py` dataset only has 2 containers). Running it surfaced a real ground-truth bug: `billing.status` has been `VARCHAR(20)` since migration 002, but migration 006 added `'released_with_outstanding'` (26 chars) to the CHECK constraint without ever widening the column — so `mark_billing_released_with_outstanding()`, the actual "release container with outstanding balance" code path, has always failed with `StringDataRightTruncation`. Fixed via migration 038 (`VARCHAR(30)`). Verified live: all 6 billing statuses now insert and list correctly via `GET /v1/billing`. Details in `13_api_payment_release.md` and `05b_database_schema_extended.md`. |
| 7.34 ✅ | Warehouse "Confirm pickup" always 500'd | `get_release_note_by_id()` selected `b.storage_fees_ngn` directly from `billing` — that column has never existed (storage fees are aggregated from `storage_fee_log`). Every call raised `UndefinedColumn`, so no warehouse attendant could ever confirm a pickup. Fixed to aggregate the same way `excel/repository.py::get_billing_for_export` does. Verified live: GET and PATCH .../release both succeed against the real DB. Invisible to the existing unit suite (mocks the DB layer entirely) — flagged as a testing-infrastructure gap in `13_api_payment_release.md`. |
| 7.33 ✅ | Multi-tenant Paystack — each tenant connects their own payment gateway | Previously a single platform-wide `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` (Terraform var, Lambda env) served every tenant — a real fund-commingling risk. Now: encrypted per-tenant key in `tenant_config.paystack_config` (KMS+Fernet, stronger than `webhook_config`'s plaintext precedent — justified by blast radius), new `PUT/DELETE /v1/tenant/config/paystack` + `POST .../test` (live API check), new `/admin/config/payments` page. No platform-wide fallback — an unconfigured tenant gets a clear error, bank transfer stays available. Webhook signature verification reordered: tenant is resolved from the billing reference *before* HMAC verification (previously verified-then-resolved, impossible once there's no single shared secret) — this is a *stricter* invariant, not weaker, since a signature only ever validates against the exact tenant its reference claims. Verified live end-to-end: two tenants, two different keys, a forged cross-tenant webhook correctly rejected, the legitimate one correctly accepted. Removed `paystack_secret_key` from Terraform entirely. Tests: `test_payment_webhook.py`, `test_payment_release.py::TestInitializeOnlinePayment`, `test_tenant_management.py::TestPaystackConfig`, `PaymentGatewayConfig.test.tsx` (7 cases). |
| 7.32 ✅ | apiDownloadFile treated JSON metadata as the file — every export button in the app was broken | Every export/pdf Lambda route responds with a JSON envelope wrapping a presigned `download_url`, never raw file bytes — `apiDownloadFile` called `response.blob()` directly on that JSON and opened/downloaded *it*, so packing-list/manifest/release-note/billing exports all silently produced a text file of metadata instead of the real document. Fixed to parse the envelope and follow `download_url`. Discovered via the customer "Download invoice" button showing raw JSON. Tests: `client.test.ts` (3 cases). |
| 7.31 ✅ | `GET /v1/billing` had zero customer-role scoping — cross-customer financial data leak | Any customer hitting `GET /v1/billing` (allowed by `_BILLING_READ_ROLES`) with no filters received every billing record in the tenant — other customers' amounts, payment status, names. `list_billing()` now force-scopes `customer_id` to the caller's own record when `ctx.role == CUSTOMER`, silently discarding any `customer_id` they attempt to pass. Found while debugging the "View invoice" flow. Tests: `TestListBilling` (5 cases) in `tests/unit/test_billing.py`. |
| 7.30 ✅ | Customer invoice download — never wired up | `GET /v1/billing/{id}/export` was staff-only and the customer billing page had no download button at all — the post-payment "View invoice" flow led nowhere. Added a customer-scoped ownership check (`export_billing()` 404s on any customer/billing mismatch, mirroring `export_packing_list_pdf`), widened the single-record export route to allow `customer` (own only — the container-level export stays staff-only), and added a "Download invoice" button to `MyBillingDetail.tsx`. Verified live: real PDF downloaded via presigned S3 URL; a second customer in the same tenant correctly gets 404, not the invoice. Tests: `TestExportBilling` (5 cases) in `tests/unit/test_excel.py`. |
| 7.29 ✅ | Auto-billing on container clear — never actually fired | `billing/handler.py`'s EventBridge `PaymentDue` branch had no rule/target wiring it to invoke the billing Lambda (`eventbridge/main.tf` only fanned it to SMS/WhatsApp queues) — a container could reach `cleared`/`at_warehouse` with zero billing rows, in prod as well as local dev. Fixed with a direct synchronous call from `tracking/service.py` to `billing.service.handle_payment_due()` on the `cleared` transition. Verified live: fresh container walked through the real API state machine → billing appeared instantly, visible to the customer via their own billing endpoint. Tests in `tests/unit/test_tracking.py`. |

**Session persistence — reload-forces-relogin bug closed (2026-07-11):**
- New backend service `services/auth/` (`handler.py`, `cognito_refresh.py`, `models.py`) — `POST /v1/auth/session|refresh|logout`, an httpOnly-cookie refresh-token flow so a page reload silently restores a session instead of bouncing to `/login`. IAM-scoped to only `cognito-idp:InitiateAuth`/`RevokeToken` (non-admin, narrower than `tenant_management`'s Cognito policy); non-VPC (no DB access needed).
- `infrastructure/aws/modules/lambda/` — new `non_vpc_api_configs` Lambda category, `cognito_client_id` variable threaded through; `infrastructure/aws/modules/api-gateway/` — 3 new public routes, `allow_credentials = true` + a Terraform validation block rejecting a wildcard `cors_allow_origins` (required for the cookie to work cross-subdomain, and browsers reject wildcard-origin + credentials outright).
- Frontend: `SessionProvider` now attempts a silent restore on mount (`status: 'loading'` until it settles); `RoleGuard`/`RootRedirect` wait it out instead of redirecting. `tokenStore.ts`'s `ActiveAuth` type no longer has a `refreshToken` field at all — the refresh token never lives in JS memory past the single handoff call at login, a stronger guarantee than "always pass null."
- **Ground-truth gap found and fixed along the way:** `POST /v1/register` (self-registration) was implemented in `tenant_management/handler.py` and called by `RegisterScreen.tsx`, but was missing from both the Terraform API Gateway route table and `scripts/local_server.py`'s routes — it 404'd in production and local dev alike. Added to both.
- **Ground-truth gap found, not fixed (flagged, out of scope for this pass):** `frontend-ci.yml`'s `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` grep guard is a bare string match with no exclusion for comments/test files — it already trips on explanatory comments and on `storage.test.ts`'s own assertion that localStorage is never called, meaning it would fail CI on `main` independent of this work. See [11_security_and_compliance.md](frontend/11_security_and_compliance.md).

## How to Use These Task Files

1. **Read `00_CROSS_CUTTING_STANDARDS.md` first** — it contains mandatory conventions for ALL tasks
2. **Read the task file** — each contains all context needed (architecture excerpt, DDL, API contracts, business rules)
3. **Check dependencies** — ensure prerequisite tasks are completed
4. **Build the deliverables** — each file specifies exact files to create with paths
5. **Reference constants** — shared types, error codes, and patterns are in tasks 02, 03, and 06
6. **Follow conventions** — all services follow the same handler → service → repository pattern
7. **Validate** — each task has specific acceptance criteria; check all boxes before moving on

---

## Key Architecture Decisions (Context for All Tasks)

- **Multi-tenancy:** Shared database with `tenant_id` row-level isolation on every table
- **Auth:** Single Cognito User Pool; `custom:tenant_id` in JWT; Platform Admin has `tenant_id = PLATFORM`
- **4-Layer Isolation:** JWT claims → Application layer → PostgreSQL RLS → S3 prefix
- **Async:** EventBridge for domain events → SQS queues → Lambda consumers (notifications)
- **API Style:** REST, JSON, RFC 7807 errors, cursor-based pagination, `Idempotency-Key` for POST
- **IaC:** Terraform with modules per AWS service, S3 + DynamoDB remote state locking
- **Observability:** Structured JSON logging, X-Ray tracing, CloudWatch Embedded Metrics
- **Compute:** ARM64/Graviton2 Lambda for 20% cost reduction
- **Region:** eu-west-1 (Ireland), DR backup to eu-central-1 (Frankfurt)
